# Chess World — implementation ledger

Every one of the 500 sections of `docs/chess-world-spec.md`, read in full, against the code in this tree.

## How this one differs from the one before it

The previous ledger was compiled **without the specification text**, which was not in the repository at the time; it
worked from the eighty-five section numbers the code happens to cite and recorded 281 sections as "not implemented"
when what it meant was "nothing here tells me either way". The specification is now at
`docs/chess-world-spec.md` (500 sections, 49 parts, none missing), so every row below was judged against what its
section actually asks for. That is why the numbers moved so much: **281 unknowns became 38 genuinely unstarted
sections and 310 partial ones.** Almost nothing was wrong; almost everything was unmeasured.

Compiled by twenty readers over twenty-five sections each, every one reading the section text before looking at the
code. The standard for `done` was strict and is worth restating, because it is what the count means:

| Mark | What it required |
|---|---|
| **done** | The behaviour is in the code **and** a test in this tree exercises it **and** that test was run and passed. The evidence names both. |
| **partial** | Some of what the section asks for is there. The gap column says which part is not. Code with no test is partial, never done. |
| **not started** | Nothing in the tree answers it. |
| **not applicable** | Only with a reason, and only twice. |

**On screen** is a separate question from status: whether somebody running Orbis today can reach the thing. A
finished, tested module nothing mounts is `done` / `no`.

## The count

|---|---|
| done | 150 |
| partial | 310 |
| not started | 38 |
| not applicable | 2 |
| **Total** | **500** |

Reachable on screen today: **310**. Built and tested but still unreachable: **11**.

## Every section

### Product Foundation — 001 to 010

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 001 | Product identity and purpose | One chess environment serving beginners through tournament players, whose major modules share the same game data, settings, storage and engine services, and whose capabilities join up — play the engine, save it, analyse it, find a recurring weakness, open the lesson or puzzle set for it. | partial | yes | src/renderer/src/components/chess/ChessView.tsx (fifteen areas over one shell); src/shared/chess/model.ts (one StoredGame for engine games, imports, broadcasts and set-up positions); src/shared/chess/rules.ts is the only chess.js importer; src/main/chess/store.ts + src/main/chess/data.ts are the only stores. The shared-data claim is exercised by rules.test.ts (15 pass), analysis.test.ts (36 pass), library.test.ts (40 pass), data.test.ts (25 pass). The live half of the journey is closed: LiveChess.tsx:364 calls chessLiveSave then onStudy, and liveView.test.ts (55 pass) covers walking back without losing the live tip. | The learning half of the journey is not wired. src/renderer/src/components/chess/coach/CoachPanel.tsx:204-212 prints on screen that "Chess World does not record what the coach reads yet, so every figure here stays empty however much you have played", and ChessView passes it only `played={games}` and no `evidence`. So a recurring weakness is never identified, and nothing leads from one to a lesson or a puzzle set. No test exercises any journey across two modules. |
| 002 | Preserve the existing ORBIS application | Add Chess World to the existing application rather than rebuilding it: no second shell, no replaced router, no changed shortcuts, and any shared-code change kept minimal, justified and regression-tested. | done | — | One lazy mount in src/renderer/src/App.tsx:36,1110; one capability row in src/shared/commandCenter.ts:292; one icon in src/renderer/src/lib/features.ts:23. Chess World renders inside the shared Surface (src/renderer/src/ui/Surface.tsx), not its own shell. The only shared-code change is additive — the `what` option and flushSync in src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts — and it is regression-covered by jsonFile.test.ts (13 pass). Guards that all pass: appFeature.test.ts (6 pass, "the window keeps no second list of its own screens"); cssCollisions.test.ts (2 pass, no chess stylesheet claims a class the global sheet claims); screenWidth.test.ts (3 pass, no chess stylesheet adds a window media query); settings.test.ts (58 pass, "no key is claimed twice in the same place" and "the app chords in this map are really in the files it says they are", read out of Board.tsx and ipc.ts as text). | — |
| 003 | Inspect the actual repository | Work from what the repository really is — its framework, process boundaries, storage, profiles, notifications, AI integration and test infrastructure — reuse what exists, write the inspection down, and never treat a nonexistent API or component as already available. | partial | — | docs/chess-world.md (376 lines) records the framework, the process split, the channel tables, the licences and a method for redoing the census. Reuse is real: JsonFile/Collection/personalFile from src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts, DEFAULT_PROFILE_ID, profilesStore.onChange, quitFlush, the commandCenter registry, shared/portable/registry.ts, and src/renderer/src/ui in 38 of the 57 chess React files. | The written inspection has rotted and nothing keeps it true. docs/chess-world.md still says ChessView mounts none of the panels (it mounts fifteen), that nothing but games is stored (five more per-profile files landed today in src/main/chess/data.ts), that endgamesData.ts has no test (endgamesData.test.ts passes 7), and that trainingView.test.ts is failing (it passes). ChessView.tsx:18 also over-claims that SESSION_RIGHTS is "read by the area itself" — exactly one area reads it. |
| 004 | Maintain an implementation ledger | Keep a checklist of every specification requirement with its state — unimplemented, in progress, implemented, tested, blocked or deferred — naming the files and the tests for the implemented ones, and keeping interface completion separate from functional completion. | partial | — | docs/chess-world-ledger.md (290 lines) has the five marks, a separate "On screen" column that is exactly the interface-versus-functional split the section asks for, and an Evidence column naming files and tests. | It was compiled without the specification text and says so in its own preamble, so it does not trace the sections' wording — it works from the eighty-five section numbers the code happens to cite and collapses the rest into ranges. Several entries are already false (it records ChessView as mounting nothing, three trainingView cases as failing, and nothing but games as stored). docs/chess-world-spec.md now exists in the tree and the ledger has not been redone against it. Nothing tests or regenerates it, unlike docs/ipc-mapping.md which has ipcMapping.test.ts (6 pass). |
| 005 | Define the initial release | Ship this set and no multiplayer: local Stockfish play, the rules, the AI Arena, the Academy, puzzles, opening exploration, game analysis, variation exploration, endgame training, local game storage, progress tracking, and live-game viewing through accessible providers. | partial | yes | Every named capability is mounted from ChessView.tsx and has a passing test: play/Arena (arena.test.ts 41 pass, engine.test.ts 39 pass), rules (rules.test.ts 15 pass), Academy (curriculum.test.ts 8 pass, academyPath.test.ts 82 pass), puzzles (puzzles.test.ts 38 pass), openings (OpeningLab + openings.test.ts), analysis and variations (analysis.test.ts 36 pass), endgames (endgames.test.ts 29 pass, endgamesData.test.ts 7 pass), storage (data.test.ts 25 pass), live (live.test.ts 41, lichess.test.ts 47, manager.test.ts 29, liveView.test.ts 55 — all pass). Grep for matchmaking, multiplayer, lobby and seek across src/shared/chess, src/main/chess and the chess components returns nothing. | Two of the twelve named items are present as a screen and not as a feature. Game analysis and variation exploration cannot be kept: StoredGame has no field for a tree, an analysis session is not one of the five files src/main/chess/data.ts holds, and src/shared/chess/pgn.ts:127 deliberately drops bracketed variations — so lines, notes and evaluations exist only while AnalysisStudio is mounted. Progress tracking is mounted but empty, because the Coach is passed no evidence (see Section 001). |
| 006 | Prohibit fake functionality | Everything in production runs on real data — engine numbers from a real search, puzzles with validated solutions, history from saved games, live displays from genuine provider data — and the interface must tell live, cached, historical, demonstration and unavailable apart rather than keep showing old moves as current. | done | yes | No engine means no opponent: engine.test.ts (39 pass) covers "with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly" and "ready" only after a real uciok/readyok handshake. Freshness is on screen — freshnessBadge from src/shared/chess/liveView.ts is drawn in live/LiveViewer.tsx:155 and live/LiveRoundGames.tsx:45, with the provider's own publication delay fed in; liveView.test.ts (55 pass) and live.test.ts (41 pass) cover a re-sent snapshot not being progress, a clock saying whether it was received or worked out here, and an embargoed broadcast never called live. lichess.test.ts (47 pass) covers a 200 whose body is an error object being a refusal, and a schema change reported as a schema change rather than falling back to scraping. src/shared/chess/reference.test.ts (18 pass) refuses to report a dataset as present from a setting alone. Puzzle themes carry evidence and puzzleGen's five checks are recomputed (puzzles.test.ts 38 pass). | — |
| 007 | Adopt local-first privacy | Personal chess data stays on the device by default, reading a public tournament feed must not upload the private archive, no cloud sync or remote personal-data database, and the core must work with no account. | partial | — | Six per-profile JSON files under userData and nothing else (src/main/chess/store.ts, src/main/chess/data.ts:CHESS_DATA_FILES). No account anywhere. The only outbound traffic in the whole chess layer is the Lichess broadcast endpoints and the Stockfish release download; src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:576-580 sends only a User-Agent and an optional user-supplied token, as headers, and lichess.test.ts (47 pass) includes "a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address". | The central claim is untested. Nothing asserts that the chess layer reaches no host other than the two documented ones, and no test shows that a live refresh or a broadcast follow sends none of the person's stored games — the property holds by reading the code, and nothing keeps it true. |
| 008 | Respect ORBIS profile isolation | Use the existing profile architecture so each profile has its own game history, saved positions, annotations, learning progress, engine preferences and repertoires, switching profiles never reveals another's data, and no parallel profile mechanism is invented. | partial | yes | ChessStore and ChessDataStore both build every path from personalFile(userData, profileId, …) and both sit in the personalStores list in src/main/index.ts:4357, so profilesStore.onChange switches all six files at once; all six are the `chess` category in src/shared/portable/registry.ts:265-270, so they travel with a profile export. data.test.ts (25 pass) proves it: "shows the other profile its own record, and never the first one's", "lands a save queued before a profile switch in the profile that made it", "does not carry what could not be read in one profile over to the next". | Three of the four window-side stores now use it: settings/chessSettingsStore.tsx, puzzles/store.tsx and academy/storage.tsx read and write chess:settings, chess:training and chess:academy, re-read on window.api.onProfileChanged, and bring their old window-storage copy across once through keeping.ts firstRead — recorded in that same storage so a second profile cannot help itself to the first person's record (keeping.test.ts, 24 pass, "does not hand the same copy to a second profile"). What is left: coach/CoachPanel.tsx:66-67 still keeps the study plan and a gamification switch in window.localStorage, namespaced by a storageKey prop, and nothing calls chess:progress or chess:openings. The settings keep one copy in window storage as a first-frame mirror, which is per computer by design — mirror.ts refuses to write it from the untouched defaults so it cannot destroy the copy it is about to carry across. Annotations and repertoires are still not persisted from any screen. And src/main/chess/store.ts has no test beside it, so the games file's own isolation is unproven. |
| 009 | Implement in dependency order | Build in a sequence that avoids rework — repository integration, rules, board, storage, engine, game lifecycle, analysis, training, openings and endgames, then live data — with advanced features reusing the established core instead of growing their own incompatible chess state, each phase usable and testable before anything is built on it. | partial | — | The layering really is a single stack: src/shared/chess/rules.ts:1 is the only file in the repository that imports chess.js, ten-plus modules import it, model.ts sits on rules.ts and everything else on those. The engine converts its own principal variation through rules.ts rather than trusting it (engine.test.ts, "a move the engine offers that cannot be played is refused, and said so", 39 pass). src/shared/chess/spaced.ts defers to the existing shared/compute/learning.ts scheduler rather than writing a second one. Every layer has its own test that needs neither Electron nor a network — thirteen chess test files run and pass individually in this audit. | Nothing guards the boundary. No test asserts that rules.ts stays the only chess.js importer, or that a new module cannot grow its own opinion about legality — the single-authority property is true today and would break silently. The build-order claim itself cannot be checked from the tree. |
| 010 | Define quality standards | Set measurable quality targets and measure the real things — engine startup time, board responsiveness, cancellation latency, live-feed update delay, persistence reliability, resource usage — under documented conditions, with repeatable tests, documented limitations, and no invented benchmarks. | partial | — | Repeatable tests exist and are large. One real measurement is in the tree: library.test.ts (40 pass) builds 5,000 games and prints timed cold and warm text search, a multi-filter narrowing, and a sort for each of six keys. engine.ts sizes threads and memory from availableParallelism and totalmem, held by engine.test.ts's "threads and memory are sized for a laptop, and never take every core". The provider's own publication delay is read and shown rather than claimed away (lichess.test.ts, "the recorded round is read, with its games, its clocks in seconds and its undocumented delay"). docs/chess-world.md has a Known limitations list and states plainly that no test has ever run the real Stockfish binary. | There is no target anywhere — not a number, not a budget, not a documented test condition. Engine startup time, cancellation latency, live-feed update delay, persistence reliability and resource usage are all unmeasured: engine.test.ts drives a scripted stand-in process, so no startup or search cost in this tree is a real figure. The documented limitations are stale (see Section 003). |

### Architecture And Core Services — 011 to 020

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 011 | Establish module boundaries | Keep the interface separate from rules, engine management, sessions, persistence, live providers, analysis, lessons, puzzles, openings and progress — a board must not spawn engine processes or make provider requests itself — without inventing layers, so changing a provider does not mean rewriting chess components. | done | — | src/main/chess/ipc.ts is the only chess file that imports Electron, and portability.test.ts (4 pass) enforces exactly that in both directions — "the parts of Orbis meant to survive the move do not touch Electron" and "everything that does touch Electron is a file we have decided will be rewritten", with main/chess/ipc.ts the single declared chess entry at portability.test.ts:207. The provider sits behind LiveProviderPort with an injected fetchFn and manager.test.ts (29 pass) swaps it for a stand-in; the engine sits behind the EngineService interface (ipc.ts:21-32) and engine.test.ts (39 pass) swaps the process. Board.tsx draws the position it is given and asks for moves, and calls neither the engine nor a provider. | — |
| 012 | Register Chess World through ORBIS | Hook into the existing navigation and module registration rather than creating a second shell or replacing the router, initialise only what the first view needs, and never let a missing asset, an absent network or an uninstalled engine stop the rest of ORBIS from starting. | done | yes | One capability row (src/shared/commandCenter.ts:292, target kind `app`, app `chess`), one icon mapping (src/renderer/src/lib/features.ts:23), one lazy mount (src/renderer/src/App.tsx:36,1110), rendered inside the shared Surface. appFeature.test.ts (6 pass) holds the registry and the window to each other in both directions — "nothing claims a screen the window does not have", "no screen the window has is invisible to the registry", "the window keeps no second list of its own screens" — and commandCenter.test.ts passes 36. Each of the fifteen areas is its own lazy() chunk; ChessEngine's constructor only records a path and spawns nothing; the live manager fetches nothing until follow(). An absent engine is honest rather than fatal: engine.test.ts, "with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly". | — |
| 013 | Manage shared services | Provide reusable, testable services for rules, engine management, game sessions, storage, analysis, live data and progress, with stated ownership and lifecycle, preventing redundant Stockfish processes or duplicate subscriptions, and letting test implementations replace external providers and engine processes without touching production logic. | partial | — | One of each, all owned by src/main/index.ts:4325-4357 and reachable only through registerChessIpc's engine()/live()/store/data: ChessEngine, LiveChessManager, ChessStore, ChessDataStore. The engine queues rather than duplicating — engine.test.ts (39 pass): "one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once", "two requests cannot share an id", "an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search". Listing is not following and an old subscription cannot speak — manager.test.ts (29 pass); lichess.test.ts (47 pass): "the same question asked twice at once costs one request", "requests go one at a time". Doubles are first-class: the EngineService and LiveService interfaces plus an injected fetchFn. | Game sessions is the one service on the section's own list that does not exist — there is no session service at all (see Section 019), so every session is React state inside the screen drawing it. And no service is disposed on a profile switch: followProfile in src/main/index.ts:4351-4356 calls useProfile on the stores and never tells the engine or the live manager. |
| 014 | Define strongly typed domain models | Type the domain — games, moves, positions, engine searches, variations, puzzles, lessons, opening lines, live events, analysis reports — represent missing information explicitly rather than inventing a clock or a rating, and validate external data before it enters the model. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/model.ts (StoredGame, GameMeta, GameMove, GameSource, VariationNode, VariationTree; every unknown field optional and left out rather than filled), evaluation.ts (Score is centipawns, a mate, or nothing — a mate is never flattened into a number), uci.ts, puzzles.ts, lessons.ts, openings.ts, live.ts, analysis.ts. Validation at every edge: normalizeGame in store.ts:32-84 replays stored notation through buildMoves instead of believing it; asMoves in ipc.ts:62-76; src/main/chess/shapes.ts reads every stored record field by field. Tests that pass: lichess.test.ts 47 ("the provider clock is centiseconds, and an implausible one is refused rather than shown", "a game with no link in it is reported and left out, never guessed at"), engine.test.ts 39 ("a mate score is a mate, and it is turned round for the side to move exactly once", "a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score"), data.test.ts 25, liveView.test.ts 55, analysis.test.ts 36. | model.ts, the file that defines the central type, has no test beside it. buildMoves, fingerprintOf, fenAt, movePairs, addToTree, pathTo and removeBranch are only exercised incidentally through other modules' tests, and treeProblems — the model's own soundness check for loops, orphans and illegal attachments — is called from exactly one place (analysis.ts:588) and has no direct test. Engine searches and analysis reports are typed but the search type crossing the window boundary is declared as `unknown` in src/shared/types.ts. |
| 015 | Standardise error handling | Route illegal moves, invalid FEN, malformed PGN, engine crashes, storage failures, disconnections, rate limits, incomplete records and unsupported formats through controlled paths with useful messages that leak no filesystem internals or tokens, recover from transient faults, preserve the person's work, and never silently replace corrupted data with an empty game. | partial | yes | Each failure class has a real path and a passing test. Illegal move: applyMove returns null and the board does nothing (rules.test.ts 15 pass). Invalid FEN: checkFen names the wrong part and ipc.ts:258 refuses before the engine is touched (variants.test.ts 44 pass, "a board that cannot be read is refused rather than half read"). Malformed PGN: readPgn keeps reading faults and playing faults apart and ImportSummary reports every game with the move that stopped it. Engine crash: "an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again" (engine.test.ts 39 pass). Storage: nothing is written over a file that could not be read and KeptAnswer.refused says the save did not happen (data.test.ts 25 pass, jsonFile.test.ts 13 pass). Disconnection and 429: bounded jittered retry then give up, and the documented minute honoured (lichess.test.ts 47 pass). | There is no one error path — the same "Error invoking remote method" message strip is written out in twelve separate chess screens with no shared helper, so each screen is its own standard. The games store is the one store with no test of its own, so "never silently replace corrupted data with an empty game" is proved for the five new files and unproved for the person's games. And damageNote in src/main/storedFile.ts:106 puts the moved-aside file's full path into the sentence shown to the person. |
| 016 | Keep expensive operations off the UI thread | Engine searches, bulk PGN imports, large archive queries and multi-position analysis must not freeze the interface; use the mechanism ORBIS already has, show progress, allow cancellation, limit concurrency, and deliver UI updates safely. | partial | yes | The engine is a child process owned by the main process, and every search is bounded — a request arriving with no limit is given movetime 2000 and all limits are clamped (ipc.ts:259-270); engine.test.ts (39 pass) covers "a search with no limit at all is refused rather than left to run for ever", "a search the engine never answers times out, and the engine is restarted", "a cancelled search fails as cancelled", "an AbortSignal cancels a running search, and removes a waiting one", and the one-at-a-time queue with interactive ahead of batch. PGN import runs in main (store.importPgn). The whole-game sweep in analysis/AnalysisStudio.tsx:186-270 runs one search at a time with SweepState {running, done, total, note} drawn and an onCancel wired at line 375. ArenaGame stops its own search by id on unmount (arena/ArenaGame.tsx:438-444). | The library's own work is all on the renderer's main thread — filterGames, sortGames, pageFrom, facetsOf and duplicateGroups run over the whole games list, which store.ts caps at 20,000, with no worker; the measured numbers in library.test.ts are for 5,000. And nothing tests the progress or the cancellation, because no test in the tree touches any chess React file. |
| 017 | Handle application lifecycle correctly | Release engine processes, subscriptions, file handles and background workers when their owners close; profile switching must stop or safely transfer active tasks; persist finished work before shutdown; and if the application exits mid-analysis, keep the last valid saved game and mark the unfinished analysis incomplete rather than final. | partial | — | src/main/index.ts:5653-5664 gives quitFlush four chess entries — the games flushed, the settings/puzzles/progress flushed, the engine shut down, and any followed broadcast let go. JsonFile.flush reads the path into a local before writing, so a save queued before a switch lands in the profile that made it. data.test.ts (25 pass): "writes a save queued microseconds earlier", "still refuses to write over a file it could not read, even on the way out". engine.test.ts (39 pass): "restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit". useEngineAnalysis removes both IPC listeners on unmount, and the analysis report leaves holes where nothing was searched rather than filling them. | Profile switching stops nothing. followProfile (src/main/index.ts:4351-4356) calls useProfile on the stores and never tells ChessEngine or the live manager, so a running search and a followed broadcast both carry across a profile change. And closing the Analysis Studio does not stop its search: the only unmount cleanup there is `sweeping.current = false` (AnalysisStudio.tsx:82), and useEngineAnalysis's cleanup removes listeners without calling chessStopAnalysis — where ArenaGame's equivalent cleanup does stop its search by id. |
| 018 | Integrate notifications conservatively | Reuse ORBIS's own notification system where it exists, for completed requested analyses, engine installation failures, selected-game completion and optional study reminders; never notify per move; let people disable optional reminders and set their frequency; and change nothing about unrelated ORBIS notifications. | partial | yes | Chess World uses the window's in-screen notice strip and nothing else — onNotify(text, kind) threaded from App.tsx with NoticeKind from src/renderer/src/lib/notices.ts. It is genuinely conservative: a failed save is said once and never per move (arena/ArenaGame.tsx:266-270, "A message for every move of a game that cannot be saved is worse than useless"), live games never notify per move, and an engine install failure is reported because ArenaGame.tsx:212-227 polls the engine status every two seconds while installing and says so. | ORBIS's own notification system (src/shared/notifications.ts, src/main/notifications/) has no chess source at all, so nothing chess reaches the one place that interrupts you, nothing is configurable, and nothing survives leaving the screen. chess:engine-failed is sent by src/main/chess/ipc.ts:252 and has no listener in src/preload/index.ts, so if the person leaves the Play area while the engine is downloading, the failure is silent — the two-second poll is what covers for it, and it unmounts with the screen. There are no study reminders and no frequency control. |
| 019 | Create a unified game-session manager | A service that manages active sessions independently of the screen displaying them, tracking id, initial and current position, move history, players, time control, status and source, supporting AI games, imports, analysis, puzzles and broadcasts as distinct types — a broadcast must not take your moves — with sessions reopenable and navigable without losing state. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/model.ts:87-96 defines SessionKind and SESSION_RIGHTS, the per-kind table of what may be done (userMoves, engineMoves, resign, variations). Four good per-type session models exist: ArenaState (arena.ts:379, arena.test.ts 41 pass), AnalysisSession (analysis.ts:84, analysis.test.ts 36 pass), MergedGame (liveView.ts:972, liveView.test.ts 55 pass), and the exercise/attempt training layer. A watched game cannot be played in practice: live/LiveViewer.tsx:175 passes interactive={false}. | There is no service. Every session is React state inside the screen that draws it, so changing area unmounts it — ArenaGame starts from useState<ArenaState \| null>(null) with no restore, so an unfinished engine game comes back as a fresh setup screen, and a study's lines, notes and evaluations are simply gone. Only the game record survives, because ArenaGame writes the moves to the store as it plays. And the rights table is read in exactly one place: AnalysisStudio.tsx:89 reads SESSION_RIGHTS.analysis; LiveViewer only mentions it in a comment and enforces read-only itself; the other thirteen areas never consult it. No test exercises the table. |
| 020 | Establish versioned local persistence | Use ORBIS's storage with versioned schemas for games, annotations, progress, preferences and saved analysis, provide safe migrations tested against representative older data, handle missing fields, invalid records, interrupted writes and duplicate imports, and store timestamps consistently with source-provided ones kept apart from local receipt times. | partial | yes | Six per-profile files on the existing JsonFile/Collection layer: atomic write-and-rename, a debounce, and four distinct cases on opening so an unreadable file is never written over (src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts, jsonFile.test.ts 13 pass). Versioning is stamped into the records — PUZZLE_STORE_VERSION (puzzles.ts:713) and PROGRESS_VERSION (academyPath.ts:57) — and a file claiming a higher number is moved aside rather than read down, which data.test.ts (25 pass) covers as "moves aside a collection written by a newer Orbis instead of dropping what it does not recognise", alongside "a field this version has never heard of survives a trip out and back", "one bad value costs the value and nothing else", and "writes nothing at all over a file it could not read, and the save says so". Duplicate imports: fingerprintOf plus a skip/keep-both policy with every game reported (store.ts:146-190). Timestamps are kept apart by type: importedAt and retrievedAt on GameSource are local receipt, meta.date is as the source wrote it and may be partial. | chess-games.json carries no version stamp at all, and no version or migrate option is passed for any of the six files — the versioning that exists is forward-refusal, not migration, so there is no migration path and nothing is tested against representative older data. Annotations and saved analysis are not persisted anywhere. And the window writes none of the five new files, so the records a person actually makes still sit in unversioned localStorage JSON (see Section 008). |

The readability evidence is real and passing — settings.test.ts (58 pass): "every theme has a visible difference between its two squares", "every piece can be seen on both of its squares, by its colour or by its outline", "the check square is painted above the square colour, not under it", "the square names can be read on the square they sit on", "the colours this panel introduces can be read on its own background", "every class this panel defines starts with its own prefix", "every board attribute the component sets is one the stylesheet acts on". cssCollisions.test.ts (2 pass) proves no chess sheet steals a global class. | The guards cover the board and the settings panel only. The Study area now distinguishes four states rather than one: opening, could not be read, no longer in the Library, and nothing opened (ChessView.tsx:203-213), and the move the position search found is carried through as atPly. | There are no breadcrumbs and no Chess-World-level back action, so leaving a subpage by the rail loses where you were inside it. A grep for beforeunload, "unsaved" or any confirmation across the chess components returns nothing. Nothing tests any of it. |

| 037 | Add legal-move visualisation | When a piece is selected, show where it may legally go — taken from the rules engine, never a geometric guess that ignores a pin or an exposed king — and let people switch the markers off for a cleaner board. | partial | yes | Board.tsx:56 takes the markers only from destinationsFrom(fen, square), and they are drawn as a dot on an empty square and a ring on a capture (board.css:49-60). The off switch is board.legalDots (settings.ts:45), carried as data-cw-dots and acted on by settings/chessSettings.css:201-205, which also restates the cursor ring so turning dots off does not lose it. Tests: rules.test.ts 'only legal destinations are offered for a square' and the pinned-bishop case (15/15 pass); settings.test.ts 'every board attribute the component sets is one the stylesheet acts on' and 'every attribute value the component can set has a rule, and there are no rules for values it cannot' (58/58 pass). | Switching the markers off only works while settings/chessSettings.css is loaded, and that file is imported solely by settings/ChessSettings.tsx:28 and settings/ShortcutHelp.tsx:17, both lazy chunks. On a fresh start the document carries data-cw-dots="off" with no rule reading it, so the dots are back until Settings (or the ? help) is opened — the preference looks like it did not save. Board.tsx's own showLegal prop is never wired to the setting by any caller. |
| 038 | Support move notation and annotations | Show standard notation and allow annotations in study and analysis, keeping engine suggestions, the person's own words and a provider's commentary apart, and keep each note attached to its own position so navigating or re-ordering the game never moves it to the wrong move. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:403-440 keys annotations to a tree node id, and keeps three things separate: annotationOf (the person's note and NAGs), sourceComment (what the file carried, analysis.ts:408) and evaluationOf (what the engine said, analysis.ts:451). model.ts:62-64 records a source comment and NAGs 'as the source wrote them'. On screen: analysis/Notes.tsx and analysis/MoveTree.tsx inside the Study screen, with marks from the report. Tests: analysis.test.ts 'a note is attached to a node and follows it through promotion', 'a note follows its node through a removal and a restore, and an empty note is no note', 'an evaluation is only recorded against the position it was calculated in' (36/36 pass). | Two parts are not there. First, the notation choice reaches no move list: settings.ts:973 notateSan (san / figurine / uci) is tested but called only inside settings/ChessSettings.tsx's own preview (lines 556 and 594), while MoveTree.tsx, ArenaGame.tsx, FamousGames.tsx and LiveViewer.tsx all print move.san directly, so choosing ♘f3 or g1f3 changes nothing outside the settings screen. Second, annotations survive neither the session nor an export: AnalysisStudio.tsx holds the session in component state with no save call, so notes and variations are lost on leaving the screen, and chess:to-pgn (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:162) writes the stored game's own moves, so a note written in Study cannot appear in a PGN — which is half of the section's acceptance criterion. |
| 039 | Implement draw and resignation controls | Support valid draw claims and agreed draws, give resignation and game-ending confirmation suited to the session, never treat a draw offer as accepted, and never let someone watching a broadcast resign or alter the recorded game. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/arena.ts:792-810 resign/offerDraw/acceptDraw/declineDraw/withdrawDraw with finish reasons 'resignation' and 'agreed-draw'; arena.ts:598 a move clears a standing offer, because playing on is declining it; arena.ts:530 claimFlag treats a fallen flag against a side that could not mate as a draw and only for the three certain cases. model.ts:90 SESSION_RIGHTS gives 'watch' resign: false, and live/LiveViewer.tsx:175 hands the board interactive={false} with no game-ending control at all. On screen: arena/ArenaGame.tsx:777-788 is a two-step Resign ('Yes, resign' / 'Never mind') with the draw-offer row beside it. Tests: arena.test.ts 'resigning hands the game to the other side, and says who resigned', 'a draw offer is an offer: it does not end the game, and a move declines it' (which asserts an offer is never a result, you cannot accept your own, and you cannot offer twice), and the after-the-end case where no move, resignation or draw is accepted (41/41 pass). | — |
| 040 | Create a position editor | Let people build a custom position — place and remove pieces, choose the side to move, set castling rights and en passant — validate it, warn when it is illegal or unsuitable for a training mode, and never silently turn an invalid position into a different one. | done | yes | editor/PositionEditor.tsx is mounted as the 'Set up a position' area: a piece palette and erase tool, a hand that moves a man anywhere with no rule consulted (PositionEditor.tsx:137-145), 120-deep undo/redo, side-to-move radios, KQkq checkboxes with a note that turns red when the rights do not match the board (PositionEditor.tsx:338-348), an en-passant field with its own problem message, clocks, a FEN box whose Read button leaves the board untouched on failure (PositionEditor.tsx:179-192) and says what it filled in, Copy, and Open for study. The checks are src/shared/chess/variants.ts inspectPosition/readFen plus rules.ts buildFen and playable, with editor/PositionReport.tsx and editor/VariantNotice.tsx showing the faults. Tests: variants.test.ts 'an illegal position is never quietly corrected', the per-fault cases at variants.test.ts:378-470, and 'anything reading had to fill in or drop is said out loud' (44/44 pass); rules.test.ts 'the board can be read and a position can be built by hand' and 'a position the game could never reach is refused, and asking about it does not crash' (15/15 pass). | — |

### Stockfish Engine Infrastructure — 041 to 050

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 041 | Use local Stockfish as the primary engine | Make Stockfish the engine behind AI opponents and analysis, using an official build that fits the operating system and processor, without assuming every machine has the same CPU features, and confirm the engine's identity from what the executable itself says. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts is the only engine in the feature, one instance created at src/main/index.ts:4341 and used by Play, Study, Puzzles and Reference. Identity comes from the handshake, not the filename: engine.ts:54 states it, EngineStatus.name/author are what the engine said in this process (engine.ts:1301) and installedVersion is kept separately as upstream's release tag. No instruction set is guessed: install.ts:84-94 assetPreference prefers the universal Windows build then the baseline one, with the reason written down that Node cannot see whether the processor has AVX2. A person's own binary can be used instead (EngineStatus.binaryFrom 'configured'). Tests: engine.test.ts 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is' and 'an engine somebody chose is used, and a bad choice is reported rather than swapped for another' (39/39 pass); install.test.ts 'the Windows build chosen is the universal one, and never an instruction set nobody checked for' and 'a platform this installer cannot unpack for is said plainly rather than guessed at' (19/19 pass). | — |
| 042 | Establish the engine acquisition and installation flow | Decide whether to bundle Stockfish or fetch it, and if fetched show progress, validate what arrived, survive interruption, report failure, never run an unverified binary, and always leave a clear way to install or repair so the AI Arena is never dead for want of an engine. | done | yes | src/main/chess/install.ts fetches from official-stockfish's own release API, skipping daily dev builds; downloadAsset hashes the bytes as they arrive against the release's sha256 digest, checks the declared length, honours an AbortSignal at four points and reports onProgress({received, total}); a release with no digest is refused unless explicitly allowed, and `verified: false` then stays visible in the status; unpackEngine takes only the executable and the licence. engine.install is safe to repeat, which is the repair path. Wiring: src/main/chess/ipc.ts:264-269 sends chess:engine-progress while installing and chess:engine-changed or chess:engine-failed after; the install button is on three screens — arena/ArenaGame.tsx:234 (which shows the progress messages via onChessEngineProgress at ArenaGame.tsx:193), analysis/AnalysisStudio.tsx:159 and search/ReferencePanel.tsx:86. Tests: install.test.ts 'a download that does not match its checksum is discarded and never left where it could be run', 'a release with no checksum is refused rather than run, unless that is asked for explicitly', 'an archive that is not an archive is refused before anything is written', 'a web page where an archive was expected, and a length that disagrees, are both refused' (19/19 pass); engine.test.ts 'installing downloads the verified archive, keeps the licence, and is safe to repeat' and 'an install that cannot be verified leaves nothing behind and says why' (39/39 pass). | — |
| 043 | Integrate Stockfish through UCI | Talk to Stockfish over the UCI protocol with a process manager that sends valid commands and parses the replies — handshake, readiness, position, search, cancellation, shutdown — and read the output as a stream rather than assuming each message arrives whole. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/uci.ts holds LineReader, parseLine, goCommand and safeOptionValue; src/main/chess/engine.ts drives the full conversation — uci → uciok → read the advertised options → setoption → isready → readyok before anything is called ready (engine.ts:590), then position/go/stop/quit. Tests: engine.test.ts 'a line split across reads, and several lines in one read, are both read correctly', 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes', 'a malformed "bestmove" ends the search honestly instead of leaving it waiting', 'an engine that never answers "uci" is a failed start, not a wait', 'an engine that answers "uci" and then never "readyok" does not reach ready either', 'an option the engine never mentioned is not sent, and one it limits is clamped to its limit' (39/39 pass). | — |
| 044 | Build a reusable engine manager | Route every Chess World feature through one shared engine service that owns startup, search allocation, configuration, cancellation, process health and delivery — reusing the process instead of launching one per button press, while keeping concurrent requests from overwriting each other. | done | yes | One ChessEngine is constructed at src/main/index.ts:4341, handed to registerChessIpc at index.ts:5445 and shut down at quit (index.ts:5665); the only route from the window is chess:analyse / chess:stop-analysis (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:280 and :314), used by analysis/useEngineAnalysis.tsx, arena/ArenaGame.tsx, puzzles/Maker.tsx and settings/ChessSettings.tsx. Requests queue with interactive ahead of batch and ids must be unique so results can be told apart. Tests: engine.test.ts 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once', 'two requests cannot share an id, because then their results could not be told apart', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', 'an update listener that throws does not take the search down with it', 'restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit' (39/39 pass). | — |
| 045 | Manage engine resources | Expose search controls such as time and node limits and the engine's own settings, choose sensible defaults from the device and the person's preferences, never run an unbounded background search, prioritise what the user is waiting for, cancel abandoned work and cap concurrency. | partial | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts AnalysisLimits carries movetime, depth and nodes; Threads and Hash are sized from availableParallelism() and totalmem() and never take every core; src/main/chess/ipc.ts:288-295 clamps each limit and substitutes a 2000 ms limit when a request carries none; priority is interactive or batch; abandoned work is cancelled. Tests: engine.test.ts 'threads and memory are sized for a laptop, and never take every core', 'limits are read defensively, and nonsense does not become an unbounded search', 'a search with no limit at all is refused rather than left to run for ever', 'a depth search with no time limit of its own is still bounded', 'cancelling everything empties the queue and stops what is running', 'an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search' (39/39 pass). | The person's own preferences are not honoured. src/shared/chess/settings.ts keeps engine.limit, thinkMs, depth, lines, threads and hashMb, and settings.ts:916 analysisRequestFrom builds a request from them — but its only caller is settings/ChessSettings.tsx:530, the test button on the settings screen itself. The Study screen uses its own analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:28 DEFAULT_ENGINE_SETTINGS, held in AnalysisStudio.tsx:65 component state that resets every time the screen is opened, so a depth of 24 and three lines chosen in Settings have no effect. Threads and hash can be set in Settings and are never sent, and no node limit is exposed on any screen. |
| 046 | Parse engine evaluations correctly | Read Stockfish's output by its documented UCI fields, keep centipawn scores and mate scores apart, account for which side is to move and which side the interface shows, never print a mate as an ordinary number, and never show an old position's evaluation under the current board. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/evaluation.ts:14 makes cp, mate and none three separate types with nothing converting between them, and 'none' is never drawn as 0.00; evaluation.ts:24 fromEngine turns the engine's side-to-move number into Orbis's White-positive convention once, at the edge. Every update from src/main/chess/engine.ts carries the requestId and the fen it was calculated in, and a bound is passed on marked and never recorded as settled; analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:160-166 says so on screen when the numbers belong to a position the board has left. Tests: engine.test.ts 'a mate score is a mate, and it is turned round for the side to move exactly once', 'a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score', 'a search that only ever reported a bound says so rather than pretending it is settled' (39/39 pass); analysis.test.ts 'an evaluation is only recorded against the position it was calculated in', 'a mate that was there and was not played is reported as that, never as a centipawn loss', 'loss and classification are read from the mover s side, not White s', 'an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match' (36/36 pass). | — |
| 047 | Display principal variations | Show one or more engine lines, turn the coordinates into readable notation against the position they were calculated in, check the line is actually playable, let people explore a line without replacing the real game, and label a line that is incomplete because the search did not finish. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/rules.ts:267 uciLineToSan walks the line move by move and reports legal:false at the first move that will not play; src/main/chess/engine.ts drops the readable line and keeps the coordinates when a PV cannot be played, so the fault is visible rather than papered over. analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:169-178 draws up to five lines with the score, the depth or 'depth not reported', and the notes 'a bound, not a settled score' and 'the rest of this line could not be played out', with 'Thinking…' while the search runs. Exploring never touches the record: the studio's tree is separate and AnalysisStudio.tsx has no save call. Tests: engine.test.ts 'a variation that cannot be played is dropped as notation and kept as coordinates', 'several lines are asked for only within the engine’s own limit, and come back in order', 'an engine with no MultiPV is never asked for it' (39/39 pass); analysis.test.ts 'a line is written out against the position it was calculated in', 'the source game survives being explored, promoted, pruned and restored' (36/36 pass); rules.test.ts 'an engine line is turned into notation only while it stays legal'. | — |
| 048 | Support search progress and cancellation | Show the search's progress — depth, nodes, elapsed time and whatever else the build reports — without assuming every engine emits the same optional fields; send a real stop on cancellation and handle the engine's reply; and never let a late answer from an abandoned search overwrite a newer one. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1228 EngineUpdate carries depth, seldepth, nodes, nps and timeMs, and readUpdate returns null for a missing number rather than 0, so a build that omits a field is handled rather than assumed. Cancellation sends stop and waits for the engine's own bestmove before the engine counts as idle, and every update carries requestId and fen so a mismatch is counted as `ignored` instead of shown. Tests: engine.test.ts 'a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after', 'an AbortSignal cancels a running search, and removes a waiting one without touching the engine', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', 'an engine that ignores "stop" is replaced rather than treated as idle', 'an id is generated when none is given, and it is on every update' (39/39 pass); analysis.test.ts 'an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match', 'each line keeps its own slot, the latest answer replacing the one before it', 'an instruction that points nowhere is ignored rather than acted on' (36/36 pass). | The progress a person actually sees is the depth alone. analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:174 prints 'depth N' or 'depth not reported' and nothing else; the nodes, nps and elapsed time that travel the whole pipeline are displayed nowhere except the engine check inside settings/ChessSettings.tsx:554-555. Neither the Study screen nor the Play screen shows an elapsed-time or node counter while a search runs. |
| 049 | Provide engine diagnostics and recovery | Detect a failed start, a process that dies early, invalid replies and repeated timeouts; capture something useful without needlessly exposing private paths; offer a restart or a check of the configured binary; and keep the person's game and analysis if the engine crashes. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts ChessEngineError carries a code (not-installed, install-failed, bad-path, start-failed, handshake-failed, engine-gone, cancelled, timeout...) and a `recoverable` flag; EngineStatus (engine.ts:265-291) carries detail, lastError, recoverable and a separate `problem` field whose contract is 'something is there and cannot be used or read — never reported as not installed'. A search rejects with the exit code and the engine's last stderr rather than hanging; restart() and shutdown() exist, and a timed-out search restarts the process. The game survives because the engine lives in the main process and a failure arrives on chess:analysis-failed (ipc.ts:310), drawn by analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:154 as a role="alert" with a Clear action while the board, tree and notes stay; search/ReferencePanel.tsx reports the engine's real state and offers the install/repair. Tests: engine.test.ts 'an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again', 'an engine that dies during the handshake is a failure, not a request left searching', 'a search the engine never answers times out, and the engine is restarted', 'an install record that cannot be read is a problem, not a missing engine', 'with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly' (39/39 pass); reference.test.ts 'the engine’s state is read as the engine reports it, and installed is not the same as running', 'an answer the panel does not understand is reported as not known, never bent into a state' (18/18 pass). | — |
| 050 | Meet Stockfish licensing obligations | Check the Stockfish licence and distribution terms before bundling or redistributing it, ship the required notices and source availability, and check the licences of the rules library, board components, tablebases, opening databases, fonts and icons rather than assuming a public download may be redistributed. | done | — | src/main/chess/install.ts:10-30 states the reasoning: Stockfish is GPL-3.0-or-later, so nothing is vendored or packaged — it is fetched from official-stockfish's own release into the person's app-data folder, upstream's Copying.txt is extracted beside the executable, and the install record names the release so the corresponding source is one address away; LICENCE_ID and PROJECT_URL surface in EngineStatus. chess.js is BSD-2-Clause (node_modules/chess.js/package.json), and scripts/generate-notices.mjs collects every installed package's licence text into resources/notices.json as part of `npm run dist`. The opening dataset is lichess-org/chess-openings at a named commit under CC0-1.0, checked against the repository rather than assumed (src/shared/chess/openingsData.ts:1-60). No piece artwork or font is bundled — Board.tsx:19 draws the pieces as Unicode characters for exactly that reason; famousData.ts:17-22 keeps the collection to fifteen hand-typed games for the same reason; src/shared/chess/reference.ts:110-153 states each dataset's licence and that no tablebase is present. Tests: reference.test.ts 'the opening dataset carries the licence that was actually checked' (18/18 pass); openings.test.ts 'the dataset is the one that was licensed, and its size is counted rather than claimed' (19/19 pass); famous.test.ts 'the collection is small, as the licensing reason for it requires' (51/51 pass); install.test.ts 'a missing licence is reported rather than invented' (19/19 pass); engine.test.ts 'installing downloads the verified archive, keeps the licence, and is safe to repeat' (39/39 pass). | — |

On the screen, ArenaGame.tsx:392-410 asks once per `${ply}:${attempt}` key with a MAX_TRIES cap, and :434 stops only its own search by id on unmount. | 057 | Add configurable AI commentary | Offer commentary voices — Friendly Coach, Grandmaster, Witty Rival, Tactical Maniac, Silent Assassin and a Custom Rival — that change tone and how often it speaks without misrepresenting the engine or bending the rules, speak on events that matter rather than after every ordinary move, stay optional, and be remembered. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/commentary.ts:32 five voices (coach, grandmaster, rival, tactical, silent) with :52 COMMENTARY_STYLES; :576 speaksAbout uses a deliberately odd cadence so it does not end up commenting on one colour only; :599 remarkOn produces at most one remark and never repeats the previous line; the voice changes only the wording, since every claim comes from the same fact keys. ArenaGame.tsx:879-901 draws the remark list and hides the whole panel on the silent voice. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/commentary.test.ts: 'the silent style says nothing about anything, ever', 'the cadence decides when an ordinary move is spoken about', 'the same line is not said twice in a row', 'every phrasing in the table is reachable and every speaking style has something to say', 'a style with more than one way of saying something uses them all, and only its own', 'the end of the game is always spoken about, whatever the cadence says'. | Two things. The sixth voice the section names, Custom Rival, does not exist — there is no user-defined voice and nothing with which to write one. And the setting is not remembered, which fails the section's own acceptance criterion: the chosen voice is plain React state at ArenaGame.tsx:136, there is no commentary field anywhere in src/shared/chess/settings.ts (its groups are board, moves, sound, engine, analysis), and a search for 'commentary' across the settings store finds nothing — so switching away from Play and back resets it to the default coach. |
| 058 | Ground chess explanations in evidence | Base every explanation on the real board state and real engine output, keep engine fact apart from interpretation, never invent a candidate move or an unproven forced mate, and say so when the search was too shallow to settle the point instead of presenting a guess as proved. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/commentary.ts:161 MoveFacts are worked out from rules.ts rather than taken on trust; :214 EngineFacts carries the depth reached and a bounded flag, and :235 MIN_JUDGEMENT_DEPTH with :243 `usable = scored && !bounded && deepEnough` means a shallow or merely bounded score is never quoted as a verdict and nothing at all is said about the move; :672 FORBIDDEN_WORDS bars fork, pin, skewer, threat, winning, tempo, weak square, outpost and a dozen more, because none of them can be seen from what this layer has; :697 CLAIM_EVIDENCE pairs every phrase with the fact key that has to be present for it to be said. On screen ArenaGame.tsx:850 shows the engine's reading with its depth and appends 'a bound, not a settled score' when it is one. No language model touches chess anywhere: grep for Orion/askModel across src/shared/chess, src/main/chess and the chess components returns nothing. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/commentary.test.ts: 'no remark in any style about any move contains a word that names something it cannot see', 'every claim a remark makes has the fact that establishes it, and every fact it cites is true', 'an evaluation that cannot be trusted is not used, and nothing is said about the move', 'a forced mate that was missed is said to be exactly that, with no invented number', 'a real evaluation is quoted as what it is, and the arithmetic is the engine’s own'. | — |
| 059 | Implement configurable hints | A ladder of help running from none through strategic hints, identifying threats, candidate moves, the best move and a full principal variation; each hint about the position in front of you, revealing no more than the level chosen, never stale, and recorded when training measures unaided performance. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/arena.ts:245 ASSISTANCE_LEVELS, each with a `reveals` bound written down as data; :719 hintRequest refuses when help is off, when it is not your turn, and while a search is running; :758 revealHint enforces the bound in one place and refuses an answer naming a different request or a different position, so a nudge carries no square and no notation in its text or in any field beside it; hintsTaken feeds :977 metaFor (the OrbisHints tag) and :922 summaryOf's `assisted`, so a game played with help is never recorded as unaided. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/arena.test.ts: 'no help means no hint, and a hint is only ever about the position in front of you', 'the level of help asked for bounds what the hint reveals, in its words and in its fields', 'a hint describes what the move does out of the rules, for every kind of move', 'there is no summary until the game is over, and then it is honest about help taken'. | The ladder has four rungs where the section asks for six. There is no threat-identification rung, no candidate-moves rung offering several moves to weigh, and no full principal variation — and the engine is never even asked for what those rungs would need: hintRequest (arena.ts:723) sends multipv: 1 and revealHint reads only `bestmove`, ignoring any lines. What exists is none / which piece / which piece and what the move does / the move itself. |
| 060 | Provide post-game review | When the game ends, show a summary with the result, how long it was and the moments that mattered, plus a way into Analysis Studio; and if a detailed review is asked for, run a real engine pass and make plain whether it has finished, never estimating accuracy from move counts. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/arena.ts:898 ArenaSummary and :922 summaryOf give the result, how it ended in words, the move count, the level, the time control, the time each side really spent and the hints taken; ArenaGame.tsx:716-772 draws it and offers 'Study this game' when the game was saved, with ChessView.tsx:162 opening AnalysisStudio on that game. The detailed review is a real engine pass: src/renderer/src/components/chess/analysis/ReportPanel.tsx:43 'Analyse the whole game', :49 '{done} of {total} positions searched', :58 positions analysed out of the total and how many were searched too shallowly to judge, and the file's own header records that there is deliberately no accuracy percentage because nobody could check one. Passing tests: src/shared/chess/arena.test.ts 'there is no summary until the game is over, and then it is honest about help taken' and 'the summary leads with how it went for the person, not with the score line'; src/shared/chess/analysis.test.ts 'coverage compares what was asked with what came back', 'a report built from the session uses the evaluations recorded on the game s own nodes', 'walking into a mate is a critical moment of its own, and mate scores read per side'. | The summary names no important moments. ArenaSummary (arena.ts:898) has no field for them and the Arena runs no engine pass of its own at the end of a game, so nothing is identified unless the person goes to Study and presses 'Analyse the whole game' themselves. Critical moments do exist, in the analysis report, but they are two screens and one deliberate action away from the summary the section asks to carry them. |

On screen: src/renderer/src/components/chess/academy/Academy.tsx:47 four tabs (Course, Lesson, Review, Standing), CourseMap.tsx:106 'I already know this', LessonPlayer.tsx:176 saying out loud when a lesson is open only because what comes before it was marked known. Passing tests: src/shared/chess/lessons.test.ts 'a lesson waits for what it needs, and says what it is waiting on', 'a lesson is finished by demonstrating its tasks', 'a curriculum is checked in order, and a lesson cannot need what comes later', 'the real curriculum is what these rules are for'; src/shared/chess/curriculum.test.ts 'the concept graph is sound and every lesson is reachable in order'; src/shared/chess/academyPath.test.ts passes in full. | — |

Every lesson mixes explanation, a diagram and real tasks played on the board (:224 onwards), the language is plain throughout, and :14 records that every position came out of an exhaustive machine search rather than from memory. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/curriculum.test.ts: 'every authored position passes the validator', 'every task can actually be solved by playing its own answer', 'every mate task mates in exactly the number of moves it claims', 'every task says where it came from and offers hints that end with the move'. | The sequence the section asks for begins with the board, coordinates and piece names, and the curriculum does not. The first lesson is how the pieces move; there is no lesson, block or task about the board itself, about reading or finding a square by name, or about which piece is which and how they start. (settings.ts:394 board.coordinates is a display option, not teaching.) Everything from legal movement onward is present and verified. |
| 063 | Teach opening principles | Explain centre control, development, king safety, piece activity and the common opening mistakes, shown through positions and moves rather than prose; distinguish a general principle from an absolute rule, and say that opening decisions depend on the position so an odd-looking move is not automatically losing. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/curriculum.ts:755 openingPrinciples — a four-point list covering a centre pawn, bringing out the knights and bishops before the heavy pieces, castling inside ten moves, and not moving one piece repeatedly or hunting pawns with the queen; three real tasks rather than prose (:786 the knight's squares from the starting position, :800 playing 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 Nc6 3.Bb5 out and seeing it is three moves from castling, :815 the f7 mate), and :781 a warning callout on f7 and f2 for the common mistake. Prerequisites are set at :764 so it opens only after the pieces, captures and checkmate. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/curriculum.test.ts: 'every authored position passes the validator' and 'every task can actually be solved by playing its own answer' both walk this lesson's three tasks. | The section's second requirement is unmet. Nothing anywhere distinguishes these four points from absolute rules, and nothing says an opening choice depends on the position or that a superficially unusual move is not automatically losing — the only caveat of that kind in the whole curriculum is about piece values (curriculum.ts:290), and a search for 'guideline', 'not a rule' and 'depends on the position' across curriculum.ts, openings.ts and the openings components finds nothing. It is also one fifteen-minute lesson, so piece activity and king safety get a line each rather than a lesson. |
| 064 | Teach tactical patterns | Interactive lessons for forks, pins, skewers, discovered attacks, double attacks, deflection, decoys, interference, clearance, removing the defender and other verified motifs, each explaining the pattern, showing a correct example, offering a guided exercise and then independent practice, on legal positions with validated solutions and feedback tied to the move actually played. | partial | yes | Five motif lessons in src/shared/chess/curriculum.ts — :835 the fork, :882 the pin, :948 the skewer, :999 the discovered attack, :1047 removing the defender — each with an explanation and at least one verified exercise carrying a three-rung hint ladder. The positions are machine-checked: :14 records that the material-winning lines were built by enumerating every legal reply, and feedback is tied to the move played by src/shared/chess/attempt.ts:444 judge, which keeps 'cannot be played' apart from 'not the idea' and never says a move loses unless the rules can show it. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/curriculum.test.ts: 'every authored position passes the validator', 'every task can actually be solved by playing its own answer', 'nothing in the curriculum claims an engine opinion'; src/shared/chess/attempt.test.ts and exercise.test.ts also pass. | Five of the ten motifs the section names. And the four-part shape the section asks for is not built: the fork lesson (curriculum.ts:835) is one paragraph and one task, with no worked example diagram and no second exercise for independent practice; the same is true of the skewer, discovered-attack and removing-the-defender lessons, whose assessments need one task each. |

The curriculum's nineteen concepts (src/shared/chess/curriculum.ts:145-164) are the rules, the two basic mates, opening principles, five tactical motifs and three endgame ideas — there is no weak-squares, pawn-structure, open-files, outposts, space, exchanges or long-term-planning concept, lesson, task or module, and the five modules at curriculum.ts:1189-1226 have no positional block. A search of src/shared/chess and the chess components for 'outpost', 'weak square', 'open file', 'pawn structure' and 'long-term plan' finds only the commentary module's FORBIDDEN_WORDS list at commentary.ts:692-693 — the words commentary is barred from using precisely because it cannot see them — plus descriptive prose inside the famous-games and openings datasets. There is no chess course in src/shared/courses/content either. |

Passing tests in src/shared/chess/calculation.test.ts: 'a line is read against the position reached, not the one it started from', 'a line that is right as far as it goes is short, not wrong', 'a line that leaves the answers reports the first place it did, and nothing after it', 'a line that cannot be played is not compared at all', 'typing only your own moves fills in the defences from the exercise', 'an ambiguous move is a notation problem, and the answers are offered'. | The machinery exists; the exercise and the screen do not. No calculation exercises are authored anywhere — nothing builds a set of positions to calculate, and the curriculum's only line work is two play-it-out tasks (curriculum.ts:800 and the rook-endgame bridge) done on a board rather than typed and compared. ### Puzzle Universe — 071 to 080

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 071 | Build a complete puzzle workspace | A dedicated place with tactical puzzles, mating exercises, defensive challenges, strategic positions and endgame puzzles, supporting a daily challenge, themed practice, rating progression, timed sessions and personalised review, with every puzzle from a validated source or a pipeline that verifies the position and the solution. | partial | yes | src/renderer/src/components/chess/puzzles/PuzzleWorkspace.tsx:42 four tabs — Solve, Today, Make, Review — reached from ChessView's Puzzles area (ChessView.tsx:176). The daily challenge is fixed once and recorded rather than recomputed (puzzles.ts:1020 pickDaily with dailyFor, and a streak that counts real completions); themed and rating-band practice via filterPuzzles, puzzleFacets and puzzles.ts:1272 RATING_BANDS; personalised review from missedPuzzles; and every puzzle comes from src/shared/chess/puzzleGen.ts scanning the person's own saved games with the position and solution verified before it is kept. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/puzzles.test.ts: 'the day is picked by the date, so the same day always gives the same puzzle', 'today cannot be rerolled by reopening the app or by making more puzzles', 'a streak counts days that were really finished, not days the app was opened', 'filters work on real stored data', 'the review list is what went wrong last time, newest first, and never new material'; puzzleGen.test.ts and puzzleRating.test.ts also pass. | Two of the five puzzle kinds and two of the five modes are absent. The generator produces only mate-in-n, win-material and find-best (puzzleGen.ts:533, :594, :647), so there are no defensive challenges and no strategic positions, and there is no import path at all — nothing in Maker.tsx reads a file, a provider or a PGN — so the only puzzles anyone will ever hold are mates and material wins from their own games. There are no timed sessions: Solver.tsx:246 shows the seconds spent on one puzzle but nothing runs a set against a clock. And rating progression cannot actually progress: puzzleGen never sets a rating, so every puzzle has rating: null, and puzzleRating.ts:241 makes rateAttempt refuse to move the person's figure on every attempt. |
| 072 | Validate every puzzle position | Before a puzzle is shown, check its FEN, that the position is legal, that the side to move matches, that the stated objective holds and that the solution line works; for imported or generated puzzles verify the intended solution is legal and consistent with the goal; and reject a malformed or ambiguous position instead of quietly displaying it. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/exercise.ts:383 validateExercise deliberately reads nothing from the stored verification and works the answer out again — :406 the start position must be playable, :413 the stated solver must match the side to move, :419 the position must not already be finished, then every line is walked with the twenty-six problem codes at :302 (illegal-move, dead-end, incomplete-replies, objective-not-met, wrong-mate-count, premature-end, line-continues-after-end and the rest), each fault carrying the ply, the move, the FEN and the line it happened in. src/shared/chess/puzzles.ts:576 verifyPuzzle is the only route to a verified stamp and recomputes the checked themes from the check. Generated puzzles are separated into Generated, Rejected and Skipped by puzzleGen.ts, so a moment whose solution did not hold up never becomes a puzzle, and Maker.tsx:373 WhyNothing shows the rejections with their reasons for debugging. Production play excludes them: puzzles.ts:1009 dailyEligible refuses a rejected puzzle, and Solver.tsx:287 prints the verification status and its problems beside the board. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/puzzles.test.ts: 'a broken puzzle gets no themes at all', 'a rejected puzzle is never the day s challenge'; exercise.test.ts and puzzleGen.test.ts also pass. | — |
| 073 | Support multiple tactical objectives | Cover forks, pins, skewers, mating attacks, discovered attacks, deflections, defensive resources and quiet tactical moves; label a puzzle with a theme only when the source or a validated annotation supports it; and let the user practise one chosen theme or a mixed set. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/puzzles.ts:102 PUZZLE_THEMES lists thirty-one themes including fork, pin, skewer, mate and its patterns, discovered attack, deflection, decoy, interference, double attack, hold-draw and zugzwang, each with a label (:111) and a plain-words meaning (:145). The honesty machinery is the strong part: :188 ThemeEvidence distinguishes checked, source, convention and claimed, :200 isFact lets only a checked claim be shown as a fact, :206 CHECKABLE_THEMES names the fourteen the rules can prove, :219 UNCHECKABLE_BECAUSE gives a specific reason for every theme they cannot, and :212 CONVENTION_THEMES marks the ones counted by a stated rule. The workspace toggles any combination of themes (PuzzleWorkspace.tsx:115) for one theme or a mixed set. Passing tests in src/shared/chess/puzzles.test.ts: 'a fork is claimed from what the piece really attacks, and only for a move that gives no check', 'a fork is not claimed when the move is a check, and the reason is written down', 'a theme from a source stays a source claim, and a checked fact beats it', 'a theme name Orbis does not know is left out rather than guessed at', 'every theme has a label and a meaning, and the ones that cannot be checked say why', 'filters work on real stored data'. | Half the objectives the section names can never appear on a puzzle in practice. Pin, skewer, discovered attack, deflection, decoy and interference are all in UNCHECKABLE_BECAUSE (puzzles.ts:219), so they can only arrive from a source — and Orbis ships no puzzle set and has no import, so those filters will always be empty. Defensive resources are in the vocabulary as hold-draw but puzzleGen never produces one. And there is no quiet-tactical-move theme at all. |
| 074 | Implement puzzle move validation | Judge each move against the puzzle's accepted solution tree or stated objective, accepting equivalent correct moves the verified solution permits; on a wrong move explain the result at a suitable level and honour the configured retry behaviour; and do not reveal the whole solution unless it is asked for. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/attempt.ts:444 judge answers legality first and never answers correctness in legality's vocabulary — :355 Verdict separates illegal, not-the-idea (with couldStillBeGood and the moves actually accepted there), on-track, solved and opponent-off-book; siblings in the solution tree are all accepted as correct answers; and :391 materialHolds plus :407 let the rules credit a solve the author never wrote down, so a solver who finds their own mate is told they solved it. Retries are configured per puzzle by src/shared/chess/exercise.ts:90 FailureCondition with :98 STRICT_FAILURE and FORGIVING_FAILURE.
