The resource policy is defined and genuine: one search at a time with interactive before batch (src/main/chess/engine.ts:1147-1150), the queue length reported in the status, and a request with no limit given one (ipc.ts:294). Tests pass — src/main/chess/engine.test.ts, ran it, 39/39: 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once', 'a search with no limit at all is refused rather than left to run for ever', 'a depth search with no time limit of its own is still bounded'; and settings.test.ts 'the engine is asked with exactly one limit, and it is the one that was chosen', 'a request is clamped even when the stored value was not', 'the engine ranges are the ones the engine channel really enforces'. | No concurrency control is offered to the person: threads and hash are sized by the engine and printed as statements because there is no channel to set them (settings.ts:507-516), so the "concurrency settings" the section asks for do not exist. The cost warning is one clause on the depth field ("Each extra one roughly doubles the work"); CPU, power and battery are never mentioned on the screen. |

It is on screen twice: the mounted Reference data area (search/ReferencePanel.tsx:54-66 via engineEvidence in shared/chess/reference.ts:451) with an install button and a re-check timer, and Play (arena/ArenaGame.tsx:180-240), which polls every two seconds during an install precisely because there is no "it failed" push channel, and reports a failed state. Tests pass — engine.test.ts 39/39: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is', 'with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly', 'an install record that cannot be read is a problem, not a missing engine', 'an engine somebody chose is used, and a bad choice is reported rather than swapped for another'; and reference.test.ts 18/18: "the engine's state is read as the engine reports it, and installed is not the same as running", 'a setting that points at a path NEVER makes a dataset present', 'a field the engine did not send is not filled in'. | — |

A file that cannot be read is never written over and the save returns refused; a file that makes no sense is moved aside under its own name with the time on it; a corrupt settings file costs one value. No renderer file calls chessSettings/chessSaveSettings or the other eight data channels (src/preload/index.ts:805-814 are all uncalled); the settings screen writes localStorage under one key, which chessSettingsStore.tsx:18-30 states belongs to the installation rather than the profile "so two accounts on one computer share them". ### Search, Command Palette And Cross-Module Integration — 291 to 300

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 291 | Integrate Chess World with ORBIS search | Let people find chess games, saved positions, studies, repertoires and training content through Orbis's existing search or command interface, from records they are actually allowed to see, with clear categories so a game is not confused with a puzzle or a setting. | partial | yes | Chess World is one capability in the single registry (src/shared/commandCenter.ts:292) with eleven keywords — chess, chessboard, stockfish, pgn, opening, endgame, puzzle, tactics, game, board, play — so typing any of them finds the module (components/CommandPalette.tsx:69-75 via searchCapabilities). Profile isolation is real at the service level: chess:search runs ChessStore.search (src/main/chess/store.ts:201) over this.all(), a collection opened at personalFile(userData, profileId, …), and matches only fields that are genuinely stored. | No record is findable from Orbis search. chessSearch is registered and exposed (src/preload/index.ts:792) and has no renderer caller at all; games, saved positions, studies, repertoires and training content appear nowhere outside Chess World's own screens. Because no chess result ever reaches the palette, there are no result categories separating a game, an opening line, a puzzle and a settings action. |
| 292 | Register Chess World commands | Register useful Chess World actions with the existing command palette — open the library, create a study, open Analysis Studio, start a training mode — resolving to real actions, asking for a missing game rather than picking one, and not intercepting other Orbis commands. | partial | yes | The one registered command resolves to a real action: capability.app becomes the window's feature id (components/CommandPalette.tsx:73 and take() at 89-93) and App.tsx:1110 mounts ChessView for feature === 'chess'. It comes from the same registry as the launcher (lib/features.ts:53-69), so nothing is replaced or intercepted. src/shared/commandCenterDestinations.test.ts and src/main/ipcMapping.test.ts both pass (6/6 each; I ran ipcMapping) and assert every capability either leads somewhere real or is refused with a reason naming where it lives. | Exactly one command exists, and all it does is open the module. None of the four actions the section names is registered, even though the registry already has the target shapes for them ({ kind: 'do', message } and { kind: 'view', view }). Opening Chess World lands on whichever of the fifteen areas was last remembered (ChessView.tsx:99-107), so a command cannot ask for a particular screen, and nothing asks for a missing game or position because no command needs one. |
| 293 | Support contextual navigation | Link related records across the product — a critical moment in a report opening that game position, a puzzle linking back to the game it came from — passing stable identifiers and state rather than relying on whatever panel is on screen. | partial | yes | The shell's study(id, ply?) passes a stable record id and an optional ply and is wired from Play, Famous games, Live, the position editor and the position search (ChessView.tsx, and its comment records that taking only an id once sent every "24… Nf3" button to move one). The report's critical moments and missed mates are buttons that jump to a ply (analysis/ReportPanel.tsx:96,115 → jumpPly at AnalysisStudio.tsx:372), and the Studio opens at a requested ply (AnalysisStudio.tsx:139-144). A generated puzzle carries its origin as stable tags, game:<id> and ply:<n> (shared/chess/puzzleGen.ts:539,602,654), with gameIdOf to read it back (puzzles.ts:1250). | The section's second example is data only: gameIdOf has no caller in the renderer, so a puzzle made from one of your games offers no way back to it. And nothing passes context into Chess World from outside — App.tsx:1110 mounts it with onClose and onNotify and no record or state — so cross-module navigation to a chess record does not exist in either direction. |
| 294 | Preserve navigation history | Keep a profile-aware history of recently opened games, positions and studies, let people go back to a previous context without resetting a running game or losing unsaved annotations, and not log every board click as a step. | not started | no | The only thing remembered is which of the fifteen areas you were last in — one value in localStorage, validated against the real area list (ChessView.tsx:96-107 and 139-145). | There is no history of recently opened games, positions or studies anywhere in the tree — no "recent" anything in src/renderer/src/components/chess or src/shared/chess — and the open game id is not kept across a close. The second requirement is actively broken: the shell renders one area at a time from a switch, so leaving Play unmounts ArenaGame, whose whole session (setup, side, difficulty, clock, commentary, arena state) is local React state with no save or resume (arena/ArenaGame.tsx:127-143), and coming back resets a game in progress. |
| 295 | Implement safe cross-module actions | When another Orbis module asks for a chess operation, run it through the normal Chess World service layer — no bypassing move validation, profile isolation or file safeguards — and return a clear error for an unsupported request while leaving the chess state untouched. | partial | — | Every way into Chess World is the one service layer and it validates: registerChessIpc (src/main/chess/ipc.ts) takes unknown on all 31 channels, caps strings, clamps numbers, checks a FEN with checkFen before a position is created or analysed (91, 285-286), and the store replays moves through the rules rather than trusting them (store.ts:38-51 via buildMoves — a file edited by hand cannot put an illegal game back), writing to a per-profile path. An absent optional service returns a stated reason instead of throwing (ipc.ts:202, 214, 221, 232, 260). src/main/ipcMapping.test.ts passes (6/6, I ran it) and asserts no channel is registered in two places. | No other Orbis module can request a chess operation at all: there is no cross-module request surface, no action vocabulary, and therefore no unsupported-request path that returns a clear error and leaves the active chess state unchanged. The guarantee the section wants holds only because nothing exercises it. |
| 296 | Connect chess records with local files | Where Orbis Files is available, open and save game records, PGNs, FEN files and exported studies through that file-management workflow, keep provenance, distinguish an external file from an internal record, and never silently overwrite the source file. | partial | yes | Import and export are honest and safe: chess:import-pgn reports what happened to every game in the file including the move that stopped each failure, and chess:export-pgn always goes through a Save dialog the person names (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:119-159). Provenance is kept and shown — an imported game records { kind: 'import', file: <basename>, importedAt } (store.ts:166) and a kept broadcast records the provider, the provider's game id, the URL and when it was retrieved (ipc.ts:247), which the Library prints as a mark (library/GameRow.tsx:59, library.ts:404). Because only the base name is stored, there is no path to overwrite, so editing a game cannot touch the source file. | Orbis Files is not involved at all: src/files has no PGN or FEN knowledge, nothing registers chess as a handler for a file type, and chess:import-pgn-text — whose own comment says it is for "a file picked elsewhere (Files, a drop)" — is called only by the Library's paste box (library/GameLibrary.tsx:466). Exported studies can only be saved through the OS dialog. src/main/chess/store.ts has no test file, so the provenance recording itself is untested. |
| 297 | Connect chess learning to ORBIS Courses where appropriate | If Orbis Courses supports cross-module lessons, surface chess learning activities through that existing mechanism, reusing the course infrastructure rather than building a second one, and without changing the course engine's completion rules for chess. | not started | no | The mechanism exists and is enforced: src/shared/courses/types.ts:97 defines a { kind: 'lab'; appId; title; does } block and src/shared/courses/validate.ts:101-111 and :352 refuse a lab whose appId is not a known one. | Nothing chess uses it. There is no chess course and no chess lab block anywhere in src/shared/courses; the only mentions of chess there are four lines of prose in the AI course (content/ai/unit1.ts:57,66,477 and unit5.ts:326,340). Chess instead carries its own parallel curriculum — curriculum.ts, academyPath.ts, lessons.ts and the Academy screens — which is exactly the second course-management system the section warns against. |
| 298 | Support local reminders and notifications | Let people schedule optional reminders for study sessions and unfinished training through Orbis's existing notification mechanism, respecting their settings, quiet periods and profile, without nagging merely because a session is saved and incomplete. | not started | no | Orbis has the mechanism — src/shared/notifications.ts with sources, categories (one of which is 'study'), priorities, statuses and held/suppressed handling, plus src/shared/notificationRoute.ts and the schedule layer under src/shared/temporal. | Chess appears in none of it. There is no chess notification source or category, nothing schedules a chess reminder, and no file in src/shared/chess or src/main/chess references the notification or schedule layers. The records a reminder would be about do exist and are kept per profile (the review schedule and attempts in chess-training.json) and nothing reads them for this purpose. |
| 299 | Define integration contracts and failure behaviour | Document the interfaces between Chess World and Orbis navigation, search, profiles, files, notifications and courses — inputs, outputs, permissions, failure states, who owns the stored data — avoid circular initialisation, and keep Chess World working when an optional module is off. | partial | — | The window-to-main boundary is fully specified and enforced: all 31 chess channels are listed with what each does in docs/ipc-mapping.md:110-112 and docs/chess-world.md:143-183, and src/main/ipcMapping.test.ts (6/6 pass, I ran it) fails if a registered channel is undocumented or registered twice. Failure behaviour is defined and contained: a missing engine or broadcast provider returns a stated reason rather than throwing (ipc.ts:202,214,221,232,260,266,282), every data answer carries notes and refused with refused meaning nothing was written (data.ts:44-56), and the rules, library, puzzles, Academy and openings all work with no engine and no network. docs/chess-world.md:183-264 documents the three outside pieces, their licences and why the engine is not bundled. | The integrations the section actually lists are unspecified. There is no contract for Chess World against Orbis navigation, search, files, notifications or courses — no inputs, outputs, permissions, failure states or data ownership for any of them — mostly because four of the five do not exist. And docs/chess-world.md is now stale on the two it does cover: its limitations still say ChessView mounts none of the sixty screens and that nothing but games is stored, both of which have changed. |
| 300 | Establish the third milestone acceptance gate | Before going further, prove the whole third block works as released by running fourteen end-to-end checks — a training session start to finish, recovering an interrupted exercise, a calculation line, a blindfold reconstruction, a historical replay, a custom position round trip, a variant, 2D/3D switching, keyboard-only play, settings that really change behaviour, a profile switch with no leakage, invoking chess from Orbis search or commands, optional modules missing, and no regression to existing Orbis shortcuts. | not started | — | The unit suite underneath is real and broad: scripts/testFiles.mjs:194-268 lists 38 chess test files that npm test runs, and every one I ran passes — settings 58/58, main/chess/data 25/25, main/chess/engine 39/39, reference 18/18, three/support 8/8, ipcMapping 6/6. | Not one of the fourteen end-to-end runs exists. Nothing drives the real application: no Electron or debugging-port harness touches chess (the scripts/*-shots.mjs harnesses cover other screens) and docs/chess-world.md:303 still records that no line of it has been seen running. Several items also cannot pass as built — keyboard-only board interaction (fifteen of the sixteen chess chords have no handler; the board has no aria-activedescendant and no live region), settings that change actual behaviour (orientation, notation, the eval bar and the engine arrow are stored and unread), a profile switch without exposure (chess settings live in installation-wide localStorage), invoking chess actions through Orbis search or commands (one command, which only opens the module), and playing a supported variant (shared/chess/variants.ts:347-350 says Orbis can really play one, standard). |

Tests: analysis.test.ts 'an update from the other process is checked, not trusted' and 'an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match' (36 pass); engine.test.ts 'an id is generated when none is given, and it is on every update'. | — |
| 304 | Separate engine management from chess-session management | Keep the engine's processes and protocol apart from games, positions, variations and notes, so restarting the engine does not disturb the open game and opening another game does not restart the engine. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts imports only uci/evaluation/rules/install — nothing about games; src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:84-114 AnalysisSession owns the tree, cursor, notes and undo and imports no engine; src/main/chess/store.ts and data.ts hold games and preferences and never reference the engine; restart (engine.ts:1063-1068) touches only the child process. Tests: engine.test.ts 'restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit' runs with no session at all, and analysis.test.ts 'the source game survives being explored, promoted, pruned and restored' runs with no engine at all — both pass. | — |
| 305 | Define the engine lifecycle state machine | Give the engine an explicit lifecycle with at least thirteen named states, say which transitions are legal and what triggers them, make every transition visible in diagnostics, reject invalid transitions, and never start a second process for an engine already running. | partial | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:67-76 names nine states (not-installed, installing, stopped, starting, handshaking, ready, searching, stopping, failed); duplicate starts are prevented by engine.ts:980-982 and the shared startingUp promise. Tests that pass: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello', 'an engine that never answers "uci" is a failed start, not a wait', 'an engine that answers "uci" and then never "readyok" does not reach ready either', 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once' (five searches, one process). | Four of the thirteen asked-for states have no representation at all (Validating, Initialising, Restarting, Shutting down — and Undiscovered/Discovered/Unavailable are folded into 'not-installed'). There is no declared transition table and no validation: note() at engine.ts:839-842 assigns this.phase unconditionally, so no invalid transition can be rejected and no test can show one being rejected. Transitions are also not diagnosed — note() never calls log(), and nothing is pushed to the window, so a state change is only visible by polling chess:engine-status. |
| 306 | Establish process ownership and isolation | One manager owns the engine process and keeps its handle, its three streams and its process id, knows which engine owns the running search, prevents duplicate processes, and when the engine dies marks its requests failed instead of leaving a search spinning. | partial | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:323-371 EngineProcess holds the child, sets up stdout, stderr and close, and :295-309 keeps the active search on the instance that owns it; :544-564 finishGone fails the running search and rejects every waiter with the exit detail and the engine's last words; :1021-1032 reports an unexpected exit; :739-763 shutdown then kill. Tests that pass: 'an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again', 'an engine that dies during the handshake is a failure, not a request left searching', 'restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit'. | The process identifier is never retained or reported — a grep for '.pid' across src/main/chess finds nothing, and EngineStatus (engine.ts:265-291) has no field for it, so the diagnostics cannot name the process they are about. An unexpected exit is also not announced to dependent surfaces: it sets the phase and fails the one active search, but nothing sends an engine-state event, so a window with no search in flight learns of it only on the next chess:engine-status poll. |
| 307 | Select compatible engine binaries | Pick a Stockfish build that really matches the operating system and processor, from a legitimate distribution under its own licence, with a documented fallback when the processor's capabilities cannot be detected, and never execute an arbitrary file somebody pointed at without checking it. | done | yes | src/main/chess/install.ts:82-94 assetPreference (win32 x64 and arm64 only, universal build first, baseline second, nothing instruction-set specific), :102-130 chooseAsset which says plainly why no asset fits, :433-466 checkBinaryPath (absolute, .exe on Windows, no newline or null byte, must be an existing file); the asset's host is checked against the project's own. The fallback is documented at docs/chess-world.md:243-245. Tests that pass: install.test.ts 'the Windows build chosen is the universal one, and never an instruction set nobody checked for', 'a platform this installer cannot unpack for is said plainly rather than guessed at', 'an asset is ignored when its address is not the project’s own host', 'a binary path is checked, and a relative one or a line break is refused' (19 pass). | — |
| 308 | Define engine installation and distribution policy | Decide and document how Stockfish reaches the user — bundled, fetched or user-supplied — weighing size, licence and offline use; verify any download; and give an honest engine-unavailable state with real instructions. | done | yes | Policy decided and documented at docs/chess-world.md:202-246 (not bundled, GPL-3.0, fetched from upstream's own release into the person's app-data folder, Windows only, universal build, licence travels with the binary, only the executable and licence unpacked). Code: src/main/chess/install.ts:227-296 downloadAsset hashes as it arrives and refuses a release with no published digest unless allowUnverified is passed, :321-354 unpackEngine, :359-413 the install record with verified/licenceId/sourceUrl; engine.ts:893-976 install is safe to repeat and stops anything running first. The unavailable state and the manual-install route are on screen at analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:58-78. Tests that pass: 'a download is kept only when its bytes hash to what the release published', 'a download that does not match its checksum is discarded and never left where it could be run', 'a release with no checksum is refused rather than run, unless that is asked for explicitly', 'a missing licence is reported rather than invented', 'installing downloads the verified archive, keeps the licence, and is safe to repeat'. | — |
| 309 | Keep engine configuration local | Store the chosen engine path, the engine options and the preferred search limits in Orbis's own local configuration, re-validate them every time they are loaded, keep game data out of that file, and never let a bad value stop Chess World opening. | partial | yes | Search limits are stored per profile and validated field by field: src/main/chess/data.ts:168-243 ChessDataStore writes personal/chess-settings.json through readSettingsFile, src/shared/chess/settings.ts:287-293 ENGINE_RANGES and :916-931 analysisRequestFrom clamp on the way out. Install facts live apart from games in installed.json (install.ts:357-413) and hold nothing personal. A missing engine does not block the screen — engine.test.ts 'with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly' and data.test.ts 'costs the value and nothing else' both pass (25 pass). | The chosen engine path is not stored anywhere. engine.ts:854 reads it only from the constructor or ORBIS_CHESS_ENGINE_PATH, and src/main/index.ts:4341 passes only `home`, so there is no screen or settings field that can point Orbis at an engine and nothing persists such a choice between runs (checkBinaryPath does validate it whenever discover runs). Engine options are not stored either: Threads and Hash are recomputed from the machine on every start (engine.ts:1044-1055) and their two settings fields are honestly drawn as statements rather than controls (settings.ts:507-516, reach 'engine-sized'), so what the person sees about them is a report, not a stored configuration. |
| 310 | Establish engine startup acceptance tests | Automate tests for discovery, validation, launch, handshake, options, readiness, search, parsing, cancellation and shutdown; a test double may stand in only for protocol and lifecycle, real integration must run a genuine Stockfish, and the report must say which is which. | partial | — | src/main/chess/engine.test.ts covers all ten areas against a scripted process (39 cases, all pass) and install.test.ts covers discovery and validation (19 cases, all pass); both are in the npm test list at scripts/testFiles.mjs:209-210. The double is labelled as one at engine.test.ts:23-32 and again at docs/chess-world.md:328-331. | There is no genuine Stockfish integration test, so the report cannot distinguish simulated from real — there is nothing real in it. docs/chess-world.md:328-331 says so outright: 'no test in the repository has ever run the real binary'. Nothing reads ORBIS_CHESS_ENGINE_PATH in a test, and there is no opt-in integration runner. |

### Uci Protocol Implementation — 311 to 320

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 311 | Implement a dedicated UCI communication layer | Put all UCI work — building commands, reading the stream, parsing messages, enforcing lifecycle rules — in one testable component, and keep it out of the user interface and the game controller. | done | — | src/shared/chess/uci.ts is the whole parsing and serialising layer (parseInfo, parseOption, parseLine, LineReader, goCommand, safeOptionValue) with no process and no network in it; src/main/chess/engine.ts:323-764 is the single conversation that uses it. A grep for parseLine, parseInfo, LineReader, goCommand and safeOptionValue finds them only in uci.ts and engine.ts — nothing in src/renderer or in the game modules builds or reads a UCI string. Tested at byte level through engine.test.ts 'a line split across reads, and several lines in one read, are both read correctly' and 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes' (both pass). | — |
| 312 | Implement the UCI initialisation handshake | Send uci, wait for the identification, set the supported options, send isready, wait for readyok, apply timeouts with useful diagnostics on failure, and accept no analysis before that sequence is finished. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:591-602 handshake and ready, :1008-1020 the start sequence (handshake, set options, ready) with a kill and a recoverable failure on error, :222-241 the handshake and ready timeouts, :566-586 per-waiter timeout producing 'The engine did not …'; analyse queues and pump awaits start (engine.ts:1156-1175), so nothing is searched before readyok. Tests that pass: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is' (asserts the exact command order uci, three setoptions, isready, ucinewgame, isready, position, go), 'an engine that never answers "uci" is a failed start, not a wait', 'an engine that answers "uci" and then never "readyok" does not reach ready either'. | — |
| 313 | Parse engine identity and capabilities | Record what the engine says it is called, its version where it gives one, and the options it advertises; configure only advertised options; and report anything it did not say as unavailable rather than inventing it. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:402-410 captures id name/author and every advertised option, :611-626 refuses to set an option the engine never mentioned, :1278-1280 advertised() returns only what this process heard, :1289-1317 status reports name/author as null before a handshake and labels installedVersion as the release tag rather than the engine's claim; the name is shown at analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:59-64. Test that passes: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is' — asserts no name, no author and no options before the handshake, then the exact Hash spin limits, the UCI_Variant vars, the button type and the full option count afterwards; and 'an option the engine never mentioned is not sent, and one it limits is clamped to its limit'. | — |
| 314 | Implement safe command serialisation | Write to the engine through one controlled writer so commands cannot interleave or arrive out of order, and validate every value that goes into a command instead of pasting input into the protocol. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:387-395 is the single writer and refuses any line containing a carriage return or newline; :1153-1211 pump runs one job at a time and :646-647 refuses a go while a bestmove is still owed; :1267-1275 refuses an option change while anything is searching. Values are checked before they are written: clampOption (engine.ts:192-218), safeOptionValue (uci.ts:206-210), cleanLimits (engine.ts:784-798), and the option name itself is screened at engine.ts:407. Tests that pass: 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once' (the scripted engine counts overlapping go commands and the count must stay zero), 'an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search', 'an option value is kept inside what the engine said it accepts, or not sent at all' (includes a value containing a newline being refused, not escaped). | — |
| 315 | Implement robust standard-output parsing | Read the engine's output as a stream, not as lines: handle half lines, several lines in one read, long lines and stream closure, parse only complete messages, and survive malformed output without taking anything else down. | done | — | src/shared/chess/uci.ts:170-197 LineReader buffers, splits on CR/LF, keeps the unfinished tail, caps the buffer at a million characters and has flush() for what is left when the stream closes; src/main/chess/engine.ts:348-351 feeds every chunk through it and :366-370 flushes on close; :439-445 drops a line that says nothing and :434-436 logs anything unrecognised instead of throwing. Tests that pass: 'a line split across reads, and several lines in one read, are both read correctly' (one read ends mid-word and the next begins mid-word), 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes' (bare 'info', 'info depth', non-numeric values, free text, blank line, nonsense line), 'a malformed "bestmove" ends the search honestly instead of leaving it waiting'. | — |
| 316 | Handle standard-error output and process diagnostics | Drain the error stream continuously so the pipe cannot block, keep a bounded amount of it for diagnostics, and never let error text be mistaken for an evaluation. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:353-361 attaches a data listener to stderr and drains it always, trimming each line to 300 characters and keeping at most 50 (splicing the oldest 25 away), logging each as 'engine stderr: …'; :373-375 stderrTail exposes the last three for a failure message. stderr never reaches take()/parseLine, so it cannot become an info or bestmove. The tail is only in memory and dies with the process. Test that passes: 'an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again' — the rejection carries the exit code and the engine's last words. | — |
| 317 | Parse UCI informational messages | Read the info fields — depth, seldepth, score, principal variation, nodes, nodes per second, time, hash usage — treating every one as optional, accepting any valid order, never turning a missing evaluation into zero, and tolerating fields it does not know. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/uci.ts:13-32 makes every field optional, :60-120 parseInfo is a key-driven loop so order does not matter, handles depth, seldepth, multipv, nodes, nps, time, hashfull, tbhits, currmove, currmovenumber, score cp and mate, lowerbound/upperbound both standalone and trailing a score, pv collected until the next known key, 'string' free text and any unmodelled field kept in rest for diagnostics; a missing score becomes { kind: 'none' } at engine.ts:458 and evaluation.ts:19 says it is never shown as 0; the panel prints 'depth not reported' when depth is absent (EnginePanel.tsx:174). Tests that pass: 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes' (asserts a line with a depth and a variation but no score produces an update whose score kind is 'none'), 'a mate score is a mate, and it is turned round for the side to move exactly once', 'a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score'. | — |
| 318 | Handle the `bestmove` response correctly | Treat bestmove as the final answer for that search, check the move against the position and variant it was asked about, cope with no legal move, and never apply it if the request that caused it is stale. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:476-542 settles the search on bestmove, validates the move with applyMove against the search's own fen and says so plainly when it will not play, validates the ponder move after the best move, and :478-487 consumes a bestmove belonging to an abandoned search without reporting it; uci.ts:155-159 turns '(none)' and '0000' into no move. Orbis only ever asks about standard chess — src/shared/chess/variants.ts refuses analyse for the rest and arena/ArenaGame.tsx:255 asks as 'standard'. Tests that pass: 'a move the engine offers that cannot be played is refused, and said so', 'a malformed "bestmove" ends the search honestly instead of leaving it waiting' (no move, note says so, the evaluation it did reach is still given), 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position'. | — |
| 319 | Implement readiness barriers | Use isready as a barrier when a configuration change must finish before the next step, track which barrier is outstanding so a late reply is not read as confirmation of something else, and keep the command order unambiguous since the protocol gives no reply identifiers. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:650-659 waits for readyok after ucinewgame and again after a MultiPV change before any go; :420-427 resolves readyok against a first-in-first-out list of waiters, each with its own timeout (:566-586), so a delayed reply settles the oldest outstanding barrier and never a newer one; overlapping barriers are prevented rather than disambiguated — the service refuses an option change while anything is searching (:1267-1275) and runs one job at a time (:1153-1211). Tests that pass: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is' asserts the whole conversation in order with no command sent between a question and its answer, and 'an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search'. | — |
| 320 | Build protocol conformance tests | Test initialisation, readiness, supported and unsupported options, search progress, multiple lines, cancellation, terminal positions, malformed output, process exit and restart — including messages with missing optional fields — against a real Stockfish for integration, with separate unit tests for the parsing edge cases. | partial | — | src/main/chess/engine.test.ts covers initialisation, readiness, an option the engine does not advertise and one it limits, search progress, MultiPV in and out of order, an engine with no MultiPV, cancellation by stop and by AbortSignal, late results dropped, malformed output, missing optional fields, process exit mid-search and during the handshake, timeout, and restart — 39 cases, all pass. | Two of the required cases are untested: a terminal position answered with 'bestmove (none)' (uci.ts:157 handles the literal but nothing exercises it — the only null-move test uses a truncated 'bestmove'), and there is no run against a genuine Stockfish, which the section requires for integration (docs/chess-world.md:328-331 confirms the real binary has never been run). There is also no separate unit test for the parser: src/shared/chess/uci.ts has no uci.test.ts and is only reached through engine.test.ts, which docs/chess-world.md:333-336 records as a gap. |

### Search Execution And Resource Management — 321 to 330

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 321 | Define the search-request lifecycle | Give each search explicit states such as queued, running, stopping, completed, cancelled, interrupted or failed; record its session, position, engine instance, limits, start time and outcome; and let a result touch the screen only if it belongs to a live request or is deliberately kept as history. | partial | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:768-781 Job records requestId, fen, limits, priority and newGame; :295-309 ActiveSearch adds startedAt and done; :265-291 status exposes the running request (id, fen, startedAt) and the queued count. Matching is complete and tested: every update carries requestId and fen (engine.ts:455-469), and src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1333-1336 refuses anything that fails either check and counts it. Tests that pass: 'an id is generated when none is given, and it is on every update', 'two requests cannot share an id, because then their results could not be told apart', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', analysis.test.ts 'an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match'. | There is no explicit per-request state. A search's state is implied by which structure holds it — the queue, this.current, or a settled promise — and no value anywhere names queued, starting, running, stopping, completed, cancelled, interrupted or failed for a given request, so nothing can report or test a request's own lifecycle. The originating session and the engine instance are also not recorded on the request, so a result cannot be attributed to the engine that produced it. |
| 322 | Support time-limited searches | Let a search run for a validated time budget, measure the elapsed time shown with a clock that cannot jump when the wall clock changes, and collect the final result when the budget runs out. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/uci.ts:199-206 builds go movetime; src/main/chess/engine.ts:784-798 clamps a think time to 1–600000 ms and src/main/chess/ipc.ts:289 clamps it again to 50–600000 at the channel; engine.ts:662-688 gives the engine its budget plus a grace period and fails the request honestly if nothing comes back; the settled result is collected at :520-541. No elapsed duration is derived from the wall clock anywhere — what the screen shows is the engine's own reported time (settings/ChessSettings.tsx:558, EnginePanel.tsx:174), and Date.now() appears only as a timestamp recording when an evaluation was stored (AnalysisStudio.tsx:128). Tests that pass: 'nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is' asserts the literal 'go movetime 10'; 'a search the engine never answers times out, and the engine is restarted'; settings.test.ts 'the engine is asked with exactly one limit, and it is the one that was chosen' and 'the engine ranges are the ones the engine channel really enforces' (58 pass). | — |
| 323 | Support node-limited searches | Let a user cap a search by number of positions searched, validate and record that limit, use the engine's own node command, and report the real completion condition rather than treating it as a time limit. | partial | no | The service can do it: src/shared/chess/uci.ts:199-206 goCommand emits 'go nodes N', src/main/chess/engine.ts:784-798 validates and caps a node budget at 1e12, src/main/chess/ipc.ts:291 accepts nodes and clamps them to 1000–1e10, and a nodes-only search is bounded by maxSearchMs rather than being turned into a time limit (engine.ts:662-664). Test that passes: 'limits are read defensively, and nonsense does not become an unbounded search' asserts the nodes clamp. | Nothing ever asks for one. src/shared/chess/settings.ts:916-931 analysisRequestFrom builds only a time or a depth, ENGINE_RANGES (settings.ts:287-293) has no node range, and no screen offers a node control — nodes appear in the window only as a number reported back ('positions looked at', ChessSettings.tsx:558). No test sends a node-limited search, so nothing has ever checked that 'go nodes' is what goes out. And the result has no field saying which limit ended the search, so a node-limited search cannot report its actual completion condition. |
| 324 | Support depth-limited searches responsibly | Let a depth be chosen, explain that a depth is not a fixed amount of time or a guarantee of quality, keep unreasonable values from exhausting the machine, and give safe defaults with documented limits. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/settings.ts:289 depth range 1–40 with the reason documented at :276-285, :491 says in words that a depth 'gives you the same answer on every computer, but a hard position can take a long time', :500-501 the field reaches the engine request; src/main/chess/ipc.ts:290 clamps 1–40 at the channel and src/main/chess/engine.ts:795 caps at 99; a depth search with no time of its own is still bounded by maxSearchMs (engine.ts:662-664, DEFAULT_TIMEOUTS at :235-241); the control is on screen at analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:31 and :116-129 with the sentence 'A fixed depth, however long it takes.' Tests that pass: engine.test.ts 'a depth search with no time limit of its own is still bounded', settings.test.ts 'the engine ranges are the ones the engine channel really enforces' and 'the engine is asked with exactly one limit, and it is the one that was chosen'. | — |
| 325 | Implement multi-principal-variation searches | Ask the engine for several lines where it supports that, validate the number against what it advertises and against sensible limits, never assume the requested number of lines comes back, and keep each line's evaluation and continuation with its own line. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:631-635 reads the ceiling from the engine's own advertised MultiPV option and returns 1 when there is none, :655-659 clamps the request to that ceiling and only sets the option when it has changed, with a readiness barrier after; each line is kept in its own slot (:472, :519) and only a settled line is recorded; src/main/chess/ipc.ts:307 clamps to 1–5; the receiving end keeps slots apart at src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1337-1350, and EnginePanel.tsx:169-180 draws whatever lines actually arrived with each one's own score, depth and continuation. Tests that pass: 'several lines are asked for only within the engine’s own limit, and come back in order', 'an engine with no MultiPV is never asked for it', analysis.test.ts 'each line keeps its own slot, the latest answer replacing the one before it'. | — |
| 326 | Implement a search scheduler | One scheduler must decide what the chess engine works on next, telling apart searches somebody is waiting on from batch jobs and low-priority background work, so a whole-game analysis cannot make the game you are playing unresponsive, and every engine operation must have one clear owner. | partial | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:817 (the queue), :1146 `take()` takes any interactive job before any batch one, :1153 `pump()` runs exactly one search at a time and never lets two UCI conversations overlap; every job carries its own requestId and resolves to its own caller (:1099). Tested and passing: src/main/chess/engine.test.ts:825 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once' (whole file 39/39 pass, 36.9s). src/renderer/src/components/chess/arena/ArenaGame.tsx:438 stops only its own search by id, never everything. | There are two tiers, not three: no low-priority background tier exists. Worse, nothing in the running app can ever use the batch tier — src/main/chess/ipc.ts:306 hardcodes `priority: 'interactive'` on every request from the window, and `'batch'` appears nowhere outside engine.test.ts. So the whole-game sweep in AnalysisStudio queues at exactly the same priority as a live Arena move, which is the starvation case the section is about. No test exercises two modules asking at once through the real channel. |
| 327 | Prevent stale results from changing the active board | Each search gets a unique id and a fingerprint of the position, and an answer is checked against both before it is shown, so an answer that arrives after you have navigated away is never displayed as the new position's evaluation — and the check must not rely on move numbers. | done | yes | Every update and result carries `requestId` and `fen` (src/main/chess/engine.ts:126-145); late `info` lines for a finished or cancelled search are dropped at the source (:439-443). The window checks both: src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1333 `acceptUpdate` rejects a mismatched id or a mismatched `positionKey` and counts what it dropped; positionKey (src/shared/chess/rules.ts:288) is placement+turn+castling+en-passant, explicitly not the move number. Recording is guarded the same way (analysis.ts:444). The panel labels numbers from a position the board has left rather than showing them (src/renderer/src/components/chess/analysis/EnginePanel.tsx:45, :162-167) and prints the dropped count (:191). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:651 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position'; src/shared/chess/analysis.test.ts:637 'an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match' and :234 'an evaluation is only recorded against the position it was calculated in' (36/36 pass). | — |
| 328 | Implement reliable search cancellation | A cancelled search must send the UCI stop and then wait for the engine's bestmove before the engine is treated as reusable, and if that never comes the engine must be recovered rather than given another search while its state is unknown. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:719 `cancel()` fails the request at once but keeps an `owed` flag until the bestmove arrives (:336, :476-487), so a later `go` waits for it (:646); a stop that is never answered returns false and the process is thrown away and replaced (:1213 `cancelCurrent`, :1063 `restart`). Timeouts escalate the same way (:1194). Reachable as the Stop button (EnginePanel.tsx:149) and the sweep's Cancel (AnalysisStudio.tsx:267). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:606 'a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after', :689 'an engine that ignores "stop" is replaced rather than treated as idle', :780 'a search the engine never answers times out, and the engine is restarted'. | — |
| 329 | Define search replacement behaviour | When a new request supersedes an old one there must be a stated policy on whether the old search is cancelled, allowed to finish or kept as a background job; stale interactive searches should normally go, but positions a batch job has already finished must be kept — results must not be thrown away just because somebody opened another panel. | partial | yes | The engine never silently replaces: a new request queues (engine.ts:1140), cancellation is explicit by id (:1228) or by AbortSignal (:1121-1139), and a job that already finished has resolved to its caller. A waiting job is removed without the engine being told anything (engine.test.ts:627, passing). The sweep keeps every position it finished and says so when stopped (AnalysisStudio.tsx:237-240, 'What was searched is kept; the rest is not analysed'). | Two parts are missing. There is no 'retain as a background job' option at all — the only outcomes are queue or cancel, and the deprioritise path cannot be reached because the IPC sends everything as interactive (ipc.ts:306). And completed results are discarded by exactly the action the section names: the analysis session holding every recorded evaluation is component state only (AnalysisStudio.tsx:62) and is never persisted, so switching area in the Chess World rail unmounts the studio and loses a finished whole-game sweep. No written policy exists in docs/chess-world.md either. |
| 330 | Establish search-resource acceptance tests | Tests must cover time limits, node limits, depth settings, multi-line searches, cancellation, request replacement, stale results and several modules asking at once, must measure responsiveness while a long job runs, and must confirm that stopping one search does not stop a different engine instance's work. | partial | — | engine.test.ts (39/39 passing) covers most of the list: :267 limits read defensively and clamped, :798 'a depth search with no time limit of its own is still bounded', :552 several lines only within the engine's own MultiPV limit, :577 an engine with no MultiPV is never asked, :606/:627/:711 cancellation three ways, :651 stale results, :825 queue priority and no overlapping conversations, :855 duplicate ids refused. | Three items on the section's own list have no test. Nothing asserts that a node-limited request really sends `go nodes N` (goCommand in src/shared/chess/uci.ts:200 builds it; cleanLimits clamps `nodes`, but no test drives it end to end). Nothing exercises `stop(requestId)` with an id that is not the running search — engine.ts:1237 returns without cancelling, and that guard is untested, which is precisely the 'stopping one search must not stop another' case. And there is no responsiveness measurement during a long job, and no test with two engine instances. |

### Engine Options, Hash Memory And Hardware Adaptation — 331 to 340

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 331 | Configure supported engine options dynamically | Read the options the engine advertises and build the configuration interface from them where practical, validating types, ranges, defaults and special values, hiding unsafe ones, and keeping the engine's own defaults distinct from the defaults Orbis chooses. | partial | no | Every advertised option is parsed with its type, min, max, default and combo values (src/shared/chess/uci.ts:45) and kept (engine.ts:406-410, exposed by `advertised()` :1278 and `status().options` :1309). Validation is per type and refuses rather than guessing: engine.ts:192 `clampOption` returns null for an unknown combo value or a button given a value, and a name the engine never advertised is never sent (:611-621). Engine-reported defaults stay on the option object; Orbis's own choices are a separate map (`defaultOptions` :1045, `applied` :1310). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:248 'an option value is kept inside what the engine said it accepts, or not sent at all', :321 'an option the engine never mentioned is not sent, and one it limits is clamped to its limit', :277 the handshake reads the option list. | No interface is generated from any of it. There is no IPC channel to set an engine option (src/preload/index.ts:801-804 has only status, install, analyse and stop; src/main/chess/ipc.ts never calls setOption), and no screen renders the advertised list — the settings screen's engine group is five fixed fields (src/shared/chess/settings.ts:340). So the advertised options, their ranges and their defaults are read, validated and then never shown to anybody. |
| 332 | Manage hash-table memory | Let the user set the engine's hash size within a documented memory budget, do not grab a large table merely because the machine has memory, and report the configuration that actually took effect when the requested size is unavailable. | partial | no | src/main/chess/engine.ts:180 `defaultHash` takes a thirty-second of physical memory, clamped to 16–512 MB and rounded to a power of two, with the reasoning written down (:179) — so a 64 GB machine still gets 512 MB. It is sent only if the engine advertises Hash (:1053) and only within the engine's own min/max (:192), and what was really sent is recorded and reported (`applied` :1310). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:231 'threads and memory are sized for a laptop, and never take every core' (defaultHash(16384)=512, (1024)=32, (256)=16) and :298-306 which asserts `applied.Hash` is '512' after the real handshake. | The user cannot configure it. `engine.hashMb` is stored and read by nothing: src/shared/chess/settings.ts:285 states there is no channel to change it, the field is declared `engine-sized` (:513) and src/shared/chess/settings.test.ts:73 ('a field that goes nowhere yet says so', passing) asserts it draws a statement rather than a control — src/renderer/src/components/chess/settings/ChessSettings.tsx:320 prints 'Nothing reads this yet, so there is no control for it.' The effective value (`applied.Hash`) is never shown on a screen either, because the window's own status type drops it (useEngineAnalysis.tsx:27). |
| 333 | Configure thread count safely | Let the user choose the engine's thread count, with a sensible default that keeps the desktop responsive rather than handing Stockfish every logical processor, and make sure the setting reaches the engine's real configuration. | partial | no | src/main/chess/engine.ts:174 `defaultThreads` takes about half the logical processors, never all of them and never more than eight, with the reason recorded (:167-173). It is applied only if the engine advertises Threads (:1053) and the value that was sent is reported (`applied.Threads`). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:231 asserts defaultThreads(10)=5, (2)=1, (0)=1, NaN=1 and that for every core count 1..64 it is strictly less than the core count; :305 asserts `applied.Threads` is '5' and :310 that `setoption name Threads value 5` really went to the engine. | There is no deliberate user change. Same hole as hash: `engine.threads` is stored, declared `engine-sized` (settings.ts:508), drawn as a statement (settings.test.ts:73, passing) and read by nothing; there is no setOption channel in the preload, and src/main/index.ts:4341 constructs the engine with only `home`, so the stored preference cannot reach it even indirectly. |
| 334 | Handle CPU capability detection conservatively | If the app picks among engine builds, decide by a reliable compatibility check rather than by an OS name or a broad CPU family, and fall back to a build that is known to work if detection fails. | done | yes | src/main/chess/install.ts:89 `assetPreference` keys only off platform plus `process.arch` and asks for the `-universal` build first, so no instruction-set assumption (AVX2/BMI2 and the like) is ever made; the plain x86-64 build is the second choice. An unsupported platform or architecture returns no asset with a plain reason and points the person at an engine they installed themselves (:116-121), which is validated before use (`checkBinaryPath`, engine.ts:852-864) and reported as a problem rather than silently swapped (:862). Tests passing (install.test.ts 19/19): :95 'the Windows build chosen is the universal one, and never an instruction set nobody checked for', :107 'a platform this installer cannot unpack for is said plainly rather than guessed at', :125 'a release with no build Orbis knows is reported with what it does have'; engine.test.ts:940 'an engine somebody chose is used, and a bad choice is reported rather than swapped for another'. | — |
| 335 | Support an engine resource profile | Offer resource profiles such as Balanced, Performance and Battery-Friendly, each defining measurable settings like thread allocation, search concurrency or background-job priority, without claiming to control power use that cannot be measured. | not started | no | No profile concept exists anywhere in the chess tree: a case-insensitive search for profile/Balanced/Performance/Battery across src/shared/chess and src/main/chess returns only the coach's weakness profile and unrelated hits. The engine has one fixed sizing path (engine.ts:1045 `defaultOptions`), one concurrency (one search at a time) and no priority setting. | Nothing in the tree answers this. There are no profiles, no profile selector, and no way to change thread allocation or search concurrency at all — which is the other half of the problem, since a profile would have nothing to set given sections 332 and 333 have no channel. |
| 336 | Prevent resource contention with other ORBIS modules | Fit engine scheduling into whatever background-work conventions Orbis already has so that Stockfish, indexing, media playback and other jobs do not compete blindly, and if there is no global resource manager, add the smallest Chess World-specific mechanism instead of redesigning other modules. | partial | — | The smallest chess-specific mechanism is there: one engine process for the whole app (engine.ts:1323 `sharedChessEngine`, src/main/index.ts:4341 one instance), one search at a time through a single queue (:1153), a thread cap that deliberately leaves the machine usable (:174, with the reasoning at :167-173), and ownership by request id so one screen cannot stop another's search (ArenaGame.tsx:432-444). It is shut down on quit (src/main/index.ts:5665). Tested and passing: engine.test.ts:231 and :825. | There is no coordination with anything outside chess. No global resource manager exists for Orbis (nothing in src/main reads load or memory for scheduling except the unrelated src/main/pulsar/runtime.ts:61), and the chess engine is not aware of indexing, media or browser work. Inside chess the one mechanism that would prevent self-starvation — the batch tier — is unreachable, because ipc.ts:306 marks every request interactive, so a whole-game sweep and an Arena move the clock is running on queue equally. |
| 337 | Handle memory pressure | Watch for failures that suggest resources have run out and, where practical, cut background work or offer a lower-resource profile; never quietly shrink a running search's settings, and if the engine has to restart with different options, keep the session and record the interruption. | partial | no | Failures are captured honestly rather than guessed at: stderr is drained and kept (engine.ts:353-361), the exit code or signal becomes the detail (:366-370), and a dead engine fails the running search with what it actually said while staying restartable (:544-564, status `lastError`/`recoverable` :1313). Settings are never changed under a running search — engine.ts:1268 refuses an option change while searching. The interruption reaches the person (EnginePanel.tsx:154, ArenaGame.tsx:364-381). Tests passing: engine.test.ts:730 'an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again', :913 'an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search'. | Nothing monitors resources. The chess code never reads free memory or load (only `totalmem()` once at construction, engine.ts:828), so there is no detection of pressure, no reduction of background workload, and no lower-resource profile to offer because section 335 is missing. A restart also starts the engine with the same options rather than lighter ones, and the restart is not recorded in the session as an interruption — the sweep simply reports the searches it managed. |
| 338 | Implement bounded background analysis | Whole-game and other batch analysis must use bounded queues and explicit concurrency limits rather than one task per position, with incremental processing, checkpoints, cancellation and backpressure, and must show the queue and allow cancelling or reordering when there is more work than can be done. | partial | yes | The work is bounded before anything runs: src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1044 `planQuickPass` turns a millisecond budget into a finite step list, lowers the time per position before skipping anything, lists what it gave up and refuses to pretend to a plan it cannot afford; :1138 `planDeeper` picks only the candidates the reserved budget covers and names what it could not deepen. The runner is strictly serial with one awaited search at a time and a cancel flag checked between steps (AnalysisStudio.tsx:229-254), results already obtained are kept on cancel (:237), and the panel shows 'N of M positions searched' with a Stop button (ReportPanel.tsx:40-54). Planning tests passing (analysis.test.ts 36/36): :483, :499, :507 'a budget that cannot cover the game keeps a run to the end, and lists what it dropped', :519, :526, :545, :564 coverage compares what was asked with what came back. | The runner itself is untested — there is no renderer test outside the three/ folder, so nothing exercises AnalysisStudio.runSweep, its cancel flag or its keep-what-was-done behaviour. There is no pause or resume and no checkpointing: cancelling and starting again re-plans from scratch, and because the session is never persisted (AnalysisStudio.tsx:62) the finished positions are lost when the area changes. No reordering. |
| 339 | Expose actual engine resource status | Show the real search status — elapsed time, nodes searched, effective thread configuration and whatever else is genuinely measured — and never invent CPU use, memory use or a completion estimate; an unavailable measurement is omitted or labelled an estimate. | partial | yes | The data layer is honest and complete: every update carries depth, seldepth, nodes, nps and timeMs taken from the engine's own `info` line and null when it did not report them (engine.ts:126-145, :455-469), a bound is marked and never passed off as a settled score (:471), and `status()` reports state, the search in progress with its start stamp, the queue length, the applied option values, the binary path and the installed version, distinguishing the engine's claim about itself from the release tag that was downloaded (:1282-1316). Nothing invents CPU percentage, memory use or an ETA anywhere. Tests passing: engine.test.ts:277, :513 'a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score', :536, and the info-parsing cases at :379-470. | Almost none of it is displayed. EnginePanel.tsx:169-180 shows only the score, the depth and the dropped-answer count; nodes, nps and elapsed time are carried into the window and never drawn, and the effective thread and hash configuration and the queue length never arrive at all, because the window's own status type is just state/detail/name/version (useEngineAnalysis.tsx:27-32) and discards `applied`, `searching` and `queued`. |
| 340 | Test resource behaviour across representative hardware | Test on representative supported machines including lower-resource devices and different CPU architectures, measure startup reliability, analysis responsiveness, memory use, cancellation latency and coexistence with normal Orbis activity, and set the defaults from those measurements without promising equal performance everywhere. | not started | — | The only hardware statement in the tree is a comment: src/main/chess/engine.ts:170 says the sizing was written for an i7-1355U laptop. engine.test.ts:231 asserts the shape of the sizing functions against synthetic core and memory counts (cores: 10, memoryMb: 16384 in the harness at :202), which is arithmetic, not measurement. docs/chess-world.md records no figures, and there is no bench script, no recorded test configuration and no CI (no .github/workflows). | Nothing in the tree answers this: no measurements of startup reliability, analysis responsiveness, memory use or cancellation latency on any machine, no lower-resource or different-architecture run, and so no evidence behind the defaults — they are reasoned from one laptop rather than measured. The arm64 asset preference (install.ts:91) has never been run on an arm64 machine as far as the tree shows. |

### Chess Rules Engine And Position Integrity — 341 to 350

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 341 | Establish one authoritative chess-state implementation | Use a single tested rules implementation as the authority for legal moves, position transitions, game endings, notation and position serialization, checking the repository for an existing dependency first, and never keep separate legality code for the 2D board, the 3D board, training and the engine service. | done | — | src/shared/chess/rules.ts is the only file in the whole repository that imports chess.js (verified by grep; package.json:102 pins chess.js ^1.4.0, BSD-2-Clause, recorded in docs/chess-world.md:38 and :187 as an ordinary dependency). Everything asks it: the 2D board (Board.tsx:2), the 3D board (three/selection.ts and Board3DArea.tsx:6), the engine service, which replays every engine line and the bestmove through it (engine.ts:449, :501), the store (store.ts:4), training, openings, endgames, puzzles and the live adapter. src/shared/chess/variants.ts holds no legality code — it refuses games that are not standard chess (variants.test.ts:47 'standard chess is the only thing Orbis can play, analyse, read or write'). Tests passing: rules.test.ts 15/15; three/selection.test.ts:60 'every square the board offers is a square the rules offered, and no others' and :271 'no illegal move can get through the board, from any square to any square' (25/25); engine.test.ts:451 'a move the engine offers that cannot be played is refused, and said so'. | — |
| 342 | Separate rendered board state from logical position state | Treat the drawn board as a view only: keep piece placement and every required chess-state field in a logical model that can generate and apply moves, reverse or reconstruct them and convert to supported formats, so the rendering can be rebuilt from state even while an animation shows a transition. | done | yes | Board.tsx takes a FEN and derives everything it draws from it — pieces from `boardSquares(fen)` (:53), legal targets from `destinationsFrom` (:56), the checked king from `outcome(fen)` (:58) — and its own state is only selection, cursor and a pending promotion, all cleared when the position changes (:67). The logical model is src/shared/chess/model.ts: StoredGame holds startFen plus moves each carrying san, uci and the position after (:67-85), `fenAt` (:191) reconstructs any ply, `movePairs` (:198) builds the move list, and the variation tree (:225-282) with `undo`/`removeLine` in analysis.ts reverses edits; writePgn converts out. Tests passing: rules.test.ts:164 'the board can be read and a position can be built by hand'; analysis.test.ts navigation, promotion and undo cases (:61, :115, :176); three/scene.test.ts builds the 3D scene from a FEN (25/25 selection, scene suites pass). | — |
| 343 | Validate all user-entered moves | Check every move for legality in the current position before applying it, and when it is illegal change nothing — not the position, the history, the clocks, the annotations or the saved game — while telling the person in a way that suits how they entered it. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/rules.ts:187 `applyMove` works on a loaded copy and returns null without touching anything when the move is illegal. The boards only offer legal destinations and ignore anything else (Board.tsx:83-90; three/selection.ts:60-78). The Arena refuses a finished game, a move out of turn and an illegal move with a sentence each and changes nothing (arena.ts:569-582), and the screen shows that sentence as an error notice (ArenaGame.tsx:364-367, :506). Persistence re-validates rather than trusting the window: store.ts:31 `normalizeGame` replays every move through `buildMoves` (:38-51) and it runs on write as well as read (jsonFile.ts:267 `put` normalizes), so an illegal game cannot be stored even by a bad payload on `chess:save-moves`. Tests passing: rules.test.ts:16 'a legal move is played and an illegal one changes nothing'; three/selection.test.ts:271 'no illegal move can get through the board, from any square to any square' and :241 'no legal move in any opening position is ever refused by the board'. | — |
| 344 | Implement promotion correctly | Support promotion to queen, rook, bishop and knight, ask explicitly which piece rather than defaulting to a queen everywhere, and make sure notation, captures, the engine's position, the move history and export all reflect the piece chosen. | done | yes | `needsPromotion` (rules.ts:210) asks the rules whether a choice is owed; the 2D board holds the move and opens a four-piece chooser with a Cancel (Board.tsx:50, :84, :166-177) and the 3D board does the same through a reducer that blocks everything else until the question is answered and refuses king or pawn (three/selection.ts:36, :66, :91-98). The chosen piece flows through one move object — promotion, san ('a8=N'), uci ('a7a8n'), captured and the position after (rules.ts:21-41) — which is what the store, the engine request and writePgn all use. No auto-queen default exists anywhere in the tree (grep); puzzles even detect genuine underpromotion (puzzles.ts:521-526). Tests passing: rules.test.ts:28 'coordinates and notation both work, and a promotion keeps the piece asked for'; three/selection.test.ts:81 'a pawn reaching the last rank asks what it becomes before anything is played', :100 'answering the question plays the move with the piece that was chosen', :113 'a pawn cannot become a king or another pawn', :123 'cancelling the question leaves the position alone', :73 'a square is offered once, even when a pawn could become four different pieces there'. | — |
| 345 | Validate castling rights and moves | Castling must respect king and rook movement, occupied and attacked transit squares, check and the castling rights themselves — availability must never be inferred from where the king and rooks happen to stand — and it must be tested for both sides, both colours and the different reasons it can be forbidden. | done | yes | Rights come from the FEN field through the one rules module (rules.ts:122 `loadFen`, :187 `applyMove`), so movement history is what decides them; the position editor sets them explicitly as flags rather than inferring them (variants.ts:548 `withCastling`, rules.ts:311 `buildFen` keeps only KQkq), and checkFen validates the field (rules.ts:66). Tests passing: rules.test.ts:38 'castling follows the rights in the position, not the look of it' — the same pieces on the same squares with the rights removed is refused, and castling through an attacked square is refused; src/shared/chess/calculation.test.ts:116, :126, :132 refuse castling for three distinct reasons (no rights, king in check, crossing an attacked square) (17/17 pass); both colours are exercised through full-game replays — blindfold.test.ts:151 and :186 ('O-O' for White and Black in one game) and famous.test.ts:72 'every game replays from the starting position and every single move is legal' over data containing 18 castling moves. | — |
| 346 | Implement en-passant state correctly | Keep the state needed to decide whether an en-passant capture is available and update or clear it correctly after each move, with position serialization, move generation, repetition handling and the engine's input all agreeing about it. | done | yes | The en-passant square lives in the FEN and is maintained by the one rules implementation; it is part of a position's identity (`positionKey`, rules.ts:288-292, which keeps placement, turn, castling and the en-passant square and drops only the clocks), it is validated when a position is read (checkFen's 'en-passant' problem, rules.ts:66) and written when one is built by hand (buildFen's `/^[a-h][36]$/` guard, :338; variants.ts:556 `withEnPassant`), and the engine is handed the whole FEN including it (`position fen …`, engine.ts:691) so its input cannot disagree. Repetition is settled by replaying the history through the same library (rules.ts:224-241), not by comparing boards. Tests passing: rules.test.ts:52 'en passant depends on the square in the position' — the same board with the square removed refuses the capture, and the legal one reports `special: 'e'` with the pawn captured; :107 includes a malformed en-passant field; :147 'a position is named by what decides its legal moves'. | — |
| 347 | Handle check and checkmate accurately | Determine check, checkmate and the legal replies from the authoritative rules, and make any highlight or checkmate message follow that game state rather than a visual guess — correct after every accepted move and when a saved position is loaded. | done | yes | rules.ts:224 `outcome` asks the library and returns either `{over:false, check}` or an ended game with its reason and result; the move object itself carries `check` and `checkmate` for the position it created (:38-39). The 2D board's check mark is derived from `outcome(fen)` and finds the king of the side to move rather than guessing (Board.tsx:58-64), and the 3D board does the same. The Arena turns it into the game's termination text (arena.ts:507-521). Loading a saved game replays its moves (store.ts:38-51) so the state is recomputed, not trusted. Tests passing: rules.test.ts:63 'checkmate, stalemate and insufficient material are reported with a result' (Fool's mate, zero legal moves, result 0-1) and :86 'a position in check is reported as such without ending the game'; three/selection.test.ts:186 'the king in check is the king the rules say is in check' and :195 'mate marks the mated king too, because the game being over does not un-check it'. | — |
| 348 | Handle stalemate and draw conditions | Support the draw conditions the chosen rules library implements — stalemate, insufficient material and the move-count and repetition rules — distinguish automatic draws from ones where a claim may be available, and never call a merely equal-looking position a draw. | partial | yes | rules.ts:224 `outcome` reports stalemate, insufficient material, threefold and fifty-move each with its own reason and the 1/2-1/2 result, and the history-dependent ones are decided by replaying the move list rather than from the board alone (:227-241). Nothing anywhere treats an equal evaluation as a draw; the Arena turns each reason into its own plain termination text (arena.ts:511-521) and the Endgame Lab reads the same reasons (endgames.ts:516-526). Tests passing: rules.test.ts:63 (stalemate from a real position, insufficient material with a result) and :92 'repetition is decided by the history, not by the board alone' — threefold after a knight shuffle, and the same position with no history behind it is not over. | The automatic-versus-claimable distinction does not exist. `Outcome` (rules.ts:214-216) has no claimable flag, and threefold and fifty-move — which under the applicable rules are claims, not automatic endings — are applied automatically: arena.ts:508-521 ends the game the moment `outcome` reports either, with no claim offered and no way to play on. A grep for claimable or 'claim a draw' across the chess tree and docs/chess-world.md finds nothing (the Arena's `drawOffer` is an offer between players, a different thing). |
| 349 | Maintain move history and position identity | Keep a dependable move history for every game and study, and identify positions by a mechanism that accounts for the chess-state fields rather than by piece placement or move number, since different histories reach the same position and similar-looking positions can differ. | done | — | History: StoredGame keeps startFen plus an ordered move list carrying san, uci and the resulting position (model.ts:67-85) with a fingerprint over start and moves (:118), the Arena keeps a parallel san history precisely because the rules need it (arena.ts:388), and a study keeps a variation tree with the played line recorded as `original` so exploring cannot rewrite the game (analysis.ts:84-122). Identity: `positionKey` (rules.ts:288) is placement + side + castling + en passant, with the comment explaining why the clocks are excluded, and it is the key everywhere it matters — evaluation caching and the problem report (analysis.ts:444, :595, :761, :1217), accepting engine answers (:1336), opening lookup (openings.ts:16-29), endgame memoisation (endgames.ts:299, :333), blindfold comparison (blindfold.ts:585). Repetition is settled from the history, never from a key. Tests passing: rules.test.ts:147 'a position is named by what decides its legal moves' and :92; analysis.test.ts:234 'an evaluation is only recorded against the position it was calculated in' and :441 'an evaluation of another position is not used, and a deeper one replaces a shallower one'. | — |
| 350 | Create a chess-rules regression suite | Build automated tests for opening positions, legal and illegal moves, promotion, castling, en passant, check, checkmate, stalemate, draws, notation parsing and position serialization, using known public test positions, with a regression test for every rules bug found — and no release of a rules change without the suite passing. | partial | — | src/shared/chess/rules.test.ts covers every topic the section lists in 15 tests, all passing (run just now, 1.99s): the opening position's twenty moves, legal and illegal moves, promotion including underpromotion notation, castling, en passant, checkmate/stalemate/insufficient material, check, threefold by history, notation parsing both ways with disambiguation, and FEN serialization both directions including eight distinct malformed-FEN cases. It is registered in the runner (scripts/testFiles.mjs:194) alongside 40-odd other chess suites, and src/shared/testSuite.test.ts checks that list against disk in both directions so a rules test cannot go orphaned. three/selection.test.ts:241 and :271 add exhaustive board-level sweeps. | The acceptance criterion is not enforced: nothing gates a release on the suite. package.json:23 `dist` runs typecheck, staging, notices and build — never `npm test` — and there is no CI (no .github/workflows), so a rules change can be released with the suite failing or unrun. The suite also uses hand-written known positions (Fool's mate and the like) rather than licensed public reference material — there is no perft or EPD suite — and no test is identified as a regression for a specific discovered bug. |

### Live Chess Data: Ingestion Architecture — 351 to 360

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 351 | Separate live-game data from local gameplay | Broadcast games must come in through their own ingestion and normalisation service, never through the controller that runs the user's own game, and live boards, clocks and move lists must be read-only. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts (whole file; imports only rules.ts and model.ts — no arena, engine or store), src/main/chess/live/manager.ts:86-440, src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:376 payloadsFromPgn. Read-only: src/renderer/src/components/chess/live/LiveViewer.tsx:172 passes interactive={false}; SESSION_RIGHTS.watch sets userMoves:false at src/shared/chess/model.ts:95. chess:live-save (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:241-254) writes a NEW stored game, never the live record. Tests: live.test.ts 'a recorded broadcast payload becomes a game, with only what the provider actually sent' and the other 40 (all pass); manager.test.ts 'listing what is on opens no connection at all' + 28 more (all pass). | — |
| 352 | Define the live-data provider interface | There should be one provider interface with explicit operations for discovering games, reading event metadata, subscribing, refreshing a single game record and closing subscriptions, and each adapter should declare what it supports, what it needs to authenticate, how it updates, its documented rate limits and its data-retention rules. | partial | — | src/main/chess/live/manager.ts:86-101 declares LiveProviderPort with name, discover(), games() (which returns the event summary as metadata) and subscribe() returning SubscriptionHandle{stop,done} (manager.ts:75-78) for closing. src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:1062 lichessProviderPort is the one implementation.
