Tests: lichess.test.ts 'the provider port flattens each tournament to the round a person means', 'the provider port lists a round's games without opening anything', 'a listing fault reaches the port as the same diagnostic, not as an empty list' (pass); manager.test.ts 'a provider nobody registered is refused by name' (pass). | Two of the five required operations and the whole capability declaration are missing. There is no operation to refresh one game record (lichess.ts:856 roundPgn exists but is not on the port and is called by nothing — grep finds only its own test). No adapter declares supported capabilities, authentication requirements, update mechanism, rate limits or retention rules in any readable form: a grep for capabilit/retention/requiresAuth across src/main/chess/live and src/shared/chess finds nothing but a rateLimitCooldownMs constant. All of it is prose in the file header (lichess.ts:6-49), so the UI cannot ask what a provider supports and unsupported capabilities are not represented explicitly anywhere. |
| 353 | Implement provider-specific adapters | Each supported source should get its own adapter written against that source's verified interface — Lichess broadcasts, Chess.com's supported interfaces — rather than one generic scraper, and one adapter failing must not take the others down. | partial | yes | src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts is a genuinely source-specific adapter: five endpoints read out of the published OpenAPI document and named in the header (lines 15-24), nothing scrapes, and a changed response shape becomes a schema fault that explicitly refuses to fall back (schemaFault at lichess.ts:722). Per-provider isolation: manager.ts:148 holds providers in a Map and manager.ts:205/224/245 return a per-provider diagnostic when one is absent. Tests: lichess.test.ts 'a round whose shape has changed degrades to a schema diagnostic and never falls back to scraping', 'the recorded NDJSON listing is read into tournaments and their rounds' (pass); manager.test.ts 'a provider nobody registered is refused by name' (pass). | Only one adapter exists. There is no Chess.com adapter and no record anywhere in the tree of its data interfaces having been investigated or ruled out — the only occurrence of the string 'chess.com' in the whole chess tree is as an unregistered provider name in manager.test.ts:216. With one provider registered, 'can fail independently of other providers' is untestable and untested. |
| 354 | Use documented streaming mechanisms where available | Where a provider documents a streaming or server-sent-events interface, use it on the provider's own terms, without inserting a relay server, and give the long-lived connection an explicit state, cancellation, timeout and reconnection behaviour. | done | yes | src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:878 subscribeRound uses the documented GET /api/stream/broadcast/round/{roundId}.pgn (named at lichess.ts:21) directly from the main process via Electron net.fetch (src/main/index.ts:4352) — no relay. State: onState emits connecting/open/reconnecting/stopped/failed (lichess.ts:529, 907, 988, 922). Cancellation: AbortController + stop() (lichess.ts:881-887). Timeout: a deliberately long idle timeout with its own idleFired flag so a cut connection is not reported as a stream that ended (lichess.ts:944-966). Reconnection: bounded, jittered backoff (lichess.ts:931) with a maxReconnects give-up (lichess.ts:916). Tests (all pass): 'the stream yields a payload per game, and uses the documented clocks and comments parameters', 'a game split across chunk boundaries is only emitted once it is whole', 'a dropped stream is retried, with a bounded jittered wait, and then given up on', 'the jitter really varies the wait', 'a stream that is stopped delivers nothing more and does not try to reconnect', 'a silent connection is treated as lost once the idle timeout passes', 'an accepted request with no stream to read is reported'. | — |
| 355 | Use responsible polling as a fallback | For a provider with no suitable stream, fall back to its documented refresh or polling mechanism, pick the interval from the provider's publication rate and limits, back off on failure, avoid multiplying requests when many games are on screen, and never present polling as instant. | partial | yes | The request-discipline half is built and tested. One request lane because the provider documents one at a time (lichess.ts:541, 631), in-flight de-duplication by URL (lichess.ts:621), a 60 s cooldown on a 429 honouring the documented minute (lichess.ts:510, 655-661, 913), jittered exponential backoff (lichess.ts:931), a circuit breaker (lichess.ts:604), and listing reuse under a TTL so several screens asking at once cost one request (manager.ts:205-221). Tests (all pass): 'a 429 is honoured by waiting, and the next request is not even sent', 'requests go one at a time, because the provider documents that they must', 'the same question asked twice at once costs one request', 'a provider that keeps failing is left alone for a while', 'a listing that is still fresh is reused, and a person pressing refresh really refreshes'. | There is no polling fallback at all. No provider-refresh loop exists in the manager or the adapter; lichess.ts:856 roundPgn is the only non-stream route to a game's moves and is called by nothing. The only poll in the product is LiveChess.tsx:84 reading the main process's own memory every 2 s, which is not a provider request. Nothing on screen says update speed depends on the provider and the network; the only timing statement is the declared embargo (liveView.ts:726 delayWords) and describeFreshness's 'Moves are arriving as they are played' (live.ts:652). So if a second provider without a stream were added there is nothing for it to fall back to. |
| 356 | Normalise live-game metadata | Turn each provider's own records into one internal shape holding game id, event id, tournament name, players, ratings, position, moves, result, clocks, source time and retrieval time — every field optional when the provider did not send it, provider identity kept, and nothing invented. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:100 LiveGameRecord carries id, provider, providerGameId, eventId, eventName?, round?, white, black, startFen+moves (position via currentFen at live.ts:251), status, result, clocks?, sourceTimestamp?, receivedAt, lastEventId?. Optionality is enforced by construction (live.ts:301-318 spreads each field only when present; put() at lichess.ts:168 does the same in the adapter). No fabrication: normalisePlayer refuses '?' as a name and 0 as a rating (live.ts:221-229), clocksFrom returns undefined rather than zeros (live.ts:254), asResult defaults to '*' (live.ts:232). Tests (all pass): 'a recorded broadcast payload becomes a game, with only what the provider actually sent', 'what the provider did not send is absent, never filled in', 'normalisePlayer keeps ratings only when they are real ones', 'a later delivery that omits a rating does not erase the one already known'; lichess.test.ts 'the recorded game becomes a snapshot payload with only what its PGN actually said'. | — |
| 357 | Preserve provider event identity | Give every provider event and game a stable local reference built from the provider's own id plus the provider's name, never from player names or event titles, and never silently merge two games when an id changes or goes missing. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:208 liveIdOf(provider, providerGameId) = 'provider:providerGameId', used for every record (live.ts:302) and every map key in the manager (manager.ts:309). The id comes from the provider's own links, not from names: lichess.ts:356 gameIdFromPgnTags reads the last URL segment and validates its shape, and a game with no link is reported and dropped rather than guessed at (lichess.ts:389-399). Merging is blocked by the wrong-game guard (live.ts:330, 346-354) and by the start-position-changed guard (live.ts:367). Tests (all pass): lichess.test.ts 'the game id comes out of the links the broadcast PGN carries', 'a game with no link in it is reported and left out, never guessed at'; live.test.ts 'an update about a different game, or from a different starting position, is refused'; manager.test.ts 'an unknown provider name on a payload still keys the game by that provider'. | — |
| 358 | Validate incoming live-game records | Check structure, move legality, position consistency, timestamps and plausible transitions before anything reaches the screen; a partly unreadable record may still show its event information as long as the screen says which part is missing; and a malformed update must never overwrite a good position without a deliberate recovery decision. | done | yes | Structure and position: live.ts:280 (no provider/game id → refused), live.ts:283-287 checkFen on the start position. Every move is replayed through rules.ts before it is believed (buildMoves at live.ts:288, 446, 534) and a failure returns the ply it failed at. A malformed snapshot leaves the held game untouched (live.ts:448-458 returns state unchanged with problem 'illegal-move'). Timestamps: freshness treats a future stamp as disagreeing clocks (live.ts:639) and clockDisplay refuses to count up (live.ts:711). The screen names the unavailable portion: LiveRoundGames.tsx:75-81 prints how many listed and how many fed games could not be read, LiveChess.tsx:434-438 shows each diagnostic, and liveView.ts:164 unreadableEvents counts dropped listings. Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'a move that cannot be played is refused, and nothing is half-applied', 'an impossible starting position is refused as a position, not as a bad move', 'a snapshot whose moves cannot be played leaves the game exactly as it was'; manager.test.ts 'a payload whose moves cannot be played is reported and no game is put on the board for it'; liveView.test.ts 'a game whose moves cannot be read is refused, not shown as a game with no moves', "a round's games are read, and an unreadable one is counted rather than silently dropped". | — |
| 359 | Maintain a source-freshness model | Keep three separate times — when the provider says it happened, when Orbis received it and when the screen processed it — measure elapsed processing with a monotonic clock, handle missing or inconsistent provider times explicitly, show source delay apart from local delay, and never call a feed live just because a local loop is busy. | partial | yes | Three times are modelled and shown: sourceTimestamp and receivedAt on the record (live.ts:118-120), and lastCheck in the window (LiveChess.tsx:259). freshness prefers the provider's own stamp, falls back to receivedAt and answers 'unknown' when there is neither (live.ts:632-645), with per-provider windows (live.ts:613) and a declared embargo capping the answer at 'delayed'. The screen separates the two delays in words: 'last move arrived … · Orbis last checked …' (LiveViewer.tsx:151) plus the badge at LiveViewer.tsx:155. Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'freshness answers how old what is shown is, with per-provider windows', 'with nothing to measure against, freshness says unknown rather than guessing', "a provider's own timestamp is preferred over when we happened to receive it", 'a timestamp from the future is a disagreeing clock, not data from the future', 'a broadcast on a declared embargo is never called live'. | No monotonic clock anywhere in the live layer: grep for performance.now across src/main/chess/live, src/shared/chess/live.ts, liveView.ts and the live components finds nothing. Every age is measured off wall-clock Date.now (manager.ts:174 default, LiveChess.tsx:143/167), so a system clock stepped backwards moves every freshness answer. No elapsed processing time is measured at all — health() (manager.ts:426) counts batches and payloads, never durations — so the 'difference between source delay and application processing delay' is only shown as two ages side by side, never computed. |
| 360 | Establish live-ingestion acceptance tests | Cover discovery, normalisation, legal and illegal moves, duplicates, delayed, out-of-order and missing events, outages and rate limits with deterministic fixtures that are clearly labelled as recordings, and keep any real-provider testing separate from them so a simulated feed never stands in as proof the provider is up. | done | — | 172 passing tests across four files, all four listed in scripts/testFiles.mjs:220-224: src/shared/chess/live.test.ts (41 pass), src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts (47 pass), src/main/chess/live/manager.test.ts (29 pass), src/shared/chess/liveView.test.ts (55 pass) — each run with node --test. Coverage by concern: discovery ('the recorded NDJSON listing is read into tournaments and their rounds', 'an empty listing is an empty listing, not a fault'), normalisation ('the recorded game becomes a snapshot payload with only what its PGN actually said'), illegal moves ('one unplayable game does not cost the readable games beside it'), duplicates ('a delivery the provider has already sent us is recognised and ignored'), delay ('a broadcast on a declared embargo is never called live'), out-of-order ('a delivery older than one already accepted is rejected on its sequence alone'), outages ('a provider that does not answer is a timeout, said as one', 'a silent connection is treated as lost once the idle timeout passes'), rate limits ('a 429 is honoured by waiting, and the next request is not even sent'). Fixture provenance is a labelled block at lichess.test.ts:19-46 naming each recording's URL, tournament, date and what was trimmed, and saying when a value was altered; no test claims the provider is currently reachable, and the manual confirmation against the running service is recorded separately in docs/chess-world.md:247-253. | — |

### Live-Game Event Ordering And Synchronisation — 361 to 370

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 361 | Define a canonical live-game state model | Hold each live game as one state with a stable source id, normalised moves, current position, clocks, result, the last confirmed source event, freshness and subscription state, keeping raw source information where it helps diagnosis, and never let the drawn board become the real record. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:184 LiveGameState = { record, seen, sequence } over LiveGameRecord (live.ts:100), which carries the stable id, startFen+moves, clocks, result, status and lastEventId ('the provider's id for the last delivery accepted', live.ts:122 — deliberately distinguished from eventId at live.ts:96-98). Freshness and subscription state are attached when the state is read, not duplicated: manager.ts:136 LiveGameView = { record, freshness, clocks } and manager.ts:420 following() gives provider, eventId, generation and connection state; the window reads both back into one value at liveView.ts:336 LiveStateView / liveView.ts:360 readLiveState. The board is derived, never authoritative: liveView.ts:863 viewerPosition computes the drawn FEN from the record and LiveViewer.tsx:172 feeds the Board from it. Tests (all pass): manager.test.ts 'a view says how old the game is and where each clock came from', 'a game nobody is holding has no view, rather than an empty one'; liveView.test.ts 'the live state reads both shapes the channel can send: a view and a bare record', 'the tip is held as "wherever the game has got to", so an arriving move moves the board'. | — |
| 362 | Handle duplicate update events | Recognise a re-sent event by its id, its move sequence or a defined fallback so a repeated move is never appended twice or counted twice, and so a repeated metadata update causes no needless board animation or repeated notification. | done | yes | Three layers. By delivery id: live.ts:356 refuses anything already in state.seen, bounded to 128 ids (live.ts:206, 266). By content: an identical snapshot comes back 'unchanged' with nothing added (live.ts:485-498). In a burst: a newer snapshot supersedes the queued one for that game rather than being applied twice (manager.ts:319). No animation exists to trigger — LiveViewer.tsx:44-46 states nothing on the screen animates — and a correction notice is only raised when the move list really changed (LiveChess.tsx:320-338). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'the same snapshot arriving twice does not double the game', 'a delivery the provider has already sent us is recognised and ignored', 'the remembered delivery ids are bounded, so a seven-hour round cannot grow without limit'; manager.test.ts 'a repeated delivery is recognised by its id and reported as a duplicate', 'several snapshots of one game in a burst collapse to the newest, and the game is not doubled'. | — |
| 363 | Handle out-of-order events | Never apply an older or inconsistent event over a newer confirmed one: use the provider's sequence where there is one, otherwise compare against the validated move history, and when the order cannot be settled either ask for a fresh authoritative snapshot or mark the state as needing reconciliation. | partial | yes | Sequence check: live.ts:360-366 drops any payload whose sequence is below the highest accepted, with the numbers in the note. Without a sequence, the snapshot's line is compared move by move against the held one and a shorter line that agrees as far as it goes is thrown away rather than applied (live.ts:476-483). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'a delivery older than one already accepted is rejected on its sequence alone', 'a snapshot that is behind what we hold is thrown away rather than rewinding the board', 'a delta that cannot be played from the current position is refused, and says a full update will settle it'; manager.test.ts 'an older snapshot arriving late is noted and does not rewind the board'. | The third requirement is absent. When ordering cannot be established — a delta that will not play from the current position (live.ts:534-546) — the payload is refused with a sentence saying 'a full update will settle it', but nothing requests a fresh snapshot and no flag is set: grep for reconcil/needs-reconciliation across the live layer finds no state field, and the provider port has no per-game refresh operation to ask with (see 352). So recovery depends entirely on the provider sending another snapshot of its own accord. |
| 364 | Reconcile missed moves after disconnection | When a subscription resumes, reconcile whatever the provider sends against the last confirmed state — append only the genuinely missing moves or rebuild from a verified snapshot — and treat a changed earlier move as a correction rather than as new play. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:442 reconcileSnapshot is written for exactly this: it replays the snapshot from the held start position, finds the first ply where the two lines disagree, and then does one of four things — extend (adds only the moves past the end of ours), unchanged, discard an older shorter copy, or take the provider's correction from the point of divergence with divergedAt reported (live.ts:460-498). Reconnection follows the same path because the feed re-sends everything (lichess.ts:28-30, 450). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'reconnecting after a dropped connection recovers the moves that were missed, and only those', 'a snapshot that extends what we hold adds only the moves we did not have', 'a provider correcting a move it already published is taken, and reported as a correction'; manager.test.ts 'a game missed while disconnected is recovered from the next snapshot, and only the new moves count'. | — |
| 365 | Distinguish snapshots from incremental events | Model a whole-state snapshot and an incremental change as two different operations, and never append a snapshot's entire move list as though every move had just been played. | done | yes | The kind is a field on the payload (live.ts:143 'snapshot' \| 'update', documented at live.ts:126-131) and ingestLivePayload dispatches on it (live.ts:558-573) to reconcileSnapshot (live.ts:442) or applyLiveUpdate (live.ts:509) — two functions with separate semantics. The adapter labels every game it reads 'snapshot' because that is what Lichess sends (lichess.ts:450). A mislabelled whole-game re-send is caught rather than trusted: applyLiveUpdate replays the 'update' from the start and hands it to reconcileSnapshot if it turns out to restate the game (live.ts:527-532). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'an "update" that is really the whole game again is spotted and does not double the moves', 'a genuine delta is appended to what we hold', 'ingestLivePayload creates on the first delivery and dispatches on kind afterwards'; manager.test.ts 'several deltas of one game in a burst are all applied, in order, and never collapsed'. | — |
| 366 | Implement live-update sequencing | Keep the order of events within each game's stream, validating a provider's sequence number or version before applying an update, and where there is none fall back to timestamps, the move history and state comparison while admitting when the order is ambiguous. | done | yes | Order is held per game, not globally: manager.ts:142-169 keeps a queue per game id, a snapshot replaces that game's queue and an update is appended so no move is lost (manager.ts:319), and the flush applies each game's queue in arrival order (manager.ts:356-375). A provider's sequence is validated before anything is applied and the highest accepted is carried forward (live.ts:360, 409). With no sequence, ordering comes from replaying against the validated history (live.ts:460-483, 527-532), and the ambiguous cases carry a plain note rather than a silent decision (live.ts:481, 543). Tests (all pass): manager.test.ts 'several deltas of one game in a burst are all applied, in order, and never collapsed', 'a round of sixty games arriving at once is one change, not sixty'; live.test.ts 'a delivery older than one already accepted is rejected on its sequence alone', 'a snapshot that is behind what we hold is thrown away rather than rewinding the board'. | — |
| 367 | Prevent stale subscriptions from overwriting newer state | Give every subscription a generation token when the user switches game or refreshes, so a late callback from a retired subscription can never change the current view, though it may still be logged. | done | yes | manager.ts:166 holds a generation counter that only rises; follow() retires the previous one and takes a new token (manager.ts:251-266); current(token) (manager.ts:288) gates payloads, diagnostics and connection states alike; unfollow() raises the generation before stopping the handle so a chunk delivered synchronously from inside stop() is already too late (manager.ts:275-285). Late batches are counted, not silently dropped (manager.ts:301, health().lateDropped at manager.ts:426) and the count is shown on screen (LiveChess.tsx:439-444). The window mirrors the rule with its own token (LiveChess.tsx:156, 195-197, 250-257). Tests (all pass): manager.test.ts 'a late delivery from a round nobody is watching any more cannot touch the board', 'a retired subscription cannot report faults or connection states either', 'what decides whether a delivery counts is the subscription it came from, not the id written on it', 'switching rounds stops the old subscription before the new one starts', 'a burst still waiting when the round is switched is thrown away, not applied to the new round'. | — |
| 368 | Implement backpressure for high-frequency updates | When updates arrive faster than the screen can draw them, do not build an unbounded queue of redundant redraws: keep every move needed to rebuild the game, collapse replaceable metadata updates, defer inessential drawing, and stay responsive across many games or a post-reconnect burst. | done | yes | A burst is gathered for coalesceMs (default 250) and reported as one change (manager.ts:133, 324-333, 345-390). Collapsing is type-aware: a snapshot supersedes the queued snapshot for that game because it is the whole truth about it, while updates are appended because collapsing them would throw moves away (manager.ts:316-321). Growth is bounded in four places: maxGames 400 per event with a diagnostic when exceeded (manager.ts:310-315), the pending map discarded on a switch (manager.ts:336), 128 remembered delivery ids (live.ts:206), and a 4 MB cap on the un-framed PGN buffer that stops the stream rather than letting it grow (lichess.ts:1021). Drawing is deferred by rate: 1 s redraws while watching, 15 s otherwise (LiveChess.tsx:165-169), one poll at a time (LiveChess.tsx:160, 246). Tests (all pass): manager.test.ts 'a round of sixty games arriving at once is one change, not sixty', 'several snapshots of one game in a burst collapse to the newest, and the game is not doubled', 'several deltas of one game in a burst are all applied, in order, and never collapsed', 'with no coalescing window, each delivery is reported as it arrives', 'a round claiming more games than Orbis will follow is capped, and says so'. | — |
| 369 | Maintain source correction history | When a provider revises a published game, keep enough of the change to understand it, show a correction as something other than a normal move, apply it only after validation, and mark any analysis that rested on the old position as out of date. | partial | yes | A correction is its own outcome, distinct from a move: live.ts:176 'corrected', set when the lines diverge (live.ts:469-474, with divergedAt and a note naming the move number) and when a published result is overruled (live.ts:422 resultCorrected, 490-494). It is applied only after the whole snapshot has been replayed through the rules (live.ts:446). The manager forwards the note (manager.ts:373) and the window raises it as a warning on the watched game (liveView.ts:897 compareLines, :926 correctionWords, LiveChess.tsx:320-338, LiveViewer.tsx:161). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'a provider correcting a move it already published is taken, and reported as a correction', 'a result being corrected is a correction; a game merely finishing is not'; manager.test.ts 'a result being overruled comes through as a correction, with a note saying so'; liveView.test.ts 'a published move that has been changed is a correction, and it says which move', 'a line that got shorter without disagreeing is a shorter line, not a correction'. | No history is kept. The superseded line is overwritten in place (live.ts:470 passes the provider's moves straight into the new record) and nothing anywhere stores what the moves used to be, when the change arrived or how many times a game has been revised; the window holds one sentence per game and replaces it on the next correction (LiveChess.tsx:335). The manager's own account of a correction never reaches the screen either — LiveChange.notes and .outcomes (manager.ts:106-116) are consumed by nothing: the window uses the push channel only as a trigger to re-poll (LiveChess.tsx:290) and recomputes the correction itself. And nothing marks dependent results outdated: there is no invalidation hook, so a kept copy of a corrected game (ipc.ts:241) silently keeps the old line. |
| 370 | Test live-state synchronisation comprehensively | Test normal progression, duplicates, missing and out-of-order events, snapshot reconciliation, source corrections, switching games and delayed callbacks — and verify that the board, the move list, the side-to-move indicator, the clocks and the freshness label all refer to the same canonical state. | partial | — | Every delivery-failure case in the first paragraph is covered and passing. Progression: 'a snapshot that extends what we hold adds only the moves we did not have'. Duplicates: 'a delivery the provider has already sent us is recognised and ignored'. Missing: 'a game missed while disconnected is recovered from the next snapshot, and only the new moves count'. Out-of-order: 'an older snapshot arriving late is noted and does not rewind the board'. Snapshot reconciliation: 'reconnecting after a dropped connection recovers the moves that were missed, and only those'. Corrections: 'a result being overruled comes through as a correction, with a note saying so'. Game switching: 'switching rounds stops the old subscription before the new one starts'. Delayed callbacks: 'a late delivery from a round nobody is watching any more cannot touch the board'. The individual view pieces are tested against a record in liveView.test.ts: 'a board walked back is historical, and says how far behind it is', 'only the side to move is counted down, and the count is marked as a count', "freshness is the ingestion layer's own judgement, asked against now". | The second paragraph is not tested. No test ties the five surfaces to one canonical state: they are only checked one at a time in liveView.test.ts, and two of the five have no test at all — the side-to-move indicator is built inline in LiveViewer.tsx:222 from position.fen, and the live move list goes through movePairs, which is exercised only against a famous game (famous.test.ts:251), never against a live record. There is no render test for any live component: find across src/renderer/src/components/chess for *.test.* returns only the eight files under three/. |

### Live Clocks And Game Status — 371 to 380

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 371 | Model clock data explicitly | Treat a clock as source-provided data with a timestamp and a written-down interpretation rather than as a display string, store the time control and increment wherever the provider gives them, and never deduce a time control from how fast a clock appears to fall. | partial | yes | Clocks are modelled as numbers with the moment they were read: live.ts:86 LiveClocks { white?, black?, at }, with the reason the stamp is not decoration written at live.ts:82-85. The interpretation is documented where it is inferred — lichess.ts:186-201 readClockSeconds records that the schema says only 'clock: integer', that it is centiseconds in practice, how that was established, and that a value over a day means the unit was guessed wrong; the documented PGN [%clk] form is preferred (lichess.ts:193, parsed at pgn.ts:144). Nothing infers a control from the rate of fall: no such code exists. Tests (all pass): lichess.test.ts 'the provider clock is centiseconds, and an implausible one is refused rather than shown'; live.test.ts 'a clock reading replaces the previous one wholesale, so two sides are never read from different moments'. | The time control and increment are never stored. The PGN TimeControl tag is parsed into meta.timeControl (pgn.ts:114) and then dropped — payloadsFromPgn (lichess.ts:440-455) does not carry it, and LivePayload (live.ts:132) and LiveGameRecord (live.ts:100) have no field for it. The tournament's own time-control text is read into BroadcastTournament.info.timeControl (lichess.ts:238) and then dropped too: summarise (lichess.ts:1063) leaves info out, so it reaches neither LiveEventSummary (manager.ts:45) nor LiveEventView (liveView.ts:102) nor the screen. The recorded fixture carries [TimeControl "90+30"] (lichess.test.ts:59) and no test asserts it survives, because it does not. |
| 372 | Separate received clock values from displayed countdowns | A timestamped clock may be counted down locally for display when the source's clock semantics are known, but the counted value must be marked as a local estimate and reconciled when a new authoritative reading arrives, and never presented as the tournament's own reading. | done | yes | The distinction is in the type: live.ts:665 ClockOrigin 'received' \| 'extrapolated', carried on every reading (live.ts:667) and flagged on the pair (anyExtrapolated, live.ts:679). clockDisplay (live.ts:699) counts down only the side to move, only from a reading that exists, and refuses four cases — no clocks, finished game, a reading in the future, no reading for the side to move (live.ts:701-717). Reconciliation: a new reading replaces the old wholesale rather than being merged, so two sides are never read from different moments (live.ts:395-397). On screen the counted value says so in words and in a title: liveView.ts:801 clockFace sets note 'Counted down by this computer from the last reading that arrived. Not a value the source sent.', rendered at LiveViewer.tsx:71-79 with a 'counted here' label and explained at LiveViewer.tsx:185-192. Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'only the side to move is counted down, and the count is marked as worked out rather than received', 'asked not to extrapolate, every shown clock is the one that was received', 'clocks disagreeing means nothing is counted down rather than counted up', 'a clock reading replaces the previous one wholesale'; liveView.test.ts 'a clock says whether it was received or worked out here'. | — |
| 373 | Handle missing clock information | When a feed publishes moves without usable clocks, show the clock as unavailable or leave it out, and never set both players' clocks to made-up values just to make the board look complete. | done | yes | clocksFrom returns undefined when neither side has a usable value, so the record simply has no clocks (live.ts:254-264), and clockDisplay returns NOTHING for a record without them (live.ts:682, 701) — the comment at live.ts:690 states the rule: a blank clock is honest, a zero is a claim. One side having a reading leaves the other blank (live.ts:702-707). The window surfaces it as a sentence, not an empty box: liveView.ts:826 clockPair.none and LiveViewer.tsx:183-184 'The source does not send clocks for this game, so none are shown.' A reading that arrives with no moment attached is dropped rather than pinned to now (liveView.ts:304-308). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'no clock was ever sent, so none is shown', 'one side having a clock and the other not leaves the other blank', 'a delivery with no clocks at all leaves the last readings alone rather than erasing them'; liveView.test.ts 'a game the source never sent clocks for shows no clocks at all', 'a clock reading with no moment attached is left out rather than pinned to now'. | partial-free: none |
| 374 | Validate clock transitions | Where the source gives enough information, check that a clock update is structurally sound and belongs to the right player and position; do not reject a record just because a clock went up, since increments and corrections do that; and where the behaviour cannot be reconciled, mark it uncertain instead of showing false precision. | partial | yes | Structural validation: readClockSeconds rejects a negative or implausible value (lichess.ts:195-202) and clocksFrom requires a finite value at or above zero (live.ts:257). Correct player and position: payloadsFromPgn takes each side's clock from the last move that side actually made, and decides which ply belongs to which side from the starting position rather than from the index being even, so a round shown from a Black-to-move position is still read correctly (lichess.ts:417-439); clockDisplay counts down only the side to move in the current position (live.ts:713-714). An increase is never grounds for refusal — no code path rejects on a rise. Tests (all pass): lichess.test.ts 'the recorded game becomes a snapshot payload with only what its PGN actually said' (asserts white 5443 / black 5385, each side's own last reading), 'the provider clock is centiseconds, and an implausible one is refused rather than shown'. | No transition is validated at all: a new reading is never compared with the previous one (live.ts:395-397 replaces the clocks wholesale and discards the old reading), so an impossible jump, a clock belonging to the wrong move, or a rise that no increment could explain is accepted in silence. There is no uncertainty state to mark it with — grep for 'uncertain' across the live layer finds nothing, and ClockReading carries only 'received' or 'extrapolated' (live.ts:665). An implausible value is dropped entirely instead of shown as doubtful, and no test exercises an increased or inconsistent clock. |
| 375 | Model live-game status | Represent the statuses the provider actually supports — scheduled, not started, in progress, completed, abandoned, unavailable, unknown — and never conclude a game has finished just because the feed went quiet, keeping an ended game distinct from a lost connection. | partial | yes | The honesty half is done and tested. statusFrom (live.ts:242-248) derives status from evidence only: a decided result settles it, the provider is believed when it said anything, moves mean the game has started, and nothing known gives 'unknown' rather than the tidier 'scheduled'. Silence never implies an ending — a quiet feed changes freshness (live.ts:632), not status, and the connection is a third, separate value (manager.ts:103 ConnectionState, shown via liveView.ts:610 connectionWords at LiveViewer.tsx:145). The round's status comes from the round's own fields (lichess.ts:283 roundStatus). Tests (all pass): live.test.ts 'statusFrom reports what is known and says unknown when nothing is'; lichess.test.ts 'a round with no startsAt, no ongoing and no finishedAt is unknown, not scheduled'; liveView.test.ts 'every status and result has words, and "not known" is one of them', 'a game nothing is known about the age of says so rather than being called live'. | The vocabulary is four values, not the seven the section lists: live.ts:29 LiveStatus is 'scheduled' \| 'live' \| 'finished' \| 'unknown'. There is no 'abandoned' and no 'unavailable' anywhere in the live layer (grep finds neither), so a provider that reports an abandoned or withdrawn game can only be shown as 'finished' or 'unknown', and a game the provider says it cannot serve is indistinguishable from one nothing is known about. Adding them touches the readers and the words too (liveView.ts:61 STATUSES, :573 statusWords, LiveDashboard.tsx:48 DOT). |
| 376 | Handle result corrections | When a provider changes a result it already published, update the record by a written policy, keep the source result and the time of the update, and mark the record inconsistent if the new result disagrees with the moves that are held. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:422 resultCorrected, used by reconcileSnapshot (live.ts:442, 463-487) and applyLiveUpdate (live.ts:509) to report outcome 'corrected' with a sentence naming the old and new result; withMeta (live.ts:380) refreshes receivedAt and sourceTimestamp; notes reach the window via manager flush (src/main/chess/live/manager.ts:345) and are shown at src/renderer/src/components/chess/live/LiveViewer.tsx:161. Passing: live.test.ts 'a result being corrected is a correction; a game merely finishing is not' and manager.test.ts 'a result being overruled comes through as a correction, with a note saying so' (41/41 and 29/29 pass). | Nothing ever marks a record inconsistent: grep for 'inconsistent' finds no hit in src/shared/chess, src/main/chess or the chess components, and a result that contradicts the held moves (provider says 1-0 over a position the rules call drawn) is applied with no flag. The superseded result survives only in a transient note — no field on LiveGameRecord (live.ts:100-131) keeps it — and LiveViewer.tsx:215-222 only handles the opposite case, where the board is over and the provider has said nothing. |
| 377 | Identify stale games | Decide that a game may be out of date using the source's own timestamps, how often that provider publishes, and the connection, with thresholds chosen per provider, and say the position may no longer be current without calling the game abandoned. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/live.ts:613 FRESHNESS_WINDOWS (lichess 15/60 minutes against a 5/30 default, with the reason), freshness (live.ts:632) preferring sourceTimestamp over receivedAt and capping an embargoed feed, describeFreshness (live.ts:648) which says 'treat this as the last thing known rather than the current position' and never 'abandoned', statusFrom (live.ts:242) which never turns silence into 'finished'; the connection question is answered separately by the manager (manager.ts:420). Passing in live.test.ts: 'freshness answers how old what is shown is, with per-provider windows', 'with nothing to measure against, freshness says unknown rather than guessing', 'a broadcast on a declared embargo is never called live, however promptly it arrives'. | — |
| 378 | Display live status and freshness clearly | Make a live-updating game, a finished game, an out-of-date record and a feed that is not arriving look visibly different, and never animate something to suggest data is arriving when it is not. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/liveView.ts:628 freshnessWord and :658 freshnessBadge (four states, each with its own word and explaining sentence), connectionWords (liveView.ts:610) for the subscription state; rendered at LiveViewer.tsx:155-162 with four distinct styles in live.css:220-241 and LiveViewer.tsx:150-153 separating 'last move arrived' from 'Orbis last checked'. live.css contains no @keyframes, animation or transition at all, so nothing pulses over a quiet feed. Passing in liveView.test.ts: 'freshness is the ingestion layer's own judgement, asked against now', 'a broadcast the provider holds back can never be called live, however promptly it arrives', 'a game nothing is known about the age of says so rather than being called live' (55/55 pass). | — |
| 379 | Synchronise clocks and board updates | Apply a new move and the clock reading that came with it as one change, so the new board is not left showing the previous player's clock, and write down the strategy used when a source sends the two separately. | done | yes | src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:376 payloadsFromPgn takes each side's clock from that side's last move of the same snapshot (lichess.ts:416-437, including the 'black to move first' case), and src/shared/chess/live.ts:380 withMeta replaces the clocks wholesale together with the moves and result in one transition (settled, live.ts:404) rather than merging two moments; clockDisplay (live.ts:699) counts down only the side to move and only from the reading's own 'at'. The strategy is written out in both files' headers. Passing: live.test.ts 'a clock reading replaces the previous one wholesale, so two sides are never read from different moments' and 'a delivery with no clocks at all leaves the last readings alone rather than erasing them'; lichess.test.ts 'the documented eval and clock comments are kept as the source wrote them, not reinterpreted'. | — |
| 380 | Establish clock and status acceptance tests | Test the clock and status display against drifting timestamps, missing clocks, increments, clock corrections, stale feeds, revised results, finished games and dropped connections, using recorded cases, and check the real provider separately. | partial | — | Recorded-case coverage that passes today: src/shared/chess/live.test.ts (41/41) for drift ('a timestamp from the future is a disagreeing clock, not data from the future', 'clocks disagreeing means nothing is counted down rather than counted up'), missing clocks ('no clock was ever sent, so none is shown', 'one side having a clock and the other not leaves the other blank'), clock corrections, stale windows, result revisions and finished games; src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts (47/47) for disconnections and the idle timeout; manager.test.ts (29/29) for dropped and late deliveries. | No test — and no model — for an increment time control through the live clock path: LiveClocks (live.ts:86) holds only white, black and 'at', the live record carries no time control, and 'increment' appears in the live tree only as arithmetic inside the readClockSeconds unit test (lichess.test.ts:346). Nothing in the tree records the separate verification against the running provider that the section asks for; every clock and status case is a recorded fixture. |

The dashboard keeps 'not asked yet', 'asked and nothing is on' and 'asked and it could not answer' apart (LiveDashboard.tsx:113-139). ### Network Recovery, Rate Limiting And Observability — 391 to 400

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 391 | Implement bounded reconnect behaviour | Reconnect to a persistent feed on a written strategy with growing, jittered delays, a limit on attempts and a way to cancel, and never hammer a provider that is down or has refused access. | done | yes | subscribeRound (src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:878-926): at most maxReconnects attempts (DEFAULTS.maxReconnects 10, :504-513), a delay doubling from 2s to a 5-minute ceiling with jitter applied across the whole delay (backoff, :931-934), a wait for the circuit before each attempt (:898-903), a stop() that aborts and leaves the loop (:883-889), and a stated give-up that reports 'failed' with a diagnostic (:918-923). Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'a dropped stream is retried, with a bounded jittered wait, and then given up on', 'the jitter really varies the wait, so many clients do not reconnect in the same instant', 'a stream that is stopped delivers nothing more and does not try to reconnect'. Reconnecting and the give-up both reach the screen (connectionWords at LiveViewer.tsx:146, diagnostics at LiveChess.tsx:434). | — |
| 392 | Handle rate-limit responses | Recognise a provider's rate-limit answer, slow down and honour its guidance instead of retrying as if it were a blip, tell the person, and keep the data already received with an honest freshness mark. | done | yes | send (src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:638-690): a 429 counts a failure on the documented minute's cooldown, sets openUntil so the next request is refused before it is sent, and reports kind 'rate-limit'; a 200 whose body is only {"error":...} is treated the same way (describeError, :1143-1156), which is the shape this provider really answers on its PGN endpoints; the stream path repeats both (readStream :950-975) and keeps at least the documented minute (:896). Data already held is kept — manager.refresh returns the previous listing and still reports the fault (manager.ts:213-220) and the games map is untouched, with the badge still saying how old it is. Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'a 429 is honoured by waiting, and the next request is not even sent', 'a 429 on the stream waits the documented minute before the round is followed again', 'a refusal in the body is recognised even when it arrives under the PGN content type'; manager.test.ts 'when a listing fails, an older one is offered rather than an empty page, and the fault is still reported'. | — |
| 393 | Define network timeout policies | Set explicit timeouts for connecting, answering and going quiet, tell a slow answer apart from a refusal, a closed subscription and a real empty result, and on a timeout keep what is cached while reporting the source as delayed or unavailable. | done | yes | A 20-second limit on every ordinary request through an AbortController (DEFAULTS.timeoutMs, lichess.ts:505; send :646-648, :680-688) and a separate 15-minute idle timeout on the long-lived stream with its own wording and an idleFired flag so a connection that was cut for silence is never reported as a round that ended (readStream :940-1000, :1019-1035). The kinds keep timeout, network, http and rate-limit apart, an empty listing stays a success (:741), and a failed refresh hands back the previous listing (manager.ts:213-220). Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'a provider that does not answer is a timeout, said as one', 'a silent connection is treated as lost once the idle timeout passes', 'an accepted request with no stream to read is reported rather than silently doing nothing', 'an empty listing is an empty listing, not a fault'. | — |
| 394 | Prevent request storms | Fold identical requests together and share discovery work between the screens that want it, schedule updates for many visible games within the provider's limits instead of one loop per component, and cancel what a closed view no longer needs. | done | — | One lane, so one request is in the air at a time and a rejection cannot wedge it (lichess.ts:631-636); de-duplication by URL for the window a request is actually in flight (request, :621-629); a shared 60-second discovery cache so several askers cost one request, with force for a person pressing refresh (manager.ts:205-212); listing never opens a connection and only follow() does (manager.ts:36-48, :245); leaving the round or the screen unfollows (LiveChess.tsx:230-242) and unfollow stops the stream and clears the queue (manager.ts:275-287). Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'the same question asked twice at once costs one request', 'requests go one at a time, because the provider documents that they must'; manager.test.ts: 'a listing that is still fresh is reused, and a person pressing refresh really refreshes', 'listing what is on opens no connection at all'. | — |
| 395 | Define live-data caching policy | Cache provider answers only as the source's terms and the person's preferences allow, keep the transport cache, the normalised live state and saved games as three categories with their own retention and deletion rules, and never let a stale cache pass for current data. | partial | — | The three categories do exist separately: an in-flight map plus a 60-second listing cache (lichess.ts:621-629, manager.ts:157 and :205-212), normalised live state in the manager's games map which is cleared on unfollow unless asked to keep it (manager.ts:275-283), and user-saved records in the profile's own file (store.ts:97-130). Passing: manager.test.ts 'a listing that is still fresh is reused, and a person pressing refresh really refreshes', 'different questions get different listings rather than one answering for the other'. | A stale cache can pass for current data at the listing level: when a refresh fails, manager.ts:213-219 returns the held events as a plain { ok: true } with no staleness marker, and the dashboard renders them exactly like a fresh list (LiveChess.tsx:171-186) while the fault is only rendered inside a round (LiveChess.tsx:434), so an unavailable provider looks like a current tournament list. No retention or deletion rule is stated for any of the three categories, and there is no user preference about caching anywhere in chess settings. |
| 396 | Add structured provider diagnostics | Record the operational facts about each provider call — who, what call, what kind of answer, latency, retries, the last success, the parse failure category — without logging secrets, so an outage, a network fault, a parsing error and an Orbis bug can be told apart locally. | partial | yes | LiveDiagnostic carries provider, operation in words, kind, message, the field that changed shape, status and a time (src/shared/chess/live.ts:41-64), built in one place per adapter (fault, lichess.ts:585-597; schemaFault :722-726) with nine kinds that separate an outage, a network fault, a parse failure and 'schema' meaning Orbis is wrong. No secrets: the token only ever goes in a header (:576-582), tested by 'a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address'. Counters exist (manager.health, manager.ts:426-435; adapter health, lichess.ts:566-574) and diagnostics are pushed to the window (src/main/index.ts:4348-4351) and shown at LiveChess.tsx:434. | Three of the named facts are recorded nowhere: there is no latency (a grep for latency or performance.now across the live files finds nothing), no retry count on any diagnostic, and no last-successful-update timestamp. The adapter's own health — circuit state, consecutive failures, last fault — is exposed by no channel, since chess:live-state returns only the manager's counters (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:230-239). Nothing is persisted, so after the fact there is nothing to investigate: the window keeps the last four diagnostics in memory (LiveChess.tsx:289-293) and only while a round is open. |
| 397 | Implement per-provider circuit breaking | When one provider keeps failing, cut back or stop asking it on a written policy, let it recover after an interval or an explicit retry, keep other sources working, and tell a passing transport failure apart from a permanent configuration or permission problem. | partial | yes | The breaker is state on the adapter instance, so it is per provider by construction: four consecutive failures open it for 60 seconds (DEFAULTS, lichess.ts:504-513; recordFailure :604-607), an open circuit refuses requests with its own 'circuit-open' message and remaining seconds (send :639-646) and makes the stream wait rather than connect (:898-903), and one good answer closes it (recordSuccess :610-613). The manager keeps providers in a map and asks each by name (manager.ts:149, :205-210). Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'a provider that keeps failing is left alone for a while instead of being asked again', 'one good answer closes the circuit again'. | No distinction between transient and permanent failure: a 401, 403 or 404 counts exactly like a timeout (send :657-660, which calls recordFailure for every non-ok status), so a permission or configuration error is retried on the same 60-second cycle for ever, and nothing offers a person an explicit retry — the circuit only reopens with time. With one provider registered, 'other sources remain usable' is true by construction and exercised by no test. |
| 398 | Handle provider schema changes safely | Validate every answer against the expected shape while tolerating extra fields, stop processing a record whose required field has changed rather than misreading it, report a structured parsing error, keep the data already validated, and never silently fall back to scraping. | done | yes | Every response goes through readers that take only the fields they know and ignore the rest (readTour lichess.ts:226, readRoundInfo :262, readGame :290, parse :692-710); a missing required field stops that record and raises kind 'schema' naming the field (schemaFault :722-726); readable siblings survive while unreadable ones become warnings (official :740-767, round :830-848), and a PGN stream that stops breaking into games where the adapter expects ends the stream rather than growing in memory (readStream :1012-1017). There is no scraping path at all, stated at :715-721. Passing in lichess.test.ts: 'a round whose shape has changed degrades to a schema diagnostic and never falls back to scraping', 'a listing where nothing can be read is a schema fault, said as one', 'one unreadable line in a listing is a warning, not a failure', 'an unreadable game is left out of the list and reported, and the round still loads'; liveView.test.ts 'a rate limit, an open circuit and a changed shape are three different things to tell somebody'. | — |
| 399 | Monitor end-to-end update latency | Measure the stages a live update passes through — source time, local receipt, parsing, normalising, applying and reaching the screen — reporting only the parts the timestamps support, allowing for clocks that disagree, and using the figures to find Orbis's own delays. | partial | no | The timestamp hygiene the section demands is there: receivedAt is stamped at the moment a chunk is read and carried on every payload (lichess.ts:1000-1003; live.ts:117), a provider's own sourceTimestamp is preferred over receipt when supplied (freshness, live.ts:632-640), a stamp in the future is treated as two clocks disagreeing rather than negative age (live.ts:637 and clockDisplay :727), and the window separates 'last move arrived' from 'Orbis last checked' so a healthy poll cannot make a dead feed look alive (LiveViewer.tsx:146-153, tested by liveView.test.ts 'how long ago something happened is coarse above a minute, and "never" when it never did'). | Nothing measures any stage. A grep for latency, performance.now, parseMs or elapsedMs across src/shared/chess/live.ts, liveView.ts and src/main/chess/live finds no hit: parsing, normalising, applying to state and reaching the screen are untimed, no latency appears in any diagnostic (live.ts:52-64) or in either health reading (manager.ts:426, lichess.ts:566), and no screen or log reports a latency component. |
| 400 | Establish the fourth milestone acceptance gate | Before going further, run a 32-point gate over the finished engine and live-data work using genuine components — engine discovery through shutdown, the chess rules, real provider ingestion, and the operational checks — and treat mocks, static lists and simulated streams as not passing. | partial | — | Much of the underlying verification exists and passes. Engine items 1-10: src/main/chess/engine.test.ts and install.test.ts ('nothing is ready until the engine has said hello, and then it says what it is', 'an option the engine never mentioned is not sent, and one it limits is clamped to its limit', 'a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', 'restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit', 'installing downloads the verified archive, keeps the licence, and is safe to repeat'). Rules items 11-15: rules.test.ts, analysis.test.ts, variants.test.ts, pgn handling in store.ts/library.test.ts. Live items 16-23 and operational 26-28: live.test.ts 41/41, lichess.test.ts 47/47, manager.test.ts 29/29, liveView.test.ts 55/55, all confirmed passing in this audit. | There is no gate anywhere: docs/chess-world.md has no milestone or acceptance-gate section (grep finds neither word) and no run is recorded. Item 24 is untested because chess:live-save has no test; item 25's provenance is untested because the games store has no test; item 29 (engine responsiveness during background analysis) and item 32 (regression against existing ORBIS and Orion behaviour) have no measurement or suite named for them; item 30 is verified for the five data files only (data.test.ts 'the profile it came from') and not for chess-games.json; item 31 has nothing asserting the absence of online multiplayer — a grep for 'multiplayer' across the chess tree returns nothing. Above all, every engine test drives a scripted process and every live test a recorded response or an injected fetch, which is exactly what this section says does not count: no run against a real Stockfish binary or a real broadcast is recorded in the tree. |

Exercised by passing tests: variants.test.ts 'a game written as “From Position” is ordinary chess and is read' and 'a real Chess960 file is refused by the importer' (44/44 pass); src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts 'the recorded game becomes a snapshot payload with only what its PGN actually said', 'the documented eval and clock comments are kept as the source wrote them' (47/47 pass); repertoire.test.ts 'a repertoire goes out to a file and comes back the same' (22/22 pass). | Variations are discarded, not parsed into a tree: readMoveText skips everything inside parentheses (pgn.ts:172-173, 194) and neither ParsedGame nor StoredGame has a field for one (model.ts:67), so the "optional variation trees" item of the required record does not exist and nested variations cannot survive an import. There is no pgn.test.ts and no .pgn fixture anywhere in the tree — all coverage is incidental, through the Lichess adapter, the variant registry and the repertoire; the limits, truncation, bad-tag and semicolon-comment paths have no test at all. |

| 408 | File Size, Encoding, and Resource Limits | Protect the import path with configurable limits on size, games, moves, variation depth, comment length, nodes and parse time, detect bad encodings, parse large files off the UI thread in bounded batches, and show progress with responsive cancellation. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/pgn.ts:57 PGN_LIMITS (64 MB, 20,000 games, 2,000 moves per game, 20,000 comment characters) with the file, game-count and move-count ceilings each reported as a 'too-large' warning the import sheet shows (:204, :248, :257); the parser is a single forward pass with no rescanning, so recovery is bounded; parsing happens in the main process rather than the renderer (ipc.ts:129); the position index does chunk its work for the UI (PositionSearch.tsx:197 stepIndex 150 games at a time). | The limits are constants, not configurable. No variation-depth, node-count, parse-time or memory ceiling. No streaming and no bounded batches: ipc.ts:129 reads the whole file with readFile(path,'utf8') and the size is only checked afterwards against the decoded string length, so an oversized file is loaded into memory (or throws a raw V8/ENOMEM error) before it can be refused — nothing calls stat first. Encoding is assumed UTF-8: malformed bytes become replacement characters with no detection and nothing said. No progress reporting and no cancellation for an import once it has started. |
| 409 | Import Preview and Transactional Commit | Separate a preview stage — filename, game counts, valid and invalid, duplicates, destination, warnings, and what will happen to matching records — from the permanent commit, which must be transactional, batched for large files, recoverable, and cancellable without touching unrelated records. | partial | no | src/main/chess/store.ts:95 DuplicatePolicy and :162 fingerprint-based duplicate skipping exist, and ImportSheet.tsx:83 reports brought in / already here / could not be read with every reason afterwards; each game is written as one whole normalised record (store.ts:163-175 via Collection.put, which rewrites the file atomically through tmp+rename in storage/jsonFile.ts:180), so a half-written game is not possible. | There is no preview stage at all: the sheet's only actions are "Choose a PGN file" and "Bring in what is pasted", both of which commit immediately, so nothing shows the game count, the duplicate estimate or the warnings before anything is written. No transaction or staged commit — importPgn loops one put per game, with no rollback if it fails partway. No batching, no import-job status, no recovery policy, and no cancellation once reading has begun. DuplicatePolicy 'keep-both' is unreachable: ipc.ts:130 and :139 never pass a policy, so the store always defaults to 'skip'. No test covers store.importPgn at all (there is no store.test.ts). |

### Local Database, Schema, Transactions, And Migrations — 411 to 420

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 411 | Define the Canonical Local Chess Data Model | Design one coherent data model on the storage technology ORBIS already uses, covering every durable chess entity, with derived values kept out of the authoritative records and keys, relationships, uniqueness, deletion and profile ownership documented, so no feature keeps duplicate state in several places. | partial | — | src/main/chess/store.ts:97 ChessStore holds games in one per-profile file; src/main/chess/data.ts:168 ChessDataStore adds five more (settings, training, openings, academy, progress) with the split and the reasoning documented at data.ts:11-56 and the shapes named once in src/shared/chess/kept.ts; identity and uniqueness are real (model.ts:67 StoredGame.id plus :118 fingerprintOf for recognising the same game twice); derived values are deliberately not stored (kept.ts:23-27 — no streak, no sittings, both worked out); profile ownership is enforced through personalFile and useProfile and documented in docs/chess-world.md:123. Tests pass: data.test.ts 25/25, including 'keeps the preferences, the puzzles, the repertoire, the Academy and the plan apart' and 'has no streak and no list of sittings'. | The duplicate state the section forbids is exactly what exists today: the ten channels for the five files (ipc.ts:179-192, preload/index.ts:805-814) are registered and exposed, and nothing in the renderer calls any of them — the screens still keep the same data in window storage (settings/chessSettingsStore.tsx:61, puzzles/store.tsx:30, academy/storage.tsx:36, coach/CoachPanel.tsx:66), which is per-computer, outside the profile and outside any export. Entities from the section's list that have no home at all: analysis sessions, analysis results and evaluation samples, engine configurations beyond the five preference fields, import and export jobs, background-job state, favourite players, and a store-wide schema version (the games file carries none). |
| 412 | Database Indexing and Query Design | Work out the real query patterns first, then add only the indexes that materially help them, use composite indexes for common multi-column filters, keep large payloads out of every row, check the plans when unsure, and start with a sensible local search rather than bolting on full-text infrastructure. | partial | — | src/shared/chess/library.ts is query design done deliberately: :200 SEARCH_FIELDS names every field matched, :219 searchText memoises the folded string per game object and checks updatedAt so a stale index cannot be reused, :362 filterGames (player, event, date window, result, source, favourite, annotated, tags, collections), :493 sortGames with the rank worked out once per game, cursor paging rather than offsets (:505-552), and facet counts over what is really there; src/shared/chess/positionSearch.ts:413 LibraryIndex is a genuine position index (key -> every game and ply, plus a material index built in the same pass) with chunked building and a verifier that replays the games to prove the index agrees. Tests pass: library.test.ts 'a library of several thousand games filters, sorts and pages quickly' with printed timings (40/40); positionSearch.test.ts 'the material index gives the same answer as a full replay' (35/35). | Only games and positions were designed for. The other patterns the section lists have no query design: analysis-cache lookups do not exist, live-game history is not stored, and puzzle history and Academy progress are read whole out of window storage. The library's own channel is a full scan with substring matching (store.ts:201 search over every game, every call) and ChessStore.all() sorts the whole collection on each read. Nothing stores the result of a query plan check because there is no query planner — the cost model is the timing assertion in that one test. |
| 413 | Transaction Boundaries and Atomic Updates | Give multi-record operations explicit transaction boundaries using the store's own mechanism — importing a game with its moves, committing an analysis session with its results, deleting a game with its owned records, applying a migration — rather than hoping a run of unrelated writes all succeed, and handle failures, disk-full and interruption. | partial | — | The storage layer makes each write all-or-nothing: src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:180 writes to <path>.tmp and renames, with every write serialised through one promise chain, and Collection.update (:296) exists to change several items in one write. A game is one record in one file, so it cannot be stored without its metadata (store.ts:128 save recomputes the fingerprint with the moves, and normalizeGame replays the notation on read). data.ts:134 reads before saving and reports refused when nothing was written, with 25/25 tests passing in data.test.ts including 'writes nothing at all over a file it could not read, and the save says so'. | No operation declares a boundary. store.importPgn issues one put per game (store.ts:175) rather than one Collection.update, so an import interrupted partway leaves some games committed with nothing recording that the job was incomplete — it only looks atomic because the 200 ms write delay usually coalesces the writes. Deleting a game cascades to nothing because nothing derived is stored. No disk-full or injected-write-failure test exists for any chess store, so the section's acceptance criterion is unverified. |
| 414 | Database Migration Framework | Implement versioned, deterministically ordered migrations that run only when needed, finish before the schema is used, record the resulting version, report failures, never silently drop data, are tested against realistic older data, and never fall back on deleting the database. | partial | — | The framework exists in the storage layer and is tested: src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:35 JsonFileOptions takes a version and a migrate(raw, from), :89 fromText reads both the bare and the {v,data} shapes, treats an unwrapped file as version 1, refuses anything written by a newer Orbis and records the version on write. jsonFile.test.ts passes 17/17, including 'brings an older wrapped file forward, and says which version it came from', 'reads a file written before versioning existed as version one', and 'writes nothing over a file written by a newer Orbis'. | Chess does not use it. Neither store passes a version or a migrate function (data.ts:101 passes only {what}; store.ts:105 passes none), so all six chess files are unversioned version-1 files with no upgrade path. The two version numbers inside the records (PUZZLE_STORE_VERSION in puzzles.ts:713, PROGRESS_VERSION in academyPath.ts:57) only refuse the future — shapes.ts:389 throws for a newer Academy record — and there is no migration from either. No test upgrades a chess database from an older shape. |

Reachable through TransferSettings.tsx and the portable:* channels (preload/index.ts:102-112). Tests pass: roundTrip.test.ts 'carries a profile out and back into an empty one', 'refuses a package with a byte changed', 'finds an entry removed from the integrity list', 'stops when cancelled, having written nothing', 'will not write a package it cannot read back'. | What a chess backup actually captures today is games only: the other five files are never written, because no screen calls the channels that write them (see 411), so puzzle progress, Academy progress, the repertoire, the study plan and the board preferences sit in window storage and travel with nothing — academy/storage.tsx:22 says so in as many words. There is also no chess-specific validation or report: nothing checks a restored chess file is readable as chess data or tells the person which chess records came back. |
| 416 | Crash Recovery and Database Integrity Checks | Handle interrupted writes, unexpected shutdowns and suspected corruption: cheap checks at launch and deeper ones in a diagnostic path, and on corruption stop writing, preserve the original, recover only when safe, offer a backup, avoid a destructive loop, and never delete a damaged file while claiming the data was kept. | partial | — | src/main/storedFile.ts:41 readStored tells missing from unreadable (only ENOENT is missing), :60 quarantine renames a damaged file to <name>.unreadable-<timestamp> and never deletes it, and jsonFile.ts:167 and :211 refuse to write at all over something that could not be read — on both the ordinary and the quitting path — leaving the question open so a cleared lock gets through next time. Writes are atomic via tmp+rename, so an interrupted write cannot truncate the file. Tests pass: data.test.ts 25/25, including 'moves one it cannot make sense of aside, and the words are still on the disk', 'moves aside a collection written by a newer Orbis instead of dropping what it does not recognise', 'writes nothing at all over a file it could not read, and the save says so', and 'still refuses to write over a file it could not read, even on the way out'. | The check is only "can this be read and parsed on open" — there is no deeper diagnostic or recovery workflow, nothing offers a restore from a backup when damage is found, and nothing records the outcome of a recovery or what could not be recovered. The games file has none of this tested: there is no store.test.ts, so the one file that holds the person's games has no corruption or interrupted-write test of its own. |

| 418 | Efficient Retention and Storage Management | Put retention controls on large derived data — engine lines, evaluations, progress logs, temporary import files, downloaded records, live snapshots, diagnostics, puzzle-generation leftovers — keep user-owned records out of it, distinguish authoritative from regenerable data, show storage use, explain what a cleanup removes, and never run an expensive sweep on every render or launch. | partial | no | The authoritative/regenerable split is deliberate and documented: kept.ts:23-27 stores no streak and no list of sittings because both are worked out from the attempts, drills and games; src/main/chess/shapes.ts:84-114 caps the stored record lists and says out loud when the cap bites ('There are N … and M is as many as are kept, so the earliest N-M were left out'), and store.ts:105 caps the games file at 20,000. reference.ts:48 DataSize reports bytes and entries for the datasets that are on the disk. Nothing expensive runs at launch. data.test.ts 25/25 and reference.test.ts 18/18 pass. | There is no cleanup operation anywhere in Chess World, and no storage figure for the person's own chess data. Almost everything the section names as large derived data is not persisted at all — engine principal variations, evaluations, analysis logs, live snapshots, puzzle-generation intermediates and temporary import files all live only for the function call that made them — so there is neither a need met nor a control built. The one real retention rule, the record caps, only exists in the store the screens do not use yet. |
| 419 | Database Concurrency and Locking | Audit every path that writes — UI, engine workers, live services, import and analysis jobs — and use a documented concurrency model that prevents concurrent migrations, duplicate writes from repeated events, results attached to the wrong game, lost progress, writes from a deleted profile, conflicting edits and long operations blocking unrelated reads. | partial | — | Writes are serialised by construction: src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:180 chains every write onto the previous one and takes the path at flush time, and :149 usePath flushes what is pending into the file it came from before switching, which is what stops one profile's save landing in another's folder (data.test.ts 'lands a save queued before a profile switch in the profile that made it', 25/25 pass). Repeated delivery is handled in the live layer: live.ts:176 LiveOutcome has 'duplicate' and 'out-of-order' with a sequence high-water mark, and a snapshot is reconciled against what is held rather than appended (:425) — live.test.ts 41/41 pass. Analysis cannot land on the wrong position or game: analysis.ts:1333 acceptUpdate matches both the request id and the position key and counts what it drops (analysis.test.ts 36/36), and engine.test.ts 39/39 covers 'two requests cannot share an id', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position' and 'one search at a time, interactive before batch'. | The model is not written down anywhere as a concurrency model, and three of the named hazards have nothing: concurrent migrations (there are no migrations), writes from a deleted profile (nothing checks the profile still exists before a queued write lands), and conflicting edits to the same annotation or repertoire entry (last writer wins — no version field, no optimistic check, and annotations are not persisted at all). No test runs an import, a live update and a library read against one store at the same time, so the section's acceptance criterion is unverified. |
| 420 | Persistence and Migration Acceptance Gate | Test the persistence layer across fifteen situations — fresh database, one needing migration, a populated library, interrupted import and analysis, lock contention, a write failure, backup and restore, a corrupted backup, profile switching, restart, cache cleanup, many games, deleting a game with derived data, and recovery after a forced kill — before calling persistence finished. | partial | — | Eight of the fifteen have a passing test somewhere. Fresh store, lock contention, profile switching, restart and recovery after a kill: data.test.ts 25/25 ('answers with the defaults on a first run', 'writes nothing at all over a file it could not read' using a directory in the file's place, 'shows the other profile its own record', 'reads back exactly what it said it stored', 'writes a save queued microseconds earlier'). Migration of an older file and a future-version file: jsonFile.test.ts 17/17. Backup, restore and a corrupted backup: roundTrip.test.ts. Interrupted analysis: engine.test.ts 39/39 ('a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after'). | Seven have nothing: an interrupted import, disk-full or an injected write failure, cache cleanup (no cache exists), a large number of games through the real store (the scale test builds games in memory and never writes them), deleting a game with associated derived data (nothing derived is stored), a chess database that actually needs migrating, and a populated library opened from disk. The gate has never been run as a gate and no result is recorded anywhere. |

### Analysis Caching And Engine-Result Integrity — 421 to 430

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 421 | Canonical Analysis Cache Keys | Give analysis a deterministic cache key covering the position and everything that materially changes the answer — side to move, castling, en passant, counters, variant, engine identity and version, configuration, depth or node limit, time limit, MultiPV, tablebase settings — so compatible requests can reuse a result and a shallow one is never shown as a deep one. | not started | no | There is no analysis cache in the tree: nothing under src/main/chess or src/shared/chess contains the word cache except a per-object memo of a game's positions (famous.ts:231). Every request runs a fresh search — AnalysisStudio's useEngineAnalysis.tsx:121 mints a new request id and calls chess:analyse, and ipc.ts:280 passes it straight to the engine with no lookup. The nearest things that exist: endgames.ts:293 keys a within-search transposition memo on `${positionKey}\|${depth}`, which is a canonical position plus its search constraint, and analysis.ts:765-774 prefers the deeper of two evaluations of the same position and says why it dropped the other. | Everything the section asks for is absent: no cache store, no key, and therefore nothing that records engine identity, engine version, configuration, time or node limits, MultiPV or tablebase settings against a result, and no distinction between position-only and full-game analysis. The parameters themselves do exist to key on (engine.ts:103 AnalysisLimits, :110 AnalysisRequest.multipv, settings.ts engine block), so this is a subsystem to add rather than data to invent. |
