| 422 | Position Identity and History-Sensitive Rules | Define precisely how a position is identified — placement, side to move, castling, en passant, the halfmove clock where it matters, repetition history where the operation depends on it — using a canonical representation rather than a hash of display text, keeping engine search and history-based draw adjudication apart, and documenting what each key includes. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/rules.ts:288 positionKey is placement, side to move, castling rights and the en passant square, with the comment stating why the clocks are excluded; :224 outcome takes the history and decides repetition and the fifty-move rule from it rather than from the board; positionSearch.ts:313-377 MATCH_RULES documents, per rule, exactly what counts and what is ignored plus the caution to show beside a result, and the exact rule's key is built from the same four fields in the same order; model.ts:118 fingerprintOf hashes the start fields and the UCI list, never display text; analysis.ts:761 validates a recorded evaluation against the game by position key. Tests pass: rules.test.ts 'a position is named by what decides its legal moves' — two boards identical but for a castling right are not the same position, and changing the clocks does not change the name — and 'repetition is decided by the history, not by the board alone' (15/15); positionSearch.test.ts asserts the exact rule's key equals positionKey character for character (35/35); analysis.test.ts 'an evaluation of another position is not used' (36/36). On screen at search/PositionSearch.tsx:51,306, where the rule selector prints what each rule counts and ignores. | — |
| 423 | Cache Validation and Provenance | Store enough about a cached result to know whether it can be used — engine name, version and build, variant, search limits and parameters, position identity, principal variation, evaluation type, depth, seldepth, nodes, duration, completion status, creation time and cache schema version — telling completed searches from partial and interrupted ones and never inventing a missing figure. | not started | no | No cached result exists to validate (see 421), so none of the required metadata is stored anywhere. The field set does exist in memory for a live search: engine.ts:126 AnalysisBase carries score, depth, seldepth, nodes, nps, timeMs, pvUci, pvSan, multipv and bound, :151 AnalysisResult adds complete, bestmove, ponder, the settled lines and a note; analysis.ts:52 NodeEvaluation keeps fen, score, depth, bestmove, pvUci, engine, at and provisional. Nothing is fabricated: engine.test.ts 'a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score' and 'a search that only ever reported a bound says so rather than pretending it is settled' pass (39/39), endgames.test.ts 'a budget that runs out is reported as unproved, never as a no' passes (29/29), and EnginePanel.tsx:174 prints 'depth not reported' rather than a number it does not have. | Nothing persists any of it, so there is no engine build identity, no record of the search limits a result was produced under, no cache schema version, no creation timestamp on a stored result, and no interface that can say a result was reused or distinguish it from a fresh search — because no result is ever reused. |
| 424 | Cache Invalidation Rules | Write explicit invalidation and versioning rules so cached analysis is invalidated or segregated when the position, a search limit, a material engine option, the binary, the variant or the cache schema changes, or when an incomplete record might be mistaken for a finished one — using versioned keys and bounded cleanup rather than clearing everything, and never dropping user data because analysis went stale. | not started | no | There is no cache, so there are no invalidation rules. Two pieces of the behaviour exist in other forms: analysis.ts:765-774 replaces a shallower evaluation of a position with a deeper one and records 'Replaced by a deeper search of the same position' or 'A deeper search of the same position is being used instead' in report.ignored (shown at ReportPanel.tsx:128, tested by analysis.test.ts 'an evaluation of another position is not used, and a deeper one replaces a shallower one', 36/36 pass); and the stored records that do carry a version refuse a future one outright rather than reading it wrongly (puzzles.ts:878, shapes.ts:389, tested in data.test.ts 25/25). | Nothing watches the engine binary, the engine version or the engine options for a change, nothing versions a cache key, and nothing segregates or cleans up stale analysis — all because no analysis is stored. The acceptance criterion is only met vacuously: an incompatible old evaluation cannot be shown as the new result because no old evaluation survives the session. |
| 425 | Shared Position Cache and Reuse Policy | Let identical positions share analysis across different games without mixing the games' own ownership — a position cache independent of the game record, a stated split between position-specific fields and those needing full game context, no duplicate evaluation trees per game, and no reuse of history-sensitive conclusions when the history is unavailable. | not started | no | No position-keyed analysis store exists, so nothing is reused between games: each game's evaluations live on its own session nodes (analysis.ts:93 evaluations keyed by node id) and vanish with it. The shape the section describes does exist for a different purpose, which is where it would hang: positionSearch.ts:413 LibraryIndex maps one position key to every (gameId, ply) that reaches it while keeping the games themselves in a separate map, so position identity and game ownership are already separate; and the history-sensitive conclusions are already kept apart from position facts (rules.ts:224 outcome takes the history, rules.ts:288 positionKey deliberately excludes the clocks). | There is no position cache for analysis, no documented split between position-specific and game-context fields for stored analysis, and no reuse policy — so repeated analysis of a position reached in two games is computed twice, every time, in both games. |
| 426 | Analysis Result Normalisation | Turn the engine's raw output into one documented score model — centipawns, a forced mate, or no score, never mixed — with one stated convention for which side a plus favours, and correct sign conversion when the same score is shown from the mover's side instead of White's. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/evaluation.ts:14 Score is a union of cp/mate/none with the White-is-positive convention written down; :26 fromEngine turns the engine's side-to-move number round once at the edge; :32 forSide; :58 comparable and :66 lossFrom both refuse to put two kinds of score on one scale; src/shared/chess/settings.ts:949 formatForSettings applies the person's analysis.from choice ('white' or 'side-to-move'); src/main/chess/engine.ts:439 onInfo keeps the raw diagnostic fields (nodes, nps, seldepth, bound, timeMs) beside the presentation score and drops an unplayable principal variation to coordinates rather than inventing notation. Passing tests: settings.test.ts "a score from the mover's side is the same score turned round, not a different one" and "a forced mate is spelt out or abbreviated, and is never turned into a number of pawns" (58/58 pass); engine.test.ts:489 "a mate score is a mate, and it is turned round for the side to move exactly once", :469 "a variation that cannot be played is dropped as notation and kept as coordinates", :513 "a bound is marked and never becomes the settled score" (39/39 pass). | — |
| 427 | Analysis Cache Resource Limits | Put bounds on the stored analysis cache — a maximum size, a maximum entry size, expiry, an eviction rule, pinned analysis, how incomplete results are kept, a rebuild after an upgrade — and never evict real user data to stay inside them. | not started | — | There is no analysis cache. Searching for cache across src/shared/chess and src/main/chess finds only unrelated memoisation: famous.ts:231 (positions of a famous game), trainingView.ts:465 (a puzzle pool), library.ts:629 (folded search text). src/main/chess/data.ts:280 lists the five files Chess World persists and none holds analysis, and src/renderer/src/components/chess/analysis/AnalysisStudio.tsx:63 keeps the whole session — its tree, evaluations and notes — in React state only, so everything is gone when the screen changes. | Everything the section asks for. Nothing analysis-related is stored, so there is no size policy, entry cap, expiry, eviction strategy, pinning, incomplete-result retention or post-upgrade rebuild. The one adjacent rule that is honoured is that a single deep search cannot grow without limit, because src/main/chess/ipc.ts:280 clamps movetime, depth and nodes — but what the search produces never reaches storage. |
| 428 | Analysis Cache Observability | Count what the analysis cache does — hits, misses, entries rejected as incompatible, evictions, read and write failures, lookup latency, stored size, how often a position is searched again — and expose it to a developer so it is provable that caching helps. | not started | — | No cache exists, so no counter does. The nearest thing is src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1315, EngineWatch.ignored, which counts engine updates thrown away for not matching the request or the position — observability of mismatches, not of a cache (tested by analysis.test.ts "an answer is taken only when both the request and the position match", 36/36 pass). src/main/chess/engine.ts:835 log() writes to an optional onLog callback that src/main/index.ts:4341 never supplies, so even the engine's own notes are discarded. | Every metric named in the section, and the acceptance criterion with it: a developer cannot tell whether an analysis was newly computed, reused, rejected as stale or failed during persistence, because none of those states exists. There is also no developer diagnostics surface for chess at all — the full channel list is src/preload/index.ts:777-814 and holds nothing of the kind. |
| 429 | Analysis Cancellation and Cache Safety | Give every search its own identifier and a clear life, so cancelling one really stops the engine rather than just a promise, answers from a dead search are thrown away, partial results are labelled as partial, and an old answer can never land on the position now on the board. | done | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:1091 every request gets an id and :1092 a second request may not reuse one; :439 onInfo drops any info line that arrives with no live search, so it cannot be attached to whatever runs next; :719 cancel abandons the search at once, sends UCI stop and waits for the engine's bestmove before the process is considered reusable, returning false when it never settles; :1196 and :1223 restart an engine that ignored stop instead of treating it as idle; :1228 stop by id removes a queued job or cancels the running one, recording ChessEngineError('cancelled'). On the window side src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1333 acceptUpdate requires both the request id and the FEN to match and counts what it drops, and useEngineAnalysis.tsx starts each run with a fresh id and watch. Passing tests (engine.test.ts, 39/39): "results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position", "a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after", "an AbortSignal cancels a running search, and removes a waiting one without touching the engine", "an engine that ignores 'stop' is replaced rather than treated as idle", "cancelling everything empties the queue and stops what is running", "a search that only ever reported a bound says so rather than pretending it is settled". Also analysis.test.ts (36/36): "an evaluation of another position is not used, and a deeper one replaces a shallower one". | — |
| 430 | Analysis Cache Acceptance Gate | Prove the analysis cache against fifteen cases — repeated requests, different search limits, engine version and option changes, differing castling rights and side to move, mate scores, perspective, cancelled partial searches, eviction, corruption, profile separation, restart, and concurrent requests — before calling caching finished. | not started | — | Five of the fifteen are covered incidentally, away from any cache: mate-score handling and perspective by engine.test.ts "a mate score is a mate, and it is turned round for the side to move exactly once" and settings.test.ts "a score from the mover's side is the same score turned round, not a different one"; partial-search cancellation by engine.test.ts "results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position"; side to move and castling rights only indirectly, through rules.ts:66 checkFen validating all four fields before a search is accepted (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:280). | The gate cannot be run. Nine cases are about a cache that does not exist — repeated identical requests, requests with different search limits, engine version changes, engine option changes, cache eviction, cache corruption, profile separation of cached analysis, application restart, and concurrent requests for the same position — and no test file addresses any of them. |

### Archive Integrity, Duplicates, And Library Operations — 431 to 440

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 431 | Stable Game Identity | Identify a stored game by a fingerprint of what actually makes it that game rather than by player names, dates, file paths, move counts, result or a single provider id, and keep that identity separate from the identity of the import that delivered it. | partial | — | src/shared/chess/model.ts:118 fingerprintOf hashes the starting position's first four FEN fields plus every move's UCI coordinates, and says in its own comment that it names a record rather than protecting one; src/main/chess/store.ts:82 and :129 keep the fingerprint following the real moves on every read and every save; model.ts:19 GameSource holds provider, providerGameId and importedAt separately, so the same game arriving twice through different files or providers is two import events and one identity. Passing tests (library.test.ts, 40/40): "the same game twice is the same game", "the same moves in two different tournaments are not one game", "two empty boards are not duplicates of each other". | Variant is not part of the fingerprint, although the section names it among the things to consider — two records with identical moves and different variants hash alike, which is why library.ts:706 compareRecords has to special-case variant before it looks at the fingerprint at all. And nothing tests the identity path itself: there is no test file for src/main/chess/store.ts or src/shared/chess/model.ts, so fingerprintOf and the reimport-recognition criterion are proved only at the comparison level and never through importPgn. |
| 432 | Duplicate Detection and Resolution | Make duplicate handling a deliberate workflow that tells seven kinds of near-match apart and offers skip, keep both, or a non-destructive merge — never silently discarding a record's own annotations or where it came from. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/library.ts:706 compareRecords gives three verdicts (same-game, same-moves, different) with the fields that conflict and the fields only one record knows; :776 duplicateGroups buckets by fingerprint first; src/main/chess/store.ts:147 importPgn takes a DuplicatePolicy of 'skip' or 'keep-both'. On screen as library/DuplicateSheet.tsx, reached from GameLibrary.tsx:349 "Find duplicates", which offers to pick extra copies only for the agreeing kind and explicitly refuses to pick for the same-moves kind. Passing tests (library.test.ts, 40/40): "duplicate groups keep the three kinds apart", "three copies of one game are one group", "one record knowing more is not a disagreement", "the same moves in two different tournaments are not one game", "two different variants are never the same game". | Three parts are absent. (1) Identity is compared over seven metadata fields only (library.ts:610: White, Black, Event, Place, Round, Result, Date) — move comments and GameSource are not among them, so two records with the same moves and metadata but different annotations, or one imported and one from a broadcast, are judged 'same-game' and the sheet offers the extra copy for deletion. Those are the section's cases 3 and 6, and the annotations are exactly what it says must be preserved or put to the user. (2) 'keep-both' is unreachable and there is no merge: src/main/chess/ipc.ts:130 and :139 both call store.importPgn without a policy, so skip is always used and the import sheet offers no choice. (3) store.ts:162 skips on fingerprint alone, so a genuinely different game that happens to share its moves — the case compareRecords is careful to call 'same-moves' — is counted as already here and dropped. |
| 433 | Library Search and Filtering | Search and filter the game library accurately over the fields that are really stored, with normalised case and accents, Unicode names kept intact, filters that combine without clearing each other, sensible handling of empty and huge result sets, and without reading every stored game just to draw a list of titles. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/library.ts:54 fold (case and accent normalisation, documented), :61 nameKey ("Carlsen, Magnus" and "Magnus Carlsen" are one person), :200 SEARCH_FIELDS written out so a search cannot silently cover nothing, :219 searchText folded once per record and invalidated by updatedAt, :246 queryTerms (several words all match, a quoted phrase stays whole), :262 LibraryFilter with nineteen parts, :330 matches, :395 withoutPart, :864 facetsOf. On screen: GameLibrary.tsx:317 search box, :375 FilterPanel, :354 removable "showing games where" chips, fifty rows a page, and three distinct empty states for no games, no matches and an unreadable library. Passing tests (library.test.ts, 40/40): "search is case- and accent-insensitive in both directions", "several words all have to match, and a quoted phrase stays whole", "searching covers the fields a game really has, and only those", "every filter matches on what is stored", "a filter that asks nothing hides nothing", "a date range includes a game that could be in it and excludes one that cannot", "a library of several thousand games filters, sorts and pages quickly". | The last rule of the section is broken by the data path. ChessView.tsx:119 calls window.api.chessGames(), src/main/chess/ipc.ts:82 answers with store.all(), and every record carries its full move array with a FEN string per move — so drawing fifty titles ships every move of every game across the boundary and holds them all in the window. There is no title-only or projected read, and no channel for one. Smaller omissions against the section's field list: move comments are not searched (library.ts:219 leaves them out), and a broadcast's provider name is not filterable — only GameSource.kind is. |
| 434 | Library Sorting and Pagination | Sort the library predictably with a stable secondary order, a defined place for missing dates and unknown metadata, and paging that still means the same place when records are added or deleted while somebody is browsing — no duplicated and no skipped rows. | done | yes | src/shared/chess/library.ts:447 rankOf returns null for anything a game does not have and zero-pads numbers so text comparison compares the number; :473 compareEntries puts null last whichever way the sort runs and breaks ties by updatedAt then id, deliberately unaffected by direction; :493 sortGames works each rank out once; :514 LibraryCursor is the sort position rather than an index, with the reason written down; :536 firstAfter finds the page by halving; :557 pageFrom; :577 pageWords. On screen: GameLibrary.tsx:325 sort picker with seven keys and a reverse button, :403 the pager with Back and Next driven by cursors, :419 "Games 51-100 of 1,234". Passing tests (library.test.ts, 40/40): "what is unknown sorts last, whichever way the order runs", "a number sorts as a number and not as its digits", "equal games keep one order, and reversing the sort does not shuffle them", "a tie is broken by what changed most recently, then by the record itself", "a page keeps meaning the same place when games are removed before it", "a page keeps meaning the same place when games are added before it", "a cursor works on every sort key, including the ones with unknowns", "every sort key has a rank or honestly has none", "a page of an empty library says so rather than showing nothing in particular". | — |
| 435 | Game Editing and Metadata Integrity | Let supported metadata and notes be edited without corrupting the moves, keep source values recoverable where it matters, invalidate only the data that really depends on the edit, and handle a stale or concurrent editor safely. | partial | no | The store half holds the invariant: src/main/chess/store.ts:133 update merges only meta, tags and favourite and routes through save, which recomputes the fingerprint from the unchanged moves (:128), so editing metadata cannot touch the line; src/main/chess/ipc.ts:107 filters the patch field by field; :96 chess:save-moves always writes the whole line so a game cannot end up half saved; model.ts:62 and :106 keep a source's own comment apart from anything Orbis worked out. AnalysisStudio.tsx:72 reopens its session when the game's fingerprint or move count changes, which is the stale-editor guard on the analysis side. | No screen edits a stored game's metadata. Every renderer call to chessUpdate passes only tags or favourite, or a programmatic meta at creation time (GameLibrary.tsx:133 and :154, OpeningLab.tsx:158/:167/:224, FamousGames.tsx:169) — there is no field anywhere for a player name, event, site or date, so the editing this section is about cannot be done. The store also keeps no distinction between an authoritative source value and a user correction, so a corrected name overwrites the original irrecoverably. Notes written in the Analysis Studio are never persisted (it makes no chessSaveMoves call at all). And store.update has no test: there is no test file for src/main/chess/store.ts. |
| 436 | Safe Game Deletion and Undo | Before deleting a game, work out what goes with it — notes, analysis, provenance, favourites, training and repertoire references, shared position data — offer an undo or explain clearly that there is none, and never leave the screen pointing at a record that no longer exists. | partial | yes | src/shared/chess/library.ts:921 deleteWarning names the games (the first eight), counts the starred ones, names the collections that will lose them, and says plainly "This removes them from Orbis. It cannot be undone, and a PGN file you saved earlier is not affected" — careful about what it claims, which is the section's last rule. library/DeleteSheet.tsx renders it as an alertdialog whose backdrop does not dismiss. GameLibrary.tsx:161 removePicked counts what really went rather than what was asked for ("3 of 5 were removed; the rest were already gone") and :86 drops ids whose games have disappeared, so a bulk action cannot silently do less. ChessView.tsx:203 distinguishes "That game is no longer in your Library" from "nothing is open". No shared position cache exists, so nothing shared can be deleted with a game. Passing test: library.test.ts "a delete says exactly what it will take" (40/40). | There is no undo window and no soft deletion — permanent deletion is explained instead, which the section allows but prefers against. The warning accounts for starred games and collections only: a puzzle generated from a game carries a game:<id> tag (puzzles.ts:1249 gameTag, puzzleGen.ts:888 sourceGameId), and deleting the game leaves that training reference dangling with nothing said, which is one of the dependencies the section explicitly lists; the game's own move comments are not mentioned either. The deletion path itself is untested — store.remove has no test file. |
| 437 | Bulk Library Operations | Support bulk actions over a selection — export, tag, favourite, reclassify, delete, reanalyse, move between collections — validating the selection, summarising the consequential ones, batching the work, reporting progress, allowing cancellation, recording partial completion honestly and never firing hundreds of engine searches at once. | partial | yes | GameLibrary.tsx:139 export selected to one PGN file, :150 applyTags worked out per game so one tag list cannot overwrite tags only some games had, :132 star/unstar, :161 remove, :86 prunes a selection whose games are gone, :404 pick-this-page and pick-all-that-match, and DeleteSheet as the summary before the consequential one. library.ts:167 nextTags. The engine already does what the section's warning asks: src/main/chess/engine.ts queues one search at a time with interactive ahead of batch (engine.test.ts "one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once", 39/39 pass), and AnalysisStudio.tsx:228 runs a whole-game pass with a budget, live progress, a cancel button and an honest partial report ("Stopped after 14 positions. What was searched is kept; the rest is not analysed."). Passing test on the pure part: library.test.ts "a bulk tag is worked out per game rather than written over the top" (40/40). | No batching: each bulk action loops one IPC invoke per game (GameLibrary.tsx:133, :154, :163), so a selection of 500 is 500 round trips and 500 whole-file rewrites. No progress for any of them — only a busy flag — and no cancellation once started. Partial completion is recorded for delete but not for star or tag, which report the requested count, and a failure mid-loop shows an error without saying how many landed. There is no bulk reanalyse and no reclassify, and no test exercises any bulk path end to end. |
| 438 | Archive Integrity Verification | Build a checker over the stored archive that validates record relationships, move legality from the stored start, result and PGN/FEN consistency, analysis and annotation references, duplicate identifiers and missing fields; classify what it finds as errors, warnings or recoverable; repair only what is deterministic and reversible; and keep a record of every repair. | not started | no | Nothing answers it. There is no integrity checker anywhere in src/shared/chess, src/main/chess or the chess components, and no channel for one (the complete chess channel list is src/preload/index.ts:777-814). What exists is read-time validation only: src/main/chess/store.ts:31 normalizeGame replays stored notation through model.ts:145 buildMoves rather than believing it, and model.ts:282 treeProblems validates a variation tree for loops, orphans and illegal moves (no stored game has a tree to check). | No checker a person or a developer can run, no classification of findings, no duplicate-identifier or dangling-reference sweep, no PGN/FEN cross-check, no repair log. The read-time validation is also silently lossy, which is the opposite of what this section asks for: store.ts:75 takes built.moves without ever looking at built.ok, so a stored game whose tenth move will not play comes back as a ten-move game with no note at all, and :82 keeps the old fingerprint over it — a quiet rewrite of somebody's game, and nothing tests normalizeGame. |
| 439 | Large Archive Performance | Measure library performance against a realistically large and messy dataset — thousands of games, many from one player, missing metadata, long comments, big analysis histories, many favourites, several sources, lots of duplicates — optimise from the measurements, and keep heavy parsing off the interface. | partial | — | library.test.ts:654 "a library of several thousand games filters, sorts and pages quickly" builds 5,000 games over eight repeated player names, every tenth undated, every seventh without an event, every eleventh starred and duplicates by construction, then times a cold and a warm text search, a four-part filter, six sort keys, a full cursor walk asserting every game is seen exactly once, facetsOf and duplicateGroups — all passing (40/40). The optimisations it guards are real and documented: library.ts:219 folds a record's text once, :610 works out identity once (the comment records that folding inside the comparison cost a second and a half on 5,000 games), :536 finds a page by halving. Parsing and normalisation stay in the main process (store.ts:31); the screen pages at fifty rows instead of mounting thousands (GameLibrary.tsx:403). | The dataset is not the one the section asks for: games are 2 to 8 moves long, no record carries a long PGN comment, there is one source kind throughout, and there are no analysis histories because none are stored. Nothing measures the path that actually feeds the screen — reading and normalising the whole chess-games.json and shipping every game's full move list over chess:games (ipc.ts:82) — so the cost that would govern a real 5,000-game library of 60-move games is unmeasured. |
| 440 | Archive Acceptance Gate | Hold the library to ten checks — predictable import and reimport, duplicate handling that destroys nothing, correct search and sorting, metadata edits that keep the moves, deletion that respects dependencies, bulk operations that recover from failure, integrity checks that catch corruption, large archives that stay responsive, exports that stay valid, and separate libraries per profile — before calling it complete. | partial | — | Four checks are genuinely covered by passing tests, all in library.test.ts (40/40): search and sorting returning correct results, duplicate detection not destroying valid records, large archives staying responsive, and what a deletion will take. Profile separation is covered for the five non-game files by data.test.ts "shows the other profile its own record, and never the first one's" (25/25) and for the path mechanism by jsonFile.test.ts "another profile keeps its own, and never sees the first's" (17/17). | Six of the ten have nothing behind them. Import and reimport: no test file exists for src/main/chess/store.ts, so importPgn has never been run by a test. Editing metadata preserving moves: no editor and no test. Deletion respecting shared dependencies: untested, and the puzzle-to-game reference is not considered at all. Bulk operations recovering from failure: untested. Integrity checks identifying corruption: there is no integrity checker (Section 438). Exported records remaining valid: there is no test file for src/shared/chess/pgn.ts, so writePgn has never been read back in. |

### Local Profile Isolation, Privacy, And Secure Persistence — 441 to 450

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 441 | Integrate with ORBIS Profile Ownership | Keep every private chess record inside ORBIS's existing local profile — games, imports, annotations, engine preferences, saved positions, analysis history, repertoire, puzzle and Academy progress, plans, favourites, challenges — so two people on one computer share nothing, and a profile switch changes which context is active rather than copying data. | partial | — | The main-process half is built and tested. src/main/chess/store.ts:97 ChessStore and src/main/chess/data.ts:168 ChessDataStore (five files, split by how often each is written) both resolve through src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:320 personalFile; both are in the personalStores list at src/main/index.ts:4357, and :4358 followProfile calls useProfile on every one of them whenever the profile changes; src/shared/portable/registry.ts:251 puts all six files in the 'chess' transfer category. Passing tests: data.test.ts "keeps the preferences, the puzzles, the repertoire, the Academy and the plan apart", "shows the other profile its own record, and never the first one's", "does not carry what could not be read in one profile over to the next" (25/25); jsonFile.test.ts "another profile keeps its own, and never sees the first's", "the default profile keeps the folders it always had" (17/17). | Three screens use it now. settings/chessSettingsStore.tsx, puzzles/store.tsx and academy/storage.tsx call chessSettings/chessSaveSettings, chessTraining/chessSaveTraining and chessAcademy/chessSaveAcademy, re-read on onProfileChanged, draw nothing until the answer arrives, and show the store's own sentence when a file could not be read rather than an empty trainer. So the board and engine preferences, the puzzle collection with its history and ratings, and the Academy record are per-profile in the running app. Two gaps remain. coach/CoachPanel.tsx:98 still writes the study plan to window.localStorage, namespaced by a storageKey prop, so chess:progress and chess:openings are registered and called by nothing — the plan is still shared between profiles. And the Orbis window is created with no session partition (index.ts:1040), so the one copy the settings keep in window storage for the first frame is shared by every profile. It is a mirror of whatever the active profile said last and never a source after the carry-over, and a profile switch re-applies what the new profile really says (chessSettingsStore.tsx subscribes to onProfileChanged at module level, so the boards follow even with no screen open) — but on a launch straight after a switch the very first frame can still be painted from the previous profile's colours, for the one frame before the profile answers. |
| 442 | Resolve Profile Paths Safely | Build chess storage paths through ORBIS's own path resolution from a stable internal profile id rather than a display name, correctly across operating systems, so a rename, a switch or an odd profile label can never redirect a write outside that profile's folder — and refuse to fall back to the shared application directory when the profile cannot be resolved. | partial | — | src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:320 personalFile joins with node:path through src/shared/profiles.ts:21 dataDirFor, which takes the internal id alone; the id is gated at profiles.ts:44 normalizeProfile by /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,80}$/, so a label containing a separator or dots can never become a folder, and renaming changes only Profile.name so the association with existing data survives. No chess file builds a storage path of its own. Passing tests: profiles.test.ts "the first profile keeps Orbis's original data folder; others get their own" and "stored profiles are checked, and the first one is always shown first", whose assertion reads "an id that could not be a folder is refused" (4/4); jsonFile.test.ts "another profile keeps its own, and never sees the first's" (17/17). | The failure bullet is not honoured. src/main/personal/profiles.ts:35 activeId falls back to DEFAULT_PROFILE_ID whenever the active id is not in the list it managed to read, and src/main/index.ts:4363 only console.errors a switch that threw — so a profile that cannot be resolved resolves silently to the default, whose dataDirFor is '.', the application's own data directory. Chess would then read and write the first profile's games with nothing on screen to say so. |
| 443 | Profile-Switch Lifecycle | Make a profile switch a defined sequence: stop taking new profile work, finish or cancel in-flight writes, cancel the old profile's analysis, drop its live subscriptions, flush, dispose and rebuild profile-scoped services, open and validate the new store, and redraw the interface — with no late answer from the old profile reaching the new screen. | partial | — | Steps 2, 5, 7 and 8 are done and tested. src/main/storage/jsonFile.ts:149 usePath flushes what is pending to the file it belongs to before taking the new path (the comment names this as the invariant that keeps one person's data out of another's folder); src/main/chess/data.ts:154 clears the old file's unreadable-field notes so they cannot be reported against the new profile; src/main/index.ts:4358 resolves the new profile and every store re-reads and re-validates, with data.ts:120 distinguishing a file that may not be written from one that merely had a problem. Passing tests: jsonFile.test.ts "a write queued before a profile switch lands in the profile it came from" (17/17); data.test.ts "lands a save queued before a profile switch in the profile that made it", "does not carry what could not be read in one profile over to the next" (25/25). | Five of the ten steps are absent for chess. Nothing stops serving profile-specific IPC during a switch. The engine is not a personal store (index.ts:4341 and :4357), so searches owned by the old profile keep running and their answers keep arriving. Live broadcasts are not detached — chessLive.unfollow is called only on quit (index.ts:5666). Nothing profile-scoped is disposed or reconstructed beyond swapping file paths. And nothing refreshes the window: 'profiles:changed' (index.ts:4309) is listened to only by ProfilesView.tsx:42, so an open Chess World keeps showing the previous profile's game list, open game and filters — the section's "do not keep private old-profile content visible". There is also no defined behaviour for a switch that fails halfway, and no test for rapid switching. |
| 444 | Local-First Privacy Boundary | Keep Chess World local: no cloud database, no automatic upload, no profile sync, no analytics, and nothing personal leaving the machine — PGNs, annotations, repertoire, puzzle and Academy history, private analysis, profile ids, local paths — with each permitted external service's network behaviour written down and optional ones off until asked for. | done | — | There are exactly two outbound edges and both are user-initiated. src/main/chess/install.ts:49 the Stockfish release API, reached only through an install the person asks for (ipc.ts:258), and src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:505 the public Lichess broadcast endpoints, reached only when the Live area asks (LiveChess.tsx:174). No fetch, net.fetch, axios or XMLHttpRequest exists anywhere else in src/shared/chess, src/main/chess or the ~60 chess components; there is no upload path of any kind; the optional provider token is used only if supplied (lichess.ts:482); and chess is registered to Orion and the Orb only as an app to open (src/shared/commandCenter.ts:292), so no model is handed a game. ipc.ts:130 records basename(path) rather than the chosen file's full path. Network behaviour is documented per service in docs/chess-world.md:202 ("The engine: why nothing is bundled") and :247 ("The live-data position"). The guards on both edges pass: install.test.ts "an asset is ignored when its address is not the project's own host, and when a field is missing" (19/19); lichess.test.ts "a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address" (47/47). | — |
| 445 | Secure Handling of Imported Content | Treat PGN files, archives and provider answers as untrusted input, validated before they reach storage, the screen, a file path, the engine's command line, a link or a log, so a crafted comment, tag, player name or event name stays data and cannot execute code or corrupt the application. | partial | — | The behaviour is there. src/main/chess/store.ts:31 normalizeGame caps every string (names 120, comments 20,000, tags 40 entries) and replays every move instead of trusting it; src/main/chess/ipc.ts:63 asMoves checks each field of every move; the store is JSON so there is no SQL to inject; and no chess component uses dangerouslySetInnerHTML or innerHTML anywhere, so comments and names are React text. The engine's command line cannot be reached from content: rules.ts:66 checkFen validates every FEN field (whitespace-split and grammar-checked, so no line break can pass) before ipc.ts:280 forwards it, limits are clamped there, install.ts:433 checkBinaryPath refuses a relative path, a line break or a null byte, and engine.ts:994 spawns with an argument array and shell:false. pgn.ts:306 escapes backslashes and quotes in tag values and :351 strips braces from a comment on the way out. A URL from a provider passes liveView.ts:1095 isWebLink before LiveChess.tsx:342 opens it. Nothing executes an imported file. Partly tested: install.test.ts "a binary path is checked, and a relative one or a line break is refused" and "only the program and its licence come out of the archive, out of everything in it" (19/19). | The acceptance criterion is untested. There is no test file for src/shared/chess/pgn.ts and none for src/main/chess/store.ts, so nothing anywhere feeds a crafted comment, tag, player name or event name through readPgn and importPgn — the length caps in normalizeGame and asMoves, the brace stripping in writePgn and the tag escaping have never been exercised. readPgn appears in only two test files (variants.test.ts, clock.test.ts) and both are about variant and time-control tags. |
| 446 | Engine Process and Binary Trust | Only launch an engine binary that resolves to an expected file in an approved or user-chosen place, never a path that came from content, using argument-safe process APIs, with writable options limited to what the engine declares, graceful handling of a missing or wrong binary, a recorded version and binary identity, and no claim of verification beyond what is really checked. | done | — | src/main/chess/engine.ts:852 discover uses the path the person chose or the one the installer put in app-data and nothing else, reporting a wrong configured path rather than quietly substituting another engine; install.ts:433 checkBinaryPath refuses a relative path, a line break, a null byte, a missing file, a directory and a non-program on Windows; engine.ts:994 spawns with an argument array and shell:false ("the path is a path, not something a shell gets to interpret"); :614 refuses an option the engine never declared and :619 clamps one it limits. The download is verified: install.ts:151 follows only https URLs on the project's own hosts, :232 refuses an asset with no published digest unless that is asked for explicitly, :289 discards bytes that do not hash to the digest, and engine.ts:945 records the version, the sha256 and a verified flag that status() reports honestly as true, false or null. Passing tests: engine.test.ts "an engine somebody chose is used, and a bad choice is reported rather than swapped for another", "with nothing installed the state says so, and an analysis is refused honestly", "an install record that cannot be read is a problem, not a missing engine", "installing downloads the verified archive, keeps the licence, and is safe to repeat", "an install that cannot be verified leaves nothing behind and says why", "an option the engine never mentioned is not sent, and one it limits is clamped to its limit" (39/39); install.test.ts "a download that does not match its checksum is discarded and never left where it could be run", "a release with no checksum is refused rather than run, unless that is asked for explicitly", "an archive that is not an archive is refused before anything is written", "a binary path is checked, and a relative one or a line break is refused" (19/19). | — |
| 447 | External Provider and Network Security | Handle the live provider securely: HTTPS endpoints, validated response structure, content type, status codes and payload sizes, timeouts and cancellation, credentials never logged, rate limits and licensing respected, no scraping, and no remote clock, rating, result or identifier trusted without checking its type. | partial | — | src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:505 an https base URL; :651 an AbortController with a 20-second timeout on every request; :655 a 429 holds the provider off for the documented minute and the next request is not even sent; :663 any non-ok status is a stated fault; :667 a 200 whose body is an error object is still a refusal, because the provider sends refusals under the PGN content type; :578 the optional token travels as a header only, never in the address; :632 a circuit breaker after repeated failures; :1021 the round stream stops rather than growing past 4 MB without yielding a game; and every field is read through a checked reader (:304, :452) with implausible clocks refused rather than shown. docs/chess-world.md:247 records that every endpoint came from the published OpenAPI document and that nothing scrapes. Passing tests (lichess.test.ts, 47/47): "a 429 is honoured by waiting, and the next request is not even sent", "a 200 whose body is an error object is a refusal, not a game", "a provider that does not answer is a timeout, said as one", "a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address", "a round whose shape has changed degrades to a schema diagnostic and never falls back to scraping", "requests go one at a time, because the provider documents that they must", "the provider clock is centiseconds, and an implausible one is refused rather than shown". | Payload size and content type are not validated for the non-streaming requests: lichess.ts:654 does await res.text() with no byte cap, so an oversized or wrongly typed answer is buffered whole before anything judges it — only the round stream has a cap (:1021). The response's content type is never inspected at all; that is a deliberate choice, because the provider sends refusals under the PGN type, but the section asks for both the type check and a size bound, and the acceptance gate at Section 450 names oversized payloads as a case to test. No test covers an oversized response. |
| 448 | Privacy-Conscious Diagnostics | Log the failures worth knowing about under stable, non-sensitive event names and a stated data-minimisation policy — never whole PGNs, private comments, full local paths or credentials — and give a way to collect a local diagnostic report whose contents are disclosed, redactable and never uploaded. | partial | no | The live side is properly shaped and minimal: src/main/chess/live/lichess.ts:584 fault builds a diagnostic of provider, operation, kind, message, optional field and status plus a timestamp, carrying no game content; it reaches the window on chess:live-diagnostic (src/preload/index.ts:789) and live/LiveFault.tsx shows it. Credentials are kept out and that is tested — lichess.test.ts "a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address" (47/47). src/main/chess/ipc.ts:130 records only basename(path) for an import, so a full local path is never stored. Nothing about chess is uploaded anywhere. | Most of the section. There is no diagnostic report to collect, no disclosure of its contents and no redaction, and no channel for one (the complete chess channel list is src/preload/index.ts:777-814). The engine's log sink is dead: engine.ts:835 log() writes to this.o.onLog and src/main/index.ts:4341 constructs ChessEngine with only home, so every engine-startup failure, cancellation, timeout and restart note is discarded. A PGN parse failure is reported to the importer but never logged; a failed profile switch is a bare console.error (index.ts:4363); a failed engine install is sent on chess:engine-failed, which nothing in the renderer listens to; and there is no cache corruption or cache-failure event because there is no cache. |
| 449 | Data Portability and Deletion | Let a person export their chess records in durable formats — PGN for games, something structured for progress and annotations — document what PGN cannot carry, and delete at a stated scope: one game, one analysis session, one training record, one collection, or everything in this profile, without touching the rest of ORBIS. | partial | yes | Export of games works and is reachable: src/main/chess/ipc.ts:144 chess:export-pgn writes up to 5,000 selected games to a file the person chooses, :160 chess:to-pgn hands one game back as text, and pgn.ts:314 writePgn puts back everything PGN can carry including the tags Orbis has no field for (model.ts:48 meta.extra); on screen as "Save as PGN" (GameLibrary.tsx:381). Structured export of the rest rides on the profile transfer: src/shared/portable/registry.ts:251 lists all six chess files in the 'chess' category with a plain description. Deleting one game is ipc.ts:116 with library.ts:921 deleteWarning, which is careful about what it claims — "It cannot be undone, and a PGN file you saved earlier is not affected" — and is tested by library.test.ts "a delete says exactly what it will take" (40/40). docs/chess-world.md:265 records that variations cannot be represented in what is stored or exported. | Four of the five deletion scopes do not exist: there is no channel to delete all of this profile's Chess World data, none to delete one training record, none for an analysis session (none is stored), and a collection can only be emptied game by game through the tag sheet. There is no export of progress, puzzle history, repertoire or Academy records from Chess World itself — only the whole-profile transfer — and because the mounted screens keep those in window.localStorage rather than the per-profile files (Section 441), that transfer would not carry what the person is actually using. |
| 450 | Security and Profile-Isolation Acceptance Gate | Run a twelve-case security and isolation pass — two independent profiles, rapid switching, malformed PGN comments and metadata, path traversal, unsafe engine paths, malformed and oversized provider responses, injection attempts, diagnostic redaction, selective deletion, restoring a backup into the right profile, and late answers from a previous profile — and write down the security boundary and what it does not cover. | partial | — | Five cases are covered by passing tests. Two independent local profiles: data.test.ts "shows the other profile its own record, and never the first one's" (25/25) and jsonFile.test.ts "another profile keeps its own, and never sees the first's" (17/17). Path traversal, at the gate where an id becomes a folder: profiles.test.ts "stored profiles are checked, and the first one is always shown first", asserting "an id that could not be a folder is refused" (4/4). Unsafe engine executable paths: install.test.ts "a binary path is checked, and a relative one or a line break is refused" (19/19). Malformed provider responses: lichess.test.ts "a round whose shape has changed degrades to a schema diagnostic and never falls back to scraping", "a 200 whose body is an error object is a refusal, not a game" and five more (47/47). Diagnostic redaction of a credential: lichess.test.ts "a token and a user agent are sent as headers, and never in the address". | Seven cases have no test: rapid profile switching, malformed PGN comments and metadata (there is no test file for src/shared/chess/pgn.ts or src/main/chess/store.ts), oversized network payloads, query-injection attempts, selective deletion, restoring a backup into the correct profile, and late asynchronous responses from a previous profile — the last of which is not merely untested but unimplemented for chess, since neither the engine nor the live manager is detached on a profile switch (Section 443). docs/chess-world.md documents the engine and live positions and eleven known limitations but contains no written security boundary, which this section asks for by name. |

### Background Jobs, Workers, And Concurrency — 451 to 460

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 451 | Central Background-Job Model | One shared way to track every long-running chess operation — imports, exports, game analyses, backups, indexing — so each has an id, a type, an owning profile, progress, a cancel request and a result, and none of it keeps running as untracked work after its screen closes. | partial | yes | src/main/chess/engine.ts:771-781 (the private Job record: requestId, fen, limits, priority, signal, cleanup), engine.ts:817 (the queue), engine.ts:286+1312 (`queued` in the status); src/renderer/src/components/chess/analysis/AnalysisStudio.tsx:189-265 (the sweep: progress, note, cancel) and :82 (unmount stops the loop). Tests: 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once' and 'an id is generated when none is given, and it is on every update' in src/main/chess/engine.test.ts — 39/39 pass. | There is no shared job abstraction. `grep -i job` across src/shared/chess, src/main/chess and the renderer chess tree finds nothing but Stockfish's own queue inside engine.ts, and that record never leaves the main process. Of the twelve job types the section lists, only position analysis and full-game analysis have any lifecycle; PGN import and export are single blocking IPC calls with no id, no progress and no cancel (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:117-160); bulk reanalysis, opening classification, puzzle validation, archive integrity checking, database backup, database restoration and large-library indexing do not exist at all. No job carries an owning profile, a retry policy, a resource requirement or a user-visible description, and there is no channel that lists jobs — docs/ipc-mapping.md:110-112 shows no job channel. |
| 452 | Worker Isolation and UI Responsiveness | Keep expensive chess work — parsing big PGN files, indexing, classifying moves, normalising engine output, preparing the evaluation graph, integrity checks — off the thread that draws the screen, so buttons and typing stay responsive. | partial | yes | The process boundary does the work for three of the heaviest things: PGN reading and writing run in the Electron main process (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:117-160, src/main/chess/store.ts:147-182), the engine is its own child process (src/main/chess/engine.ts), and live ingestion runs in main (src/main/chess/live/manager.ts). Test: 'a library of several thousand games filters, sorts and pages quickly' in src/shared/chess/library.test.ts — passes and prints each cost (~1.3 s total across eleven passes over 5,000 games). | No worker of any kind exists in the chess tree — `new Worker`, `worker_threads`, `utilityProcess`, `startTransition` and `useDeferredValue` all return nothing across src/main/chess and src/renderer/src/components/chess. Three expensive things still run synchronously on the renderer's main thread: the endgame proof search (EndgameLab.tsx:120-134 calls `verifyEntry` inside a `setTimeout(…, 0)` purely so the busy frame can paint first, and the comment says so in terms — "It is still on the main thread"); the library's filter, sort, facet and duplicate passes over the whole collection (GameLibrary.tsx:70); and evaluation-graph preparation (EvalGraph.tsx:43-44). And `readPgn` is synchronous inside the main process, so a very large import blocks every IPC channel in Orbis, not just chess's. No measurement of UI responsiveness under any of this has ever been taken. |
| 453 | Cancellation Propagation | When somebody cancels something, the cancellation must reach whatever is actually doing the work — the engine gets a stop, a network request is aborted, an import stops reading, an export stops writing and tidies up — and nothing may be marked cancelled while its process carries on. | partial | yes | Engine: AbortSignal and an explicit `stopping` state (engine.ts:75,121,1117-1138,1213-1249), and a cancel is not settled until `bestmove` arrives. Tests in src/main/chess/engine.test.ts, all passing: 'a cancelled search fails as cancelled, and the engine is usable again straight after', 'an AbortSignal cancels a running search, and removes a waiting one without touching the engine', 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', 'an engine that ignores "stop" is replaced rather than treated as idle', 'cancelling everything empties the queue and stops what is running'. Network: 'a stream that is stopped delivers nothing more and does not try to reconnect' in src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts. Subscription: 'switching rounds stops the old subscription before the new one starts' and 'a retired subscription cannot report faults or connection states either' in src/main/chess/live/manager.test.ts. Bulk: AnalysisStudio.tsx:268-271 (`cancelSweep` clears the flag and stops the engine). | Three of the section's six examples have no cancellation at all. An import cannot be stopped: `chess:import-pgn` reads the file and parses every game in one awaited call (ipc.ts:117-134). An export cannot be stopped and writes no temporary file to clean up (ipc.ts:143-160). The endgame integrity scan cannot be stopped once started — `verifyEntry` is a synchronous search with only a node budget (EndgameLab.tsx:131, endgames.ts:336-340). And the sweep's unmount cleanup clears its own loop flag but does not call `engine.stop()` (AnalysisStudio.tsx:82), so the search in flight runs to its limit after the screen has gone. |
| 454 | Job Scheduling and Resource Arbitration | Coordinate background work so it cannot starve the thing the person is waiting for: explicit concurrency limits, priorities, a cap on how many engine processes may run, a way to pause or stop bulk work, and defined behaviour when the machine is short of processor or memory. | partial | yes | One engine process, one search at a time, and a two-level priority queue that takes the first interactive job ahead of any batch job (engine.ts:62-64, 1147-1150). Resources sized rather than assumed: `defaultThreads` takes about half the logical processors and never more than eight, `defaultHash` a thirty-second of memory between 16 and 512 MB (engine.ts:174-183, applied at 1047-1048). Tests passing in src/main/chess/engine.test.ts: 'threads and memory are sized for a laptop, and never take every core', 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once', 'an option cannot be changed in the middle of a search'. Provider side: 'requests go one at a time, because the provider documents that they must', 'the same question asked twice at once costs one request', 'a provider that keeps failing is left alone for a while instead of being asked again' in src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts. | The batch lane is unreachable from the window: `chessAnalyse` is declared without a priority field (src/shared/types.ts:2360) and `chess:analyse` hardcodes `priority: 'interactive'` for every request (ipc.ts:303), so the whole-game sweep's hundreds of searches sit in the same lane as the board's own ask — the exact case the section says must not happen. There is no pause for any bulk operation, only stop. Nothing reacts to processor or memory pressure at runtime: the Threads and Hash values are fixed once at construction and never revisited. A profile switch swaps only the two chess stores (src/main/index.ts:4357-4361), leaving a running engine search and a followed broadcast belonging to the previous profile. No backup snapshot and no migration exist to arbitrate. |
| 455 | Job State Persistence and Recovery | Where recovery genuinely helps, remember what a job was doing across a restart and classify it honestly afterwards — completed, failed, cancelled, interrupted, recoverable, needs the person — never resuming blindly and never showing an interrupted engine search as a finished one. | not started | no | Nothing persists job state. The quit path flushes data only: src/main/index.ts:5661-5666 (games, the five data files, engine shutdown, live unfollow). src/main/chess/data.test.ts proves the data half ('writes a save queued microseconds earlier', 'still refuses to write over a file it could not read, even on the way out') — 25/25 pass, but these are about records, not jobs. | No job state is written anywhere, so after a forced restart the application has no account of an interrupted import, export or game sweep and cannot report or resume one. The nearest thing is the exercise state machine's `interrupted` state — "Taken away -- the window closed, the app quit. Can be picked up again" (src/shared/chess/attempt.ts:59, with transitions leading back to `loading` and never straight to `active`) — and nothing drives it: `grep "'interrupted'"` over the renderer chess tree finds no caller, and the attempt session lives only in React state (puzzles/Solver.tsx:71, academy/TaskCard.tsx:111) so only a finished Performance is ever written. None of the six classifications the section lists appears in any store shape (src/main/chess/shapes.ts). |
| 456 | Retry Policy and Idempotency | Bounded retries with backoff, no endless retrying of permanent failures, respect for provider rate limits, and enough care about record identity that retrying never produces duplicate games or contradictory analysis records. | partial | yes | Live: bounded jittered exponential backoff with a ceiling and a give-up (lichess.ts:512-513, 899-932), a 429 honoured by waiting, and a circuit breaker that stops asking and reopens on one good answer (lichess.ts:640-650). Tests passing in src/main/chess/live/lichess.test.ts: 'a dropped stream is retried, with a bounded jittered wait, and then given up on', 'the jitter really varies the wait, so many clients do not reconnect in the same instant', 'a 429 is honoured by waiting, and the next request is not even sent', 'a 429 on the stream waits the documented minute before the round is followed again', 'a provider that keeps failing is left alone for a while instead of being asked again', 'one good answer closes the circuit again'. Engine: a retry is a new identifiable request — 'two requests cannot share an id, because then their results could not be told apart' and 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position' (engine.test.ts). Import identity: `fingerprint` with a skip policy (store.ts:150, 162, 176). | The import side is untested — `src/main/chess/store.ts` has no test file at all (only data.test.ts, engine.test.ts and install.test.ts exist under src/main/chess), so `importPgn`'s fingerprint deduplication, which is the only thing standing between a repeated import and duplicate games, has never been exercised. No job anywhere records an attempt count or a retry policy, and nothing offers a retry: a failed import, export or sweep simply ends. A failed write is never retried — `data.ts` refuses and says so instead (which is correct, but it is a refusal, not a retry policy). |
| 457 | Worker Communication Contracts | Strongly checked messages between the window and background services, each identifying its request, its type and its error state; incoming messages validated rather than trusted; no arbitrary executable strings as commands; and progress throttled instead of flooding the screen with per-node engine output. | partial | yes | Every chess IPC handler takes `unknown` and checks it (src/main/chess/ipc.ts:18 and the `str`/`asMoves`/`checkFen` guards throughout). Every engine update carries its `requestId` and the `fen` it was calculated in (engine.ts:125-131), and the window validates then matches before using — `readUpdate` reads field by field and `acceptUpdate` drops anything whose id or position does not match, counting the drops (src/shared/chess/analysis.ts:1333-1359; useEngineAnalysis.tsx:99-113). Live channels are read the same way (liveView.ts:357-404 `readLiveState`, :406+ `readDiagnostic`, counting `unreadable`). Nothing arbitrary reaches the engine: `safeOptionValue` refuses any value with a newline or over 200 chars (uci.ts:210-214) and the binary path is validated by `checkBinaryPath`. Tests passing: 'a line split across reads, and several lines in one read, are both read correctly', 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes', 'a malformed "bestmove" ends the search honestly instead of leaving it waiting', 'an update listener that throws does not take the search down with it' (engine.test.ts); 'an update from the other process is checked, not trusted' (analysis.test.ts); 'a connection state that is not one of the five is read as connecting rather than trusted' (liveView.test.ts). | Nothing is throttled. The engine calls `onUpdate` for every `info` line (engine.ts:473, 1109) and ipc.ts:308 forwards each one straight down `chess:analysis`, and useEngineAnalysis.tsx:102-103 calls `setWatch` on each, so a three-line search produces an IPC message and a React state update per engine line per slot — the per-node flood the section names. No chess message carries a payload version or any size expectation, and none carries an owning profile. |
| 458 | Resource Cleanup and Process Ownership | Every process, worker, timer, stream, file handle and subscription has a named owner and a disposal path; closing a view removes its listeners, stops its timers and prevents late callbacks touching dead state; and the engine process's life is not tied to one panel. | partial | — | The engine process is owned by the service, not a panel: `restart` replaces it and `shutdown` asks it to quit — 'restart replaces the process, and shutdown asks it to quit' and 'an engine that dies mid-search fails the search with what it said, and can be started again' (engine.test.ts). Subscriptions are retired before replacement: 'switching rounds stops the old subscription before the new one starts' and 'a retired subscription cannot report faults or connection states either' (manager.test.ts). The 3D scene disposes everything: 'taking the scene down lets go of every shape and every material' and 'a scene that cannot be built does not leave half of one behind' (three/scene.test.ts). Quit path registers all four chess owners (src/main/index.ts:5661-5666). Renderer counts balance: 5 addEventListener / 5 removeEventListener, 7 setInterval / 7 clearInterval, 3 requestAnimationFrame / 3 cancels across the chess tree, 25 effect cleanups. AnalysisStudio.tsx:82 clears the sweep flag on unmount. | The acceptance criterion is a repetition measurement — "repeatedly opening and closing Chess World views does not steadily increase process count, timer count, or memory usage" — and no test or probe does that; docs/chess-world.md:303 still records that nothing here has been seen running. Two owned things are not released on the events that should release them: the sweep's unmount stops the loop but not the engine, so the search in flight continues; and a profile switch (src/main/index.ts:4357-4361) leaves the engine searching and the broadcast followed for the profile that has gone. |
| 459 | Concurrency and Race-Condition Testing | Deterministic tests for the races a desktop chess app really hits — rapid position changes, several analysis requests, a profile switch mid-analysis, closing the window during an import, duplicate live events, out-of-order cache writes, an old worker reporting after a new job starts — verifying the resulting state, not just that nothing threw. | partial | — | Six of the eleven named scenarios have passing deterministic tests, all using injected clocks and controlled promises rather than sleeps. Rapid position changes and multiple analysis requests: 'results that arrive after a cancel are dropped, and never land on the next position', 'one search at a time, interactive before batch, and never two conversations at once', 'two requests cannot share an id, because then their results could not be told apart' (src/main/chess/engine.test.ts). Duplicate live events: 'a repeated delivery is recognised by its id and reported as a duplicate' (src/main/chess/live/manager.test.ts). Cache writes finishing out of order: 'an older snapshot arriving late is noted and does not rewind the board', 'several snapshots of one game in a burst collapse to the newest, and the game is not doubled', 'several deltas of one game in a burst are all applied, in order, and never collapsed'. An old worker reporting after a new job starts: 'a late delivery from a round nobody is watching any more cannot touch the board', 'what decides whether a delivery counts is the subscription it came from, not the id written on it', 'a burst still waiting when the round is switched is thrown away, not applied to the new round'. A profile switch against a pending write: 'lands a save queued before a profile switch in the profile that made it' (src/main/chess/data.test.ts). | Five of the eleven have no test. Nothing covers profile switching during an analysis (and the code would not survive it — index.ts:4357-4361 does not touch the engine), closing the window during an import, cancelling a job during a database transaction, restarting during an export, or two imports of the same file. Concurrent writes to puzzle progress are untested because `src/main/chess/store.ts` has no test file. No test fixture drives a worker or a second process, because no worker exists. |
| 460 | Background-Work Acceptance Gate | Show, as one demonstration, that long jobs stay off the UI's critical path, cancellation propagates, resource limits hold, retries are bounded, worker messages are validated, interrupted jobs are reported correctly, shared resources are disposed, profile ownership is respected, concurrent work keeps data intact, and chess stays responsive under realistic load. | partial | — | Four of the ten items hold with passing tests: resource limits ('threads and memory are sized for a laptop, and never take every core', 'requests go one at a time, because the provider documents that they must'); bounded retry ('a dropped stream is retried, with a bounded jittered wait, and then given up on'); message validation ('an update from the other process is checked, not trusted', 'malformed lines are skipped and the search still finishes'); concurrent operations preserving integrity (the manager's burst, duplicate and late-delivery tests, and data.test.ts's profile-switch write test). | Three items fail outright. Interrupted jobs are not reported at all (451/455 — nothing persists job state). Profile ownership is not respected by the engine or the live feed (index.ts:4357-4361). Responsiveness under realistic load has never been measured — docs/chess-world.md:303 still says "Not one line of this has been seen running. There is no screenshot and no runtime check over the debugging port", and no script under scripts/ photographs or probes Chess World. The remaining three — jobs off the critical path, cancellation propagating, shared resources disposed — are each only partly true, as 452, 453 and 458 set out. There is no gate artefact of any kind: no checklist, no acceptance test, no recorded run. |

### Renderer Performance, Memory, And Accessibility — 461 to 470

| # | Section | What it asks | Status | On screen | Evidence | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 461 | Renderer Performance Baseline | Measure the real integrated Chess World screen — opening it, opening the board, loading a game, stepping through moves, showing engine output, opening a big library, searching, opening Analysis Studio, switching modules, showing a live game, switching profiles — under repeatable conditions, with startup, route activation, query, engine and render costs kept apart, and budgets set from what was measured. | partial | — | Two real measurements exist and pass. src/renderer/src/components/chess/three/scene.test.ts: 'the three things that happen while somebody is playing all cost well under a frame' — 200 rounds each after a warm-up, asserting placing the pieces under 8 ms, moving the marks under 4 ms, moving the camera under 1 ms, with the reasoning written out. src/shared/chess/library.test.ts: 'a library of several thousand games filters, sorts and pages quickly' — eleven operations over 5,000 games, each cost printed. And EndgameLab.tsx:42-61 sets its node budgets from a measured ~2 ms per position, correcting an earlier comment that claimed numbers nobody had timed. | None of this measures the integrated UI, which is what the section asks for. Not one of the eleven listed timings has been taken: opening Chess World, opening the chessboard, loading a saved game, navigating between moves, displaying engine analysis, opening a large game library, searching and filtering on screen, opening Analysis Studio, switching between training modules, displaying a live game, switching local profiles. There is no split of startup cost from route-activation cost from query time from engine time from render time, and no budget is set anywhere. docs/orbis-performance.md (33 lines) does not mention chess, and docs/chess-world.md:303 records that nothing here has been seen running. |
| 462 | Chessboard Rendering Efficiency | Stop the board being rebuilt for every engine update — keep high-frequency evaluation data apart from board structure, use stable component identities — while keeping legal-move highlights, selection, promotion, drag-and-drop, keyboard use, coordinates, orientation and correctness at every window size intact. | partial | yes | The 3D board is the tested half and it is strong, all passing in src/renderer/src/components/chess/three/: 'the board is built out of two meshes and not sixty-four', 'showing a different position moves the pieces that are there rather than building more', 'the marks are reused rather than rebuilt, however many times they change', 'each kind of mark is drawn once per square and no more', 'turning the board round moves the pieces and nothing else', 'nothing on the board is left standing on a square it has left', 'the viewport is only taken when it is a real size', 'the number of pixels drawn is capped rather than believed'; plus selection.test.ts on highlights from the rules, selection, the promotion question and keyboard walking. The 2D board uses stable keys (`key={square}`, `key={rank}` — Board.tsx:131,150), derives pieces, legal squares and the checked king through `useMemo` on `fen` (Board.tsx:53-64), and evaluation lives in separate components (EvalBar, EvalGraph). | Nothing in the chess tree is memoised — `React.memo` appears nowhere — so every engine `info` line re-renders AnalysisStudio and reconciles all 64 cells of the 2D board even though `fen` has not changed; combined with the unthrottled update stream (457) that is many times a second. There is no component test for the 2D board at all: the only tests under src/renderer/src/components/chess are the pure helpers in three/. And neither board has drag-and-drop (`ondrag`, `draggable`, `dragstart` return nothing), so "Drag-and-drop works" has nothing to be true of — play is click or keyboard only. |
| 463 | Virtualisation for Large Collections | Use virtualised lists or an equivalent strategy for collections that grow — game libraries, move lists, puzzle sets, opening trees, engine variations, live directories, lesson catalogues, import histories — keeping selection, keyboard use, scroll position and accessibility, and without virtualising tiny fixed lists. | partial | yes | The game library uses the equivalent strategy the section permits: fifty rows at a time, found by cursor rather than by counting so removing games before a page cannot silently skip ten (GameLibrary.tsx:26-38,70-82; `pageFrom` at src/shared/chess/library.ts:557-584). Tested hard and passing in src/shared/chess/library.test.ts: 'a page is a page, and it says which games it is showing', 'a page keeps meaning the same place when games are removed before it', 'a page keeps meaning the same place when games are added before it', 'a cursor works on every sort key, including the ones with unknowns', 'a page of an empty library says so rather than showing nothing in particular', and the 5,000-game walk that sees every game exactly once in exactly 100 pages. Selection survives paging (GameLibrary.tsx:115-129). Small lists are capped rather than virtualised: opening search at 12 (OpeningExplorer.tsx:92), engine variations at 5 (ipc.ts, EnginePanel.tsx:140), facet lists at 24 and 30, import warnings at 40. | The library is the only collection handled. Nothing else the section names has virtualisation or a cap: the analysis move list renders every row including variations with no window (analysis/MoveTree.tsx:40), and the puzzle collection, the opening tree, the live-game directory, the academy lesson catalogue, the evaluation-sample list and the import history all render in full. No test anywhere checks that a view mounts fewer elements than the collection holds, so even the library's claim is proved at the data layer only, never at the DOM. |
| 464 | Memory Ownership and Leak Prevention | Audit the long-lived and memory-heavy things — engine output buffers, accumulated listeners, retained PGN strings, duplicate histories, stale analysis trees, unbounded caches — profile them, and set explicit bounds on in-memory histories and streaming buffers so repeated use does not grow without limit. | partial | — | Explicit bounds are set and written down: the UCI line buffer at 1 MB, keeping the tail rather than growing (src/shared/chess/uci.ts:174-189); the engine's stderr ring at 50 lines trimmed to 25 (engine.ts:357-358); diagnostic lines truncated to 400 characters (engine.ts:836); the stored-record caps in `KEPT` — exercises 4,000, cards 8,000, ratings 20,000, lines 2,000, goals 60, plan items 200 — each with a note telling the person what was left out (src/main/chess/shapes.ts:88-119); the position editor's undo at 120 (PositionEditor.tsx:123); the endgame log at the last six lines; the live diagnostics list at four (LiveChess.tsx:296). Disposal is tested and passing: 'taking the scene down lets go of every shape and every material' and 'a scene that cannot be built does not leave half of one behind' (three/scene.test.ts). | No profiling has been done, which the section asks for explicitly, and no test repeats opening games, running analysis, switching modules and returning to the library while watching memory — that repetition is the acceptance criterion and nothing exercises it. Two unbounded holdings remain: the analysis session's tree and its recorded evaluations grow for the life of the studio with no cap (AnalysisStudio.tsx, `recordEvaluation`), and `importPgn` holds the whole PGN text and every parsed game in memory at once with no streaming (store.ts:147-182). |
| 465 | Efficient Evaluation Graphs and Analysis Views | Generate and draw the evaluation graph efficiently and honestly: handle centipawn and mate scores, missing and interrupted evaluations, long games, perspective changes, move selection, critical-moment markers and variation navigation; make mate scores look like mate rather than a huge number; map graph clicks to the right move; and never invent intermediate values to smooth the line. | partial | yes | Mate scores are pinned rather than scaled — `barFraction` returns 1 or 0 for mate and a bounded curve for centipawns so nothing but mate reaches the end of the bar (src/shared/chess/evaluation.ts:48-54). Unanalysed positions become `fraction: null`, drawn as a shaded gap with nothing across it, and the graph is built as separate runs (EvalGraph.tsx:41-80; analysis.ts:782-783, 1418-1446). Missing evaluations are never filled in — `lossFrom` returns null rather than putting a mate and a centipawn score on one scale (evaluation.ts:65-68). The bar turns round with the board; critical markers come from the report's `CriticalReason`; variations are navigated through the tree.
